壹點學園图书馆 · 教练研究

教练其人，而非问题

Coach the Person, Not the Problem
用反思式探询做教练的指南（第二版）
作者 Marcia Reynolds　2026
本电子书由原文 md 自动生成，左侧目录可点击跳转；右上角可切换「英文 / 中英对照 / 中文」。
已译 120 节，其余标「中译待补」显示原文。

导言 · Introduction

I WRITE MY books because I am passionate about the importance of coaching especially in these chaotic, uncertain times. My purpose in writing this edition is not only to elevate your skills but also to boost your enthusiasm for the amazing work you do to uplift spirits, increase confidence, and expand possibilities for your clients.

我写这些书，是因为我深深相信，在这个混乱而不确定的时代，教练（coaching）至关重要。这一版写作的目的，不仅是提升你的技巧，更是要唤起你对自己这份了不起的工作的热情——你振奋他人的精神、增强他们的信心、拓展他们可能性的工作。

why do people need to be coached?

As we go about our daily lives, our brains create the beliefs and rules that define our self-concept and how we interpret our world. The brain then protects these concepts so we have a sense of certainty about how to live. Our brains don’t like to have this sense of certainty disrupted, even when we know our mental habits are no longer serving our higher selves.

在我们日常生活中，我们的大脑会建立起定义「自我概念」以及我们如何解读世界的信念与规则。大脑随后保护这些概念，让我们对生活该如何过产生一种确定感。即便我们知道自己的心智习惯已不再服务于更高的自我，大脑也不喜欢这种确定感被打破。

The brain’s desire to protect our beliefs and identity blocks external attempts to challenge our thoughts unless we openly invite someone to help us think differently. Without invitation, when someone asks us why we did something, we immediately come up with a justification and then defend it even if we could easily find another reason given more time to think about it. Rationalizing is a well-honed skill we developed in our childhood.

大脑想要保护我们的信念与身份，这会阻挡外部对我们思绪的挑战——除非我们主动邀请他人来帮我们换个角度思考。若无人邀请，当有人问我们为何这样做时，我们会立刻想出一个理由来辩解，即便多给点时间我们本可轻松找到另一个理由。合理化是我们童年时就练就的纯熟技能。

When we attempt to sort out a problem on our own, we don’t recognize the impact of these protective mechanisms. Neuroscientist Michael Gazzaniga says our deductions and decisions are subject to our “automatic thought-processing that leads us to fool ourselves into thinking we are right.” 1 We instantly concoct a brilliant reason for procrastinating on a task, prioritizing reading email over working on a project deadline, or making life decisions based on how we will feel in the future when, in truth, we can never be sure how we will feel.

当我们试图独自厘清一个问题时，我们意识不到这些自我保护机制的影响。神经科学家 Michael Gazzaniga 说，我们的推论与决定受制于「自动化的思维处理，它让我们自欺欺人地以为自己是对的」¹。我们会立刻为一桩拖延找了个精彩借口、把读邮件置于项目截止日期之前、或基于「未来自己会有什么感受」来做人生决定——而事实上，我们永远无法确定自己届时究竟会有何感受。

For the same reason you can’t tickle yourself, your brain resists self-imposed testing of thoughts and reactions.

正如你无法胳肢自己发笑，你的大脑也会抗拒由自己发起的对思绪与反应的检验。

We justify our choices instead of questioning them. The brain prefers self-preservation over self-actualization.

我们为自己的选择辩护，而非质疑它们。大脑偏好自我保护，而非自我实现。

This natural resistance is why you can’t just tell someone to think differently even if you have strong evidence that pokes holes in their beliefs. The more emotions attached to their perspective, the more they steadfastly hold on to their beliefs. Sometimes this resistance is simply tied to how much we dislike having someone tell us what to do or think, as if they know better than we do about our personal situations.

这种天然的阻抗，正是为什么即使你握有能戳破对方信念的有力证据，也无法仅仅叫他们换种方式思考。一个视角所承载的情绪越多，他们就越固执地紧抓自己的信念不放。有时，这种阻抗仅仅源于我们多么不喜欢被人指挥该怎么做、怎么想，仿佛对方比我们自己更了解我们的处境。

Professor Carin Eriksson Lindvall at Uppsala University said humans don’t change habitual behaviors even when they experience undesirable results. 2 We might disturb a routine for a short time, but when our attempts to change feel awkward, we return to old, familiar behavioral patterns. We don’t want others to see and judge our fumbles.

乌普萨拉大学的 Carin Eriksson Lindvall 教授指出，即使尝到不如意的后果，人类也不会改变习惯性行为²。我们或许会短暂地打破惯例，但当改变的尝试让人感到笨拙时，我们就会退回熟悉的老模式。我们不想让别人看见并评判我们的跌跌撞撞。

The fear of looking incompetent shakes our confidence. Our brains then conjure justifications for going back to the way we used to behave to save us from the discomfort of change.

害怕显得无能会动摇我们的自信。于是大脑编造出理由，让我们回到旧有的行为方式，以逃避改变带来的不适。

To override our brains when we genuinely desire to alter our behaviors, we need a thinking partner to help us see our rationalizations and what we are protecting. We need someone outside of ourselves to summarize our words and ask questions about what we mean and want in order to stop the brain’s automatic manufacturing of protective reactions.

当我们真正渴望改变行为、需要绕过大脑的阻抗时，我们需要一位思维伙伴，帮我们看清自己的合理化说辞，以及我们究竟在保护什么。我们需要一个身外的他人，来归纳我们的话语，并就我们的本意与渴望发问，从而中止大脑自动生成的保护性反应。

Skilled thought disruptors notice and ask how the reasons we give for acting and feeling the way we do contradict what we most desire to create. They share the statements we make to justify hesitations. They invite us to consider how likely the negative consequences we fear are to happen and, even if they did, what we would do next. We can then see our thoughts with clarity, hear and recognize the holes in our justifications, admit to the fears limiting our choices, and identify the shoulds we have been dutifully obeying even when they don’t align with our values and desires.

娴熟的「思绪搅动者」会留意并追问：我们为某种行为与感受所给的理由，如何与我们最渴望创造的背道而驰。他们会把我们为犹豫辩护的话语复述出来。他们邀请我们思考：我们所害怕的负面后果真的发生概率有多大，而即便发生，我们下一步又会怎么做。于是我们得以清晰地看见自己的思绪，听见并认出辩解中的漏洞，承认那些限制我们选择的恐惧，并辨认出那些即使与我们的价值观和渴望不符、却一直被我们毕恭毕敬遵从的「应该」。

Coaching the person is the art of disrupting thoughts with the purpose of fostering creative insights that change minds and behavior.

教练其人，是一门以搅动思绪为目的的艺术，旨在催生能够改变心智与行为的创造性洞见。

It’s as if external thought disruptors lay our thoughts out on a table in front of us, beyond the shield of our overly protective brains. The insights that then emerge are disruptive because we have to face what we had previously avoided or never stopped to consider.

仿佛外部的思绪搅动者把我们的想法摊开在面前桌上，置于我们过度保护的大脑盾牌之外。随之涌现的洞见是搅动性的，因为我们不得不直面此前回避过、或从未驻足思量过的事。

Using the skills of reflective inquiry in a conversation deflates the dependency on rationalizations. We coach to liberate people’s minds from the past so they can more freely move toward their desired futures.

在对话中运用反思式探询的技巧，会削弱对合理化的依赖。我们做教练，是为了把人们的心智从过去中解放出来，使他们更自由地走向所渴望的未来。

If you choose to give someone advice instead of coaching them to think differently, you get either compliance, which pacifies the creative, learning brain, or resistance because they don’t feel like you really listened to them. If your suggestion is simple, they might feel embarrassed or they resent that you think they are so ignorant that they hadn’t considered the obvious answers. Their shame or frustration may lead them to sabotaging your good idea.

如果你选择给人建议、而非教练他们换种方式思考，你换来的要么是一味顺从——这会平息那创造性、爱学习的大脑；要么是抗拒——因为他们觉得你并未真正倾听。若你的建议过于简单，他们可能感到尴尬，或愤懑于你认为他们蠢到连显而易见的答案都没想过。他们的羞耻或挫败，或许会让他们暗中破坏你的好主意。

If you instead choose to start the conversation with a coaching approach, summarizing what you hear and then asking them to clarify what key words and ideas mean to them, they at least feel seen and heard. They are more willing to discuss their beliefs and perspectives with you. New ideas on how to solve their dilemmas will then likely emerge.

相反，如果你以教练的方式开启对话——归纳你所听到的，再请他们厘清那些关键词与想法对他们的意义——他们至少会感到被看见、被听见。他们更愿意与你探讨自己的信念与视角。关于如何解决困境的新想法，便可能由此浮现。

When you choose to coach, the experience is transformational because it transforms thinking. New thoughts are formed, which generate new actions.

当你选择教练，这段经历是转化性的，因为它转化了思维。新的想法得以形成，进而催生新的行动。

But transformational conversations can be uncomfortable. The realizations often trigger an emotion. Their old reasoning is now discredited, producing a moment of uncomfortable uncertainty before a new reality appears. Nessa Victoria Bryce wrote in Scientific American Mind, “This pause in certainty as the brain rushes to reinterpret information is necessary for a clearer and broader understanding of the situation to emerge.” 3 Profound learning often happens in these awkward moments of not knowing that create the opening for a new knowing to form.

但转化性的对话可能令人不适。醒悟往往会触发情绪。他们旧有的推理已被推翻，在新现实浮现之前，会有一刻令人不安的不确定。Nessa Victoria Bryce 在《Scientific American Mind》中写道：「当大脑仓促地重新诠释信息时，这种确定感的暂停，是澄清并拓宽对情境的理解所必需的」³。深刻的学习常发生在这些「不知」的尴尬时刻——正是它们为新知的萌生打开了缝隙。

This discomfort is why most people, from leaders to friends, would rather avoid the emotionally unpredictable path that is an important element of coaching. They prefer transactional processes that focus only on the possible actions that could be taken. These conversations feel easier but are less effective when looking to create sustainable change.

这种不适，解释了为何大多数人——从领导者到朋友——宁愿避开那条情绪难以预测的道路，尽管它正是教练的重要元素。他们偏爱「事务性」流程，只聚焦可采取的可能行动。这类对话感觉更轻松，但在谋求可持续的改变时，效果却逊色得多。

My question for you is “Do you want to take the more comfortable approach of problem-solving or step into the unknown to coach your clients’ thinking and possibly expand their ability to navigate their lives with more self-awareness and confidence?” Yes, this is a leading question, but even when you coach people into uncharted waters, if you give them a quiet moment to process their experience, their emotions will subside. Then, you can ask with curiosity and care, “Would you be willing to share what you are aware of now?” The aha experience then comes to life.

我想问你的是：「你是要走更舒服的问题解决路线，还是愿意踏入未知，去教练客户的思维，并由此拓展他们以更强的自我觉察与自信驾驭人生的能力？」是的，这是个诱导式提问，但即便你教练人们驶入未知水域，只要给他们片刻安静去消化体验，他们的情绪便会平复。接着，你便能带着好奇与关怀问：「你愿意分享此刻你觉察到了什么吗？」于是，顿悟的体验便鲜活起来。

what defines an aha moment?

A creative solution to a problem often includes one or more aha moments when people feel safe enough from judgment to dissect their thinking. As they hear their thinking reflected by someone else and dig into what their thoughts mean to their choices and sense of self, the brain reconfigures information, triggering a realization.

对一个问题的创造性解决方案，往往包含一个或多个「顿悟时刻」——当人们对评判感到足够安全，便能剖析自己的思维。当他们听到自己的思维被他人映照出来，并深入探究这些想法对他们的选择与自我感意味着什么时，大脑会重组信息，触发一种领悟。

The sudden shift in comprehension feels like the thought leaped out of their head. Their eyes might widen with a flash of a new insight, often referred to as a lightbulb moment. They might smirk or smile with a simple realization, or “duh” moment, when a person discovers that their circular and limited cogitating caused them to miss seeing an obvious solution.

理解上的骤然转变，感觉像是那个念头从他们脑中跳了出来。他们的眼睛可能因闪现的新洞见而睁大，这通常被称为「灵光一闪」的时刻。他们可能因一个简单的领悟而窃笑或微笑，或「哎呀」一刻——当一个人发现自己循环而有限的思虑，竟让自己错过了显而易见的解决方案。

Neuroscience reveals these creative insights emerge from the restructuring of past knowledge and experiences. When the brain is freed to rummage through its stockpile of memories, creativity flourishes.

神经科学揭示，这些创造性洞见源于对过往知识与经验的重构。当大脑被解放出来，翻找它囤积的记忆时，创造力便蓬勃绽放。

An emotional reaction often accompanies the aha moment. In researching how coaching works for my book The Discomfort Zone, I found both the uncomfortable moments of uncertainty and the ensuing reactions are vital for learning to occur. The aha experience can show up in many different ways with varying levels of intensity, ranging from laughter to tears or deep embarrassment. The emotions are never bad; they indicate the person is seeing something new. The new perspective encourages new behaviors.

情感反应常伴随着顿悟时刻。在为我的《The Discomfort Zone》一书研究教练如何起作用时，我发现那些不确定的不适时刻及其随后的反应，对学习的产生至关重要。顿悟体验可以以许多不同的方式出现，强度各异，从欢笑到流泪，再到深深的尴尬。这些情绪从来不是坏事；它们表明这个人正在看见某种新东西。新的视角会催生新的行为。

That is the gift and value we give with coaching.

这正是教练所赋予的礼物与价值。

can ai coach the person, not the problem?

Since Coach the Person, Not the Problem was published in 2020, the rapid rise of generative artificial intelligence (AI) prompted me to dive deeper into the research of the transformational change that coaching is capable of generating. Specifically, I wanted to convey how good coaching requires a human connection that would be difficult to replicate with AI.

自《Coach the Person, Not the Problem》于 2020 年出版以来，生成式人工智能（AI）的迅速崛起，促使我更深入地探究教练所能催生的转化性改变。具体而言，我想阐明：优秀的教练需要一种人与人之间的联结，而这很难被 AI 复制。

Yes, there are times when discussing a problem and analyzing possible solutions can benefit from AI-assisted learning, but when emotions are attached to the problem or outcome, sorting through options is more difficult. If people are afraid to change or believe there is no solution that avoids a negative outcome, their anxiety and frustration decreases their ability to talk through possibilities. Ruminating on risks drains instead of stimulating creative insights. Even if they are open to talk about what good can come from changing, their stewpot of beliefs, assumptions, evaluations, and expectations limits their perspective.

是的，有些时候，讨论问题、分析可行的解决方案，可从 AI 辅助学习中获益；但当情绪与问题或成果纠缠在一起时，梳理选项就变得困难。如果人们害怕改变，或认为不存在任何能避开负面后果的解决方案，他们的焦虑与挫败就会削弱他们谈论可能性的能力。对风险的反复思虑只会消耗、而非激发创造性洞见。即便他们愿意谈论改变可能带来的好处，他们那由信念、假设、评判与期待搅成的思绪，仍在局限着他们的视角。

Due to confusion and fear, clients have difficulties seeing beyond the negative aspects of their stories or what horrible events will occur if they risk going for what they want. They often come to coaching knowing only what they do not want to happen. Coaching them to sort through the jumble of thoughts and emotions that are holding their brains hostage until they feel safe enough to let go requires more than technical skills. The human connection is needed to support sustainable changes in thinking and behavior.

出于困惑与恐惧，客户难以看穿自己故事中的负面因素，也难以看清若他们冒险去争取所想，会发生什么可怕的事。他们来到教练面前时，往往只知道他们「不想要」什么发生。教练他们去梳理那团囚禁其大脑的思绪与情绪，直到他们感到足够安全才肯松手——这需要的远不止技术技巧。要支撑思维与行为中可持续的改变，需要的是人与人之间的联结。

Yes, clients need to feel understood, which AI can do with summarizing, reflecting, and seeking confirmation. But the need to use our emotions to ease their fears and open their minds is a human energetic exchange.

是的，客户需要感到被理解，而 AI 可以通过归纳、映照与寻求确认来做到这一点。但用我们的情绪去缓解他们的恐惧、敞开他们的心扉，是一种「人」的能量交换。

If the conversation needs to go beyond considering options to choose a reasonable solution, it is more effective to work with a coach trained to create a connection that generates the psychological safety necessary for the client to bypass their protective brain and then willingly explore their thinking. Therefore, I have shifted the focus of the first part of the second edition of this book to how we use our emotions with and without words to make the human connections that generate the significant insights and transformation. The more adept we are at generating an energetic field with courage and compassion, the more our coaching skills rise above the capacity of AI to mimic our impact.

如果对话需要超越考虑选项、去选择一个合理的解决方案，那么与一位受过训练、能建立联结的教练合作会更有效——这种联结能催生客户绕过其保护性大脑、进而心甘情愿地探索其思维所需的心理安全感。因此，我在本书第二版的前半部分，将重点转向我们如何运用带字与不带字的情绪，去建立那种能催生重大洞见与转化的人的联结。我们越善于以勇气与慈悲生成一种能量场，我们的教练技巧就越能超越 AI 模仿我们影响力的能力。

what’s new in this edition

My intention for this edition is to take you even further on your path of mastery than the first edition. I hope the book will be your new go-to guide to easily and effectively use your coaching skills with a clear understanding of what you are trying to achieve with your skills.

这一版的目标，是带你在精通之路上比第一版走得更远。我希望这本书会成为你随手可用的指南，让你清晰理解自己想用技巧达成什么，从而轻松而有效地运用教练技巧。

The essential skills for coaching a person’s thinking have been updated from my experiences and research since the first edition was published. With the recognition of the importance of the human connection, I switched parts 2 and 3 from the original text so the updated version teaching the mental habits comes before clarifying the specific skills. This gives the human relational skills priority because these practices make exercising the practical skills more effective.

自第一版出版以来，基于我的经验与研究，教练一个人思维的核心技巧已得到更新。随着对人之联结重要性的认识，我将初版中的「第二部分」与「第三部分」对调，使讲授心智习惯的更新版本排在明确具体技巧之前。这让关系性的人本技巧获得优先地位，因为这些练习能让实用性技巧的运用更见成效。

After exploring the value of coaching and the revised five Misleading Coaching Beliefs, part 2 delves into the three essential mental practices, accompanied by helpful exercises so you can apply the mental habits needed to make coaching a co-creative process:

在探索了教练的价值，以及修订后的五大「误导性教练信念」之后，第二部分深入探讨了三种必要的心智练习，并配有实用的练习，好让你应用那些将教练变为共创过程所需的心智习惯：

  • Align your nervous system.

- 调谐你的神经系统。

  • Receive (don’t just listen).

- 接收（而不只是倾听）。

  • Catch and release judgment.

- 捕捉并释放评判。

Part 3 is your guidebook to five active coaching skills. This new edition includes updates with what I have discovered from teaching coaching skills around the world. You will be given explanations of the intent of each skill, important actions to include to meet the objectives of the skill, and examples and case studies to see the skills in action. The skills include the following:

第三部分是你的指南，涵盖五项主动教练技巧。这一新版纳入了我环球教授教练技巧中所发现的新内容。你会看到每项技巧意图的说明、为达成技巧目标所应包含的重要行动，以及让技巧「动起来」的示例与案例研究。这些技巧包括：

  • Focus—Shifting to coaching the person, not the problem

- 聚焦——转向教练其人，而非问题

  • Active replay—Playing back the pivotal pieces for review

- 主动重播——回放关键片段以供审视

  • Navigating desire—Clearing the fog to see the road

- 导航渴望——拨开迷雾以看见前路

  • Brain hacking—What are you listening for?

- 大脑黑客——你在倾听什么？

  • New and next—Coaching insights and commitments

- 崭新与下一步——教练洞见与承诺

You will also find new coaching approaches, such as how to listen for and reflect key components in the context of clients’ narratives and how to effectively include who clients are being when they envision what they want to create. Coaching the identity of clients, including exploring fears around releasing their self-image and courageously taking steps to live more in alignment with their values, boosts their commitment to change. Identity shifts lead to sustainable change.

你还会发现新的教练方法，例如如何倾听并在客户叙事的脉络中映照关键要素，以及如何有效地纳入客户在构想所欲创造之物时，其「所是之人」。教练客户的身份——包括探索围绕放下自我形象、以及勇敢迈出步伐以更贴合自身价值观生活的恐惧——会增强他们改变的承诺。身份的转变带来可持续的改变。

Whether you are a coach or you are in another role and know the value of using a coaching approach in your conversations, these chapters provide the mental habits and essential skills for developing your ability to quickly prompt others to transform their perspectives and motivate actions.

无论你是一名教练，还是身兼他职、却深知在对话中运用教练方法的价值，这些章节都提供了培养你能力的心智习惯与核心技巧，让你能迅速促使他人转化视角、激发行动。

With the big shifts we are experiencing not just in technology but also in leadership, personal relationships, and geopolitical uncertainty, coaching is vital to support a confident outlook and adaptability. We make a meaningful difference with every person we touch with our efforts. I hope you find gems in this book to help you do your important work as a coach.

随着我们在技术、领导力、人际关系与地缘政治不确定性上的巨大转变，教练对于支撑一种自信的前景与适应力至关重要。我们每以努力触及一个人，便实实在在地带来了改变。希望你能在这本书中发现珍宝，助你完成作为教练的重要工作。

Part 2

Part I What is a Coaching Conversation?

The human soul doesn’t want to be advised or fixed or saved. It simply wants to be witnessed—to be seen, heard, and companioned exactly as it is.

人的灵魂不想要被劝告、被修理或被拯救。它只想被见证——如其本然地被看见、被听见、被陪伴。

— P ARKER P ALMER

— PARKER PALMER

T HE FOUNDING MEMBERS of the International Coaching Federation ( ICF ) asked the question, “What makes coaching different from therapy and consulting?” The first ICF definition of coaching emerged from their discussions: coaching is “partnering with clients in a thought-provoking and creative process that inspires them to maximize their personal and professional potential.” 1

国际教练联合会（ICF）的创始成员曾提出一个问题：「教练与治疗和咨询有何不同？」首个 ICF 教练定义便从这些讨论中诞生：教练是「与客户结成伙伴，进入一个激发思考与创意的过程，激励他们最大化个人与专业的潜能」¹。

The key word in the definition is partnering. Coaches do not act as experts or analysts even when they have relevant experience and education. Coaches are thinking partners focused on helping clients use their creativity and resourcefulness to see beyond their blocks to solve their problems on their own.

这一定义中的关键词是「结为伙伴」。即便教练拥有相关经验与学历，他们也不充当专家或分析者。教练是思维伙伴，专注于帮助客户运用自己的创造力与机变，看穿阻碍，从而独自解决自己的问题。

The passion and commitment that fuels the continual growth of coaching is grounded in the coaching experience for both coach and client. For me—when I give up the urge to advise—nothing is more fulfilling than seeing my clients laugh at themselves when they realize they’ve been clinging to an outdated belief. I love the spark in their eyes when they discover the answer to their dilemmas. It’s a gift to witness their faces relax and their postures rise up when they recognize they won’t be hurting anyone, or themselves, when they decide they can and are worthy of reaching for their dreams. When I feel the courage in my clients bubble up, it is my pleasure to help them put their desires into motion.

推动教练持续成长的激情与承诺，根植于教练与客户双方的教练体验。对我而言——当我放下劝告的冲动——没有什么比看见客户在意识到自己一直紧抓着过时信念时，笑起自己来，更让我满足。我喜爱他们眼中那发现困境答案时的火花。当他们认识到，若决定去够向梦想、并相信自己值得，便不会伤害任何人或自己时，能见证他们面容放松、身姿挺拔，是一种礼物。当我感受到客户勇气涌流，助他们将渴望化为行动，便是我的乐事。

When we partner with our clients so they feel seen, heard, and valued, their creative brains are activated. They feel safe enough to explore their thinking and actions. Surfacing their judgments and fears may be uncomfortable for them—and you—but when clients see how to move beyond their blocks, they feel empowered.

当我们与客户结成伙伴，使他们感到被看见、被听见、被重视，他们的创造性大脑便被激活。他们感到足够安全，去探索自己的思维与行动。浮现他们的评判与恐惧，对他们——也对你——或许不适，但当客户看清如何迈过阻碍，他们便感到被赋能。

The psychologist Alfred Adler explored the concept that people don’t need to be fixed or saved because they are naturally creative, resourceful, and whole. He said, “Man knows much more than he understands.” 2 In breaking away from the ideas of his teacher Sigmund Freud, Adler said we do not have to plumb the depths of one’s psychological history to help normal people progress. If we determine ourselves by the meanings we give to situations, then changing or expanding the meanings opens new possibilities to redefine ourselves and our actions. 3

心理学家 Alfred Adler 探讨过一个理念：人不需要被修理或拯救，因为他们天生具有创造性、机变与完整。他说：「人所知，远多于其所理解。」² 在脱离其师 Sigmund Freud 的思想时，Adler 说，我们无需深挖一个人的心理史去帮助普通人进步。若我们以赋予处境的意义来定义自己，那么改变或拓宽这些意义，便为重新定义自我与行动开启了新的可能³。

The regard Adler held for humans is a foundational concept for coaching. We are respectful partners, not expert advisers, who believe in our clients’ abilities to think differently. We use our skills to enable them to more broadly and courageously think about their thinking.

Adler 对人的尊重，是教练的基石性概念。我们是尊重的伙伴，而非专家顾问，相信客户有能力换个方式思考。我们运用技巧，使他们更广阔、更勇敢地思考自己的思维。

where does the term reflective inquiry come from?

Even though coaching may be similar to cognitive behavioral therapies and question-based relationship consulting, the actual practice of coaching can be dated back to John Dewey’s learning theory.

尽管教练可能与认知行为疗法以及基于提问的关系咨询相似，但教练的实际实践可追溯到 John Dewey 的学习理论。

In 1910, Dewey defined the practice of reflective inquiry in his classic book, How We Think . 4 As an educational reformer, Dewey wanted to change the practice of dumping information in students’ brains and then testing their memorization skills. He wasn’t just advocating for teachers to ask more questions. He defined methods of inquiry that prompted students to doubt what they thought they knew so they would open up to learning.

1910 年，Dewey 在其经典著作《How We Think》中定义了「反思式探询」的实践⁴。作为一位教育改革的推动者，Dewey 想改变那种把信息灌进学生大脑、再测试其记忆技巧的做法。他不只是倡导教师多提问。他定义了探询的方法，促使学生怀疑他们自以为知道的，从而向他们敞开学习之门。

Dewey said combining tools that provoke critical thinking with Socratic questioning would prompt students to go inward to give their thoughts serious consideration. They would then be better able to discern what they know from what they don’t know, confirm or negate a stated belief, and verify whether a fear or doubt held any value.

Dewey 说，将激发批判性思考的工具与苏格拉底式提问结合，会促使学生向内探究，认真审视自己的思想。他们便能更好地分辨自己所知与所不知，确认或否定一个既述的信念，并验证一份恐惧或怀疑是否真有价值。

He said that, metaphorically, reflective inquiry enables us to climb a tree in our minds. 5 We gain a wider view to see the connections and faults in our thinking to better assess what to do next.

他说，比喻而言，反思式探询使我们得以在心智中爬上一棵树⁵。我们获得更广阔的视野，看清思维中的关联与谬误，从而更好地评估下一步该做什么。

the power of active replay

Reflective inquiry includes statements that hold up a mirror to our thoughts and beliefs to provoke evaluation. The practice of mirroring, or what I call active replay, includes when the coach summarizes, paraphrases, acknowledges key phrases, and shares the emotions and gestures clients express. Clients expand on the meaning of their words with explanations or corrections. They may drop into silence, shifting their eyes up, down, or sideways as they look into their thoughts. Coaches pause to let their clients think. If the pause is unbearably long, the coach might offer a reflection and question, such as “It looks like you are considering something. Would you share what is coming up for you?”

反思式探询包含这样一些陈述：它们举起一面镜子映照我们的思想与信念，以激发评估。这种映照的实践——我称之为「主动重播」——包括当教练归纳、转述、确认关键措辞，并分享客户所表达的情绪与姿态时。客户会通过解释或修正，拓展其话语的意义。他们可能陷入沉默，眼神向上、向下或侧向游移，沉入自己的思绪。教练暂停，让客户思考。若停顿长得难以忍受，教练或许会给出一个映照与提问，例如：「看起来你在思考什么。你愿意分享此刻浮现在你心头的是什么吗？」

When coaches use reflective statements, people hear their words, see how their beliefs shape their perspectives, and consider the emotions they are expressing. Then, when coaches follow up with a question to confirm their observation (such as “Is this the belief you are holding?”) or to explore what they expressed (such as “How does that belief relate to the uncertainty you said you feel?”), clients are prompted to examine their thinking.

当教练运用反思性陈述，人们便听见自己的话语，看见自己的信念如何塑造其视角，并思量他们正在表达的情绪。接着，当教练以一个问题跟进，去确认其观察（如「这就是你所秉持的信念吗？」）或去探索其所表达的内容（如「这个信念与你所说的那种不确定感有何关联？」）时，客户便被促使去检视自己的思维。

We use reflective statements plus questions to prompt people to reflect on and assess how they think.

我们运用反思性陈述加上提问，促使人们反思并评估他们如何思考。

Coaching includes noticing and asking about energy shifts, tone of voice, pace of speech, and changes in body movements and facial gestures. Coaches play back the stated beliefs and assumptions to consider what is true now or limiting. They concisely summarize key words and phrases, expressed desires, and the doubts impinging on their clients’ possibilities, offering these reflections for clients to accept or alter. They present observations when clients show resistance. They reflect progress to reinforce the insights discovered and subsequent decisions and commitments to actions. The goal of offering reflective statements is not to lead clients to a specific way of thinking but to clarify their thoughts so they discover new ways of thinking on their own.

教练包含「留意」并「询问」能量的转移、语调、语速，以及身体动作与面部表情的变化。教练「回放」客户所陈述的信念与假设，以考量此刻何为真实、何为局限。他们「扼要归纳」关键词与短语、所表达的渴望，以及侵扰客户可能性的怀疑，将这些映照提供给他们接受或修正。当客户表现出阻抗时，他们「呈现观察」。他们「映照进展」，以强化所发现的洞见，以及随后的决定与行动承诺。提供反思性陈述的目的，不是把客户引向某种特定的思维方式，而是厘清他们的思绪，使他们靠自己发现新的思考路径。

By using reflective practices, coaches encourage clients to think about what they express when talking about their situations. The coach accepts the clients’ responses, even if they respond defensively or doubtfully. Giving clients a judgment-free space to process the reflected observations is critical to their progress.

通过运用反思性实践，教练鼓励客户去思考：当谈论自己的处境时，他们所表达的是什么。教练接纳客户的回应，即便他们防御性或怀疑性地回应。给予客户一个免于评判的空间去消化那些被映照出来的观察，对他们的进步至关重要。

John Dewey may not have been successful at transforming our educational systems, but his gift of defining how to expand people’s minds can be seen today in the actions of trained coaches.

John Dewey 或许未能成功变革我们的教育体系，但他界定如何拓展人心的那份礼物，今天在受训教练的行动中清晰可见。

coaching isn’t rah-rah

Most people I know like the idea of having someone act as a sounding board who offers a few questions when thinking through a dilemma. Talking about a problem can help analyze what could help or hinder their goal achievement. They don’t want the listener to only give simple suggestions they have already considered or shallow encouragement. “You can do this” statements can feel patronizing, especially to the high achiever.

我认识的大多数人，都喜欢有个人当回音板、在梳理困境时给几个问题的主意。谈论问题，有助于分析什么能助、什么会碍于目标的实现。他们不想要倾听者只给出他们早已考虑过的简单建议，或肤浅的鼓励。「你能行」这类话可能让人觉得居高临下，尤其对高成就者。

In fact, as I explained in the introduction, good coaching often spurs moments of discomfort. Learning often happens in a moment of awkward uncertainty when we come to doubt the beliefs and assumptions that underlie our choices or inaction. A disruptive reflection or incisive question is needed to break down what we think we know. The experience may be unsettling, but then we become open to learning. Over time, we are grateful for the insights we gained.

事实上，正如我在导言中所说，好的教练常激起不适的时刻。学习常发生于「我们开始怀疑那些支撑选择与不作为的信念与假设的尴尬不确定一刻」。需要一个打破我们所自以为知的搅动性映照或尖锐提问。这段经历可能令人不安，但随后我们便向学习敞开。久而久之，我们会感激所获的洞见。

For example, I had a boss who had developed his ability to read people. When we talked, he seemed to know what barriers in my brain got in the way of my getting what I really wanted. His questions instantly opened my eyes to my blind spots. My realizations were often painful, but I knew what I had to do differently even after a brief interaction.

例如，我曾有位老板，他练就了读懂人的能力。当我们交谈时，他似乎知道是什么阻碍在我脑中，挡住了我真正想要的东西。他的问题瞬间让我看清自己的盲点。我的领悟常是痛的，但即便在简短互动之后，我也知道必须做何不同。

Once, when I was raging about the incompetency of my peers and the overload of work I then had to do, he said, “It seems that everyone disappoints you.” Taken aback, I paused to think about his observation. Before I responded, he asked, “Will anyone ever be good enough for you?” Defenseless, I couldn’t think of anything else to say other than “Let me think about that.”

有一次，当我怒斥同侪的无能，以及我不得不承担的超额工作时，他说：「看来每个人都让你失望。」我一愣，停下来思量他的观察。在我回应前，他问：「会有人让你满意吗？」毫无防备之下，我除了说「让我想想」，想不出别的话。

Back at my desk, I wondered if I had always focused on other people’s flaws. I saw how this pattern had hurt both my personal and work relationships for years. With one reflection and question, I faced how I was playing out this pattern in my current work situation. I then knew I would never see my work relationships the same again.

回到办公桌前，我思忖自己是否一直盯着别人的缺点。我看见这个模式多年来如何伤害了我的人际与工作关系。仅以一个映照加一个问题，我便直面了自己如何在当前工作情境中重演这一模式。我于是明白，我对这段关系的看法将再也不同。

My boss’s observation and question made me stop and examine my thinking, which was terribly uncomfortable. In the midst of this discomfort I became more conscious of how I distanced myself from others by my need to prove I was better than them. I wanted to be a leader. Instead, I was a judgy complainer. The painful truth led me to learn how I could better work with others—and someday lead them.

我老板的观察与提问，让我停下并检视自己的思维，这令人极度不适。在这不适之中，我更清楚地意识到，自己如何通过「需要证明自己胜过他人」来与他人疏远。我想成为领导者，却反过来成了一个爱评判的抱怨者。这痛的真相，引我学会如何更好地与人协作——并终有一日领导他们。

The best coaches help us recognize that we have gaps in our reasoning. The moment we become unsure of what we know, learning happens.

最好的教练帮我们认识到，自己的推理存在缺口。就在我们对自己所知变得不确定的那一刻，学习发生了。

drifting away from the intention of coaching

My second master’s degree is in adult learning and instructional design. I’ll never forget a professor saying we should always tell students what they will be tested on so they could focus their learning. As a student, I loved this advice. My hope was to get an A in the class. I wasn’t considering how I would apply what I learned when I graduated; I just wanted to learn enough to be an A student.

我的第二个硕士学位主攻成人学习与教学设计。我永远忘不了一位教授的话：我们应总是告诉学生将会考什么，好让他们聚焦学习。作为学生，我钟爱这建议。我的希望是在课上拿个 A。我没去想毕业后如何运用所学；我只想学到足以当个 A 等生。

I still believe you should test on what you teach, but I have noticed a growing number of students in coach training programs worldwide are demanding to learn precise coaching steps to earn their credentials. The focus of teaching has shifted to the test of specific coaching behaviors with less on the importance of the energetic relationship.

我仍相信，你应考你所教的；但我注意到，全球教练培训项目中，越来越多的学员要求学习精确的教练步骤以拿到证照。教学的重点已转向对特定教练行为的考核，而较少关注那能量性关系的重要性。

Efforts by certifying organizations to legitimize coaching by making sure demonstrated behaviors are specific and replicable often overshadow the purpose of the conversation. Taking an evidence-based approach to limit assessor subjectivity is important, and behavioral descriptions are useful for trainers and mentors, but the attempts to reduce ambiguity when defining coaching behaviors has contributed to coaches believing they must follow a formulaic process. The focus on remembering what must be asked obscures people’s ability to allow their conversations to spontaneously flow toward revelation and perceived possibilities.

认证机构为使教练合法化，确保所展示的行为具体且可复制，往往盖过了对话的目的。采取循证方法以限制评估者主观性，这很重要；行为描述对培训师与导师也有用；但界定教练行为时试图减少模糊性，却助长了教练相信必须遵循一套公式化流程。专注于记住必须问什么，遮蔽了人们让对话自发流向启示与可见可能性的能力。

The original ICF coaching competencies were never intended to be a checklist of behaviors. I was a part of the ICF leadership when the competencies were written. The focus was on the transformational experience, where people learn from the inside out. The competencies were not meant to be taken in any order other than to spend time at the beginning to see and agree on what the client wanted to change or create as a result of the conversation and then to determine what is getting in the way of achieving this desire. To close out the conversation, the coach asks for a commitment to what the client will do next to ensure progress. The remaining competencies are guidelines for holding a conversation that could spawn a new awareness based on the quality of the coach’s presence and listening. There isn’t one right way to coach.

最初的 ICF 教练能力，从未意图成为一份行为清单。撰写这些能力时，我是 ICF 领导层的一员。重点在于那转化性的体验，即人们由内而外地学习。这些能力本不要求按任何顺序使用，只要求在一开始花时间看清并商定：客户想要因这次对话而改变或创造什么，继而确定是什么阻碍着实现这一渴望。为收尾对话，教练会请求客户承诺下一步将做什么，以确保进展。其余能力，是就「如何维持一场可能基于教练的临在与倾听品质而萌生新觉察的对话」的指引。教练没有唯一正确的方式。

Coaching involves so much more than mechanically reviewing what was said, asking overused questions, and following specific models. Coaches must first create an energetic bond of trust and safety that encourages a willingness to learn and change. The relationship is essential for the coach to be an effective thinking partner. The courage, care, and curiosity coaches feel, and their enduring belief in their client’s potential to learn and grow, make the competencies work.

教练远不止机械地回顾所说、问用滥了的问题、套用特定模型。教练必须先在教练与客户间建立信任与安全的能量纽带，催生学习与改变的意愿。这段关系，是教练成为有效思维伙伴的关键。教练所感受到的勇气、关怀与好奇，以及他们对客户学习与成长潜能历久弥新的信念，才让这些能力发挥作用。

During my first class at my coaching school, the founder, Thomas Leonard, said to learn how to coach, we had to get out there and coach people. We resisted, saying we didn’t know what to do. He said we would acquire our skills with experience. For now, he said, “Just go love them.”

在我教练学校的第一堂课，创始人 Thomas Leonard 说，要学会教练，我们得走出去去教练人。我们抗拒，说不知该做什么。他说我们会从经验中习得技巧。眼下，他说：「只管去爱他们。」

I have used this advice for over thirty years. Most times, I feel my clients love me back.

这建议我已用了三十多年。多数时候，我感到我的客户也以爱回馈我。

The intention of this book is to bring back the heart as well as the art of coaching into all our conversations. The book is a guide for all who use coaching, regardless of the school you attend, the credential you seek, or the role you play where having good conversations is essential.

本书的意图，是把心、把教练的艺术，带回我们所有的对话。这本书是所有运用教练之人的指南，无论你上的是哪所学校、求的是哪张证照，抑或你扮演的角色中，良好对话至关重要。

Chapter 3

CHAPTER 1 WHEN COACHING ISN’T COACHING

Ideas are our rulers—for better or worse.

观念支配着我们——无论好坏。

—J OHN D EWEY

——约翰·杜威

M ANY POPULAR BOOKS, leadership guides, and coach training outline rules for asking good questions. Typically, the instructions say to ask only open questions, avoid why questions, and never interrupt your clients.

许多畅销书、领导力指南和教练培训都会列出“如何提问”的规则：通常要求只问开放式问题、避开“为什么”、绝不打断客户。

These rules are misleading.

这些规则会误导人。

Coaches and leaders spend more time trying to remember the questions they’re supposed to ask than staying present with the client. They end up “checklist coaching” to ensure their questions follow the model they were taught or formulaic questions they were told they must ask, which is more frustrating for the client than helpful.

教练和领导者把更多时间花在回忆“该问什么问题”上，而不是安驻在当下、与客户同在。他们最终变成“清单式教练”——为了确保问题符合所学模型或被告知必问的套路，反而让客户更沮丧而非更有帮助。

Not only do coaches spend more time thinking than listening, they make coaching more complex than it should be. They don’t realize that being present and using reflective statements, such as summarizing, paraphrasing, and drawing distinctions to clarify the meaning of statements, is more powerful—and easier— than seeking the magical questions. When a coach reflects what seems to be important to the client and then asks a clarifying or confirming question, the question arises from curiosity, not memory. At this point, even a closed question seeking to verify the reflection can lead to a breakthrough in thinking.

教练不仅想得比听得多，还把教练搞得比本该有的更复杂。他们没有意识到：临在当下、运用反思性陈述（如总结、转述、辨析以厘清语意），比寻找“神奇问题”更有力，也更轻松。当教练反映客户看似在意的内容，再问一个澄清或确认性问题，问题就出自好奇而非记忆。此时，哪怕是一个用于核实反映的封闭式问题，也能带来思维上的突破。

Coaching should be a process of inquiry, not a series of questions. The intent of inquiry is not to find solutions but to provoke analysis of current perspectives. Inquiry helps clients discern faults in the logic, reveal unfounded fears that feel like walls, and identify the shoulds holding them back from their desires. Solutions emerge as their awareness expands.

教练应当是一个探询的过程，而非一连串问题。探询的目的不在找方案，而在激发对当下视角的分析。探询帮助客户辨识逻辑中的漏洞、暴露那些像墙一样挡路的莫须有的恐惧，并找出束缚他们靠近渴望的“应该”。当觉察扩展，方案自然浮现。

Statements that prompt us to think about what we are saying are reflective. They trigger reflection. Reflective statements include sharing what we hear and notice by concisely summarizing, paraphrasing, using metaphors, identifying key or conflicting points, and recognizing emotional shifts. Inquiry combines questions with reflective statements.

促使我们思考自己所言的陈述，就是反思性的。它们触发反思。反思性陈述包括：通过简洁地总结、转述、使用隐喻、指出关键或冲突点、识别情绪转变，来分享我们所听到和注意到的。探询把问题与反思性陈述结合在一起。

When using reflective statements in coaching, clients hear their words, come to understand how their beliefs affect their perceptions, and are willing to consider the sources and meaning of the emotions they are expressing. Then, a follow-up question that either confirms (“Is this true for you?”) or prompts exploration (“How does this relate to what you want to change?”) provokes clients to assess the merit and usefulness of their thoughts.

在教练中运用反思性陈述，客户得以听见自己的话，理解其信念如何影响感知，并愿意去考量他们所表达情绪的来源与意义。随后，一个既确认（“这对你而言是真的吗？”）又激发探索（“这和你想要改变的事有何关联？”）的追问，会促使客户评估自己想法的价值与用处。

Adding reflective statements to questions makes coaching feel like an organic and effortless conversation. You don’t have to worry about asking the right question.

把反思性陈述加进问题，让教练像一场自然、不费力的对话。你不必担心问出“正确”的问题。

Pairing reflective statements with questions frees the coach of the burden of finding the perfect, best, or right question.

将反思性陈述与问题配对，解除了教练寻找“完美/最好/正确问题”的负担。

On the other hand, some professionals who call themselves coaches ask questions for the purpose of determining what advice to give. A Harvard psychology professor told me she wasn’t interested in earning an ICF credential because her high-level executives didn’t want that type of coaching. “It’s a waste of time to question their thoughts and emotions,” she said. “They want my expertise. They are clueless and need advice or a kick in the butt.” It’s possible that is what her clients need, but it’s not coaching. It’s face-slapping mentoring. Her view of her clients as being clueless reinforces their lack of confidence and dependence on her for answers.

另一方面，一些自称为教练的从业者提问，是为了判断该给什么建议。一位哈佛心理学教授告诉我，她对考取 ICF 认证没兴趣，因为她的高管客户不想要那种教练。“质疑他们的想法和情绪是浪费时间，”她说，“他们要我的专业，他们一头雾水，需要建议或被踢一脚。”也许那正是她的客户所需，但这不是教练，这是打脸式指导。她把客户看成一头雾水，反而强化了客户的不自信与对答案的依赖。

I fear the loss of coaching as a distinct profession when the word coaching is diluted by people preferring to give advice. The greatest value of coaching is prompting people to quickly shift and reframe perspective so they redefine their situations and themselves. As a result of this new perspective, clients discover new solutions, take actions they had avoided, and commit to making changes more often than when they are told what to do.

我担心：当“教练”一词被偏好给建议的人稀释，教练作为一门独特职业便会消失。教练最大的价值，是促使人们迅速转换并重构视角，从而重新定义处境与自我。有了这新视角，客户比被告知该做什么时，更常发现新方案、采取曾被回避的行动、并承诺做出改变。

The goal of coaching is to get clients to stop and question their thoughts and behaviors that limit their perspective so they can see a new way forward to achieve their desires. Reflective practices provide the means for clients to objectively hear the stories they are telling. The follow-up questions then help them identify the beliefs and behavioral patterns they are clinging to. They see for themselves what actions are ineffective, even damaging to their relationships as well as to themselves. If done with patience and respect, the people you coach will likely see what they need to do without your brilliant advice.

教练的目标，是让客户停下、质疑那些局限其视角的想法和行为，从而看见通往渴望的新路。反思性练习提供了让客户客观听见自己所讲故事的方式；后续问题则帮助他们辨认所执着的信念与行为模式。他们自己就能看清哪些行动无效、甚至损害关系与自我。若以耐心与尊重行之，你所教练的人很可能自己看见该做什么，无需你高明建议。

how coaching works

As humans, we are masters at rationalizing our hastily made choices no matter how logical we think our thoughts are. Then, we are exceptional at blaming whomever or whatever we can when our choices lead to bad results.

作为人类，我们极擅长为自己匆忙做的选择找理由，无论我们以为自己想得多么合乎逻辑。接着，当选择导致坏结果，我们又极擅于责怪任何可责怪的人或事。

As Daniel Kahneman said in his book Thinking, Fast and Slow, we resist self-exploration, especially when emotions are involved. We don’t change well on our own. We cling to how we rationalize our actions and emotions and spend time seeking information that confirms our beliefs. 1

正如 Daniel Kahneman 在《思考，快与慢》中所说，我们抗拒自我探索，尤其当情绪卷入时。靠自己我们很难改变。我们紧抓着如何合理化自己的行动与情绪，并花时间寻找印证我们信念的信息。

To stop adverse thinking patterns, Kahneman said we need someone outside our heads to disrupt our thinking by reflecting our thoughts back to us and asking questions that prompt us to wonder why we think the way we do. These statements and questions enable us to see our fabricated stories as if they were laid out in front of us in a movie to be watched and analyzed.

Kahneman 说，要中止有害的思维模式，我们需要一个“身外之人”来打断我们的思考——把我们的想法反映回来，并提问促使我们自问：为何如此思考。这些陈述与问题，让我们得以像看一部摊开在眼前、可供观看与分析的电影那样，看见自己编造的故事。

The process helps bring limiting beliefs, overly negative or positive assumptions, and conflicts of values to the surface where they can be evaluated and revised to make better decisions.

这个过程把限制性信念、过度消极或积极的假设、以及价值冲突带到表面，使其得以被评估与修正，从而做出更好的决定。

Reflective practices can lead us to say, “Wow, look at what I’m doing to myself.” Sometimes, we say, “I don’t know why I think that way.” We become objective observers of the stories we have been telling.

反思性练习能让我们说出：“哇，看看我在对自己做什么。”有时我们会说：“我不知道自己为何那样想。”我们成了自己所讲故事的客观观察者。

Reflections followed by questions prompt us to stop and question our thinking and behaviors. This disruption initiates a shift in how we see ourselves and the world, or at least how we are framing a dilemma. We see a new way forward with a stronger commitment to taking action than if we were told what to do by an expert.

反映之后紧跟问题，促使我们停下、质疑自己的思维与行为。这一打断启动了自我与世界（或至少我们如何框定某个困境）看法的转变。我们看见新路，且比被专家告知该做什么时，更坚定地承诺采取行动。

Reflective statements help people think about what they are saying. The follow-up question seals the deal, creating the shift in awareness that resolves issues and prompts new actions.

反思性陈述帮助人们思考自己所言；后续问题敲定这笔交易，创造化解议题、激发新行动的觉察转变。

Leadership expert Hal Gregersen says we must all go beyond the bounds of what we think we know. 2 Unexpected shifts in life and business are always around the corner. We all can navigate daily dilemmas better with coaches who use reflective inquiry.

领导力专家 Hal Gregersen 说，我们都必须超越自以为知道的边界。生活与商业中意外的转变总在转角。若教练运用反思式探询，我们都能更好地应对日常困境。

coaching versus telling

Many leaders think giving advice is easier than taking the time to coach others to find their own solutions. They don’t realize they are wasting time instead of saving it.

许多领导者认为，给建议比花时间教练他人自己找出方案更容易。他们没意识到，自己是在浪费时间而非节省时间。

When you tell people what to do, you tap into their cognitive brain where they can analyze your words in the context of what they already know. If what you suggest relates to or confirms their views, they are likely to agree with you. They might have needed outside confirmation to fortify their confidence before acting.

当你告知人们该做什么，你触达的是他们的认知脑，他们能在已知语境中分析你的话。若你建议的与其看法相关或印证，他们很可能同意。行动前，他们或许正需要外部确认来巩固信心。

Offering ideas might be an efficient way to guide people’s actions, but you also run the risk of making them dependent on you for answers or approval before they act. You won’t create independent thinkers.

提供想法也许是引导人们行动的高效方式，但你也要冒让他们依赖你给答案或批准的风险。你无法培养出独立的思想者。

The results of giving advice are often less productive if they didn’t seek your counsel. They might hear you but then forget what you said in a very short time. The cognitive brain uses short-term memory, which is limited by both time and capacity. Other issues arise that rank higher in importance, crowding out your requests and ideas. We often don’t remember what we ate for breakfast, much less what someone suggested we do.

若他们本没寻求你的参谋，给建议的结果往往更低产。他们可能听了，但很短时间就忘了你说什么。认知脑用的是短期记忆，受时间与容量所限。其他更紧要的事浮现，把你要求与想法挤出去。我们常不记得早餐吃了什么，更别提别人建议我们做什么。

Even if you do remember what someone told you during the day, you lose ideas once you go to sleep. The brain takes this opportunity to sort through daily input to determine what is worth saving in your long-term memory. It retains bits of information that triggered emotions; emotions tell the brain something might be worth remembering. Unless you inspire people with your ideas or shock them with startling facts, they won’t remember what you said. Or they might remember but confuse details when they attempt to reconstruct it.

即便你当天记得别人说了什么，一入睡就丢了。大脑借此机会梳理每日输入，决定什么值得存入长期记忆。它保留触发了情绪的信息碎片；情绪告诉大脑某些东西或许值得记住。除非你用想法启发他们，或用惊人事实震撼他们，否则他们不会记得你说的话。或者他们记得，却在试图重构时混淆细节。

Remember all those quizzes you took in grade school to test your learning? How many would you pass today? Unless you continually use information you memorize, you lose it. A typical brain finds no reason for keeping useless stuff.

还记得小学那些测验你学习的测验吗？今天你能过几关？除非持续使用记住的信息，否则就会丢失。典型的大脑找不到保留无用之物的理由。

When we tell people what to do, unless we rouse a strong emotion, our words land in short-term memory, where learning is least effective.

当我们告知人们该做什么，除非激起强烈情绪，否则我们的话落入短期记忆——那里学习效果最差。

The cognitive brain may be good at problem-solving and decision-making, but it’s not good at learning. A study was done in April 2022 in Spain using electroencephalography ( EEG ) to record activity in the regions of the brain thought to be associated with problem-solving. Researchers studied three different approaches with each participant: (1) solitary sorting, (2) receiving direct opinions and advice, and (3) nondirective coaching using reflections and open-ended questions. Not only did the study demonstrate that different areas of the brain were activated by each of the approaches, the coach approach led to the highest activity in the region of the brain associated with creativity. The participants generated far more insights they called “aha” moments that enhanced their problem-solving capacity than when trying to figure something out on their own or when they were told what was best to do. 3

认知脑或许擅长解决问题与决策，却不擅长学习。2022年4月西班牙一项使用脑电图（EEG）记录与解决问题相关脑区活动的研究，让每位参与者尝试三种方式：（1）独自排序，（2）接收直接意见与建议，（3）运用反映与开放式问题的非指导性教练。研究不仅显示不同方式激活了脑的不同区域，教练方式更在与创造力相关的脑区引发了最高活动。参与者产生了远多于独自琢磨或被告知最佳做法时的“顿悟”时刻，提升了解决问题的能力。

Also, if people depend on you for answers, they lose motivation to think for themselves. This approach may work for consultants who want long-term work, but it’s not the best approach if you are a leader, parent, or coach who wants people to think for themselves.

此外，若人们依赖你给答案，便失去为自己思考的动机。这方式或许对想要长期合同的顾问有效，但如果你是想要人们独立思考的领导者、父母或教练，这就不是最佳方式。

coaching versus threatening: the feedback myth

When people experience a threat, they move into a defensive position. Threats include negative feedback and what people must do now “or else.” If they comply, their brains lodge the direction given along with feedback as critical to implement “or else.” This is fear-based learning. These memories are encoded as survival responses in the primitive brain.

当人们体验到威胁，便进入防御姿态。威胁包括负面反馈，以及他们现在“否则就得”做什么。若他们顺从，大脑会把所给指令连同反馈一起存档为“否则就得”执行的关键。这是基于恐惧的学习。这些记忆作为生存反应编码进原始脑。

When faced with similar situations, people reflexively react in the same way as before to avoid the threat of “or else” even if circumstances have changed. Their brains don’t trust being told to act differently. Resistance to change occurs because this learning is rooted in fear.

面对类似情境，人们反射性地以同样方式反应，以避免“否则”的威胁——即便情势已变。他们的大脑不信任被要求的不同做法。抗拒改变，正因这种学习根植于恐惧。

Learning based in fear fortifies behavior. The brain then resists change. Survival-based learning limits the agile thinking and risk-taking needed for change.

基于恐惧的学习强化行为。大脑于是抗拒改变。生存式学习限制了改变所需的敏捷思维与冒险。

Additionally, giving feedback often triggers stress, shame, and fear. The helpful information you give people often raises defenses and lowers confidence, decreasing initiative and innovation.

此外，给反馈常触发压力、羞耻与恐惧。你给的有益信息常激起防御、削弱自信，降低主动性与创新。

Harvard Law professors Sheila Heen and Douglas Stone found that even well-intentioned feedback “can spark an emotional reaction, inject tension into the relationship, and bring communication to a halt” no matter the position or experience of either the feedback giver or recipient. 4 People want to learn and grow, but they also have a basic human need for approval. Feedback, especially if unsolicited, is painful.

哈佛法学院教授 Sheila Heen 与 Douglas Stone 发现，即便出于好意的反馈，“也会引发情绪反应、给关系注入紧张、并让沟通陷入停顿”——无论给予者或接收者的职位与经验如何。人们想要学习成长，但也有被认可的基本人性需求。反馈，尤其未经请求者，是痛苦的。

When I start coaching, leaders often tell me that people want clear feedback. Yet when I interview their direct reports about the effectiveness of their leader, they say they want to improve but don’t want one-way feedback and advice on how to fix what they did wrong. They want two-way conversations that generate new ideas and open their eyes to greater possibilities.

当我开始教练时，领导者常告诉我人们想要清晰反馈。然而当我访谈他们的下属关于领导效能时，他们说自己想要进步，却不想要单向反馈，也不想要关于如何修正错误的建议。他们想要双向对话，产生新想法、打开视野看见更大可能。

Unfortunately, coaching is often confused with giving feedback. Even if feedback is received well, if you then tell people what to do instead of coaching them to discover what they could do differently, the outcome won’t be ideal. Unless someone asks you for advice because they truly don’t have a clue what to do, your feedback creates resistance or compliance. You stunt instead of grow people’s minds.

不幸的是，教练常被与给反馈混淆。即便反馈被良好接收，若你接着告知人们该做什么，而非教练他们发现能有何不同，结果也不理想。除非有人真心不知该做什么而问你建议，否则你的反馈制造的是抗拒或顺从。你阻碍而非成长人们的心智。

coaching taps into the middle brain

When it comes to influencing a change in behavior, don’t start by telling people what they did wrong. When you ask them to review what happened in a situation, they usually know what they did that didn’t work. People tend to be their harshest critics.

要影响行为改变，别从告诉人们他们做错了什么开始。当你请他们回顾某情境发生了什么，他们通常知道自己哪些做法无效。人往往是自己最严厉的批评者。

Instead, ask them if they would be willing to look at what they might do differently that could help them achieve their goals. If they show willingness, you can then use a coaching approach to tap into the creative part of their brain to explore possibilities.

反之，问他们是否愿意看看自己能有何不同，以助其达成目标。若他们显出意愿，你便可用教练方式触达大脑的创造部分，探索各种可能。

Creativity occurs in the middle brain, the limbic system, where long-term memory is housed. This part of the brain uses memories and motivations to process thoughts, generate creative ideas, and produce insight-based learning.

创造力发生在中间脑，即边缘系统，长期记忆所在之处。这部分脑用记忆与动机来处理思想、产生创意、并达成基于洞见的学习。

Exploring meaning, desires, fears, and beliefs can stimulate new ideas for moving forward. If improvement conversations started with a coaching approach instead of feedback, a creative conversation will likely follow instead of a defensive one. People are more likely to leave the conversation with a sense of responsibility and courage.

探索意义、渴望、恐惧与信念，能激发前行的想法。若改善对话以教练方式而非反馈开始，很可能随之而来的是创造性对话，而非防御性对话。人们更可能带着责任感与勇气离开对话。

Reflective inquiry can be called insight-based learning. Creative ideas emerge as people connect bits of stored information to formulate new ideas in response to questions. When their thoughts, beliefs, and emotional reactions are reflected and questioned by the coach, justifications unravel, prompting their brains to reorder bits of information to provide insights that will help them make sense of their new awareness. Perception of their situations and themselves shifts and changes.

反思式探询可称为洞见式学习。当人们的想法、信念与情绪反应被教练反映与质疑，正当化便瓦解，促使大脑重新排序信息碎片，以提供帮助他们理解新觉察的洞见。对处境与自我的感知随之转变。

The examination that prompts awareness overrides the protective brain, rejecting rationalizations while accepting new definitions of situations and the self even if they are initially disturbing. Insight-based learning is the best learning technology we have today.

促使觉察的审视压过保护性脑，拒绝合理化，同时接纳对处境与自我的新定义——即便起初令人不安。洞见式学习是我们今日拥有的最佳学习技术。

Don’t be afraid of the emotions that accompany insight-based learning. My clients often laugh after they gasp at what they see. The emotion can be more intense, but the reaction will diminish if you stay courageously and compassionately present, waiting for it to subside before asking if they would be willing to share what they now see. Then, we partner together to clearly articulate the new awareness so the learning gets embedded in long-term memory. Reflective inquiry works better than giving feedback and advice if you want to open and change minds. 5

别害怕伴随洞见式学习的情绪。我的客户常在倒抽一口气看见真相后大笑。情绪可能更强烈，但若你勇敢而慈悲地临在，等它平息再问他们是否愿意分享此刻所见，反应便会减弱。然后我们结伴清晰表述这新觉察，让学习嵌入长期记忆。若你想打开并改变心智，反思式探询比给反馈与建议更有效。

Case Study

案例研究

I had a client who always found fault in the ideas her peers offered in strategy meetings. Her leader said he wouldn’t give her the promotion she desired until she earned the respect of her peers. He gave her clear feedback of the impact of her behavior and what he expected to change. She complied by refraining from openly criticizing her peers in meetings. Instead, when their ideas ran counter to hers, she rolled her eyes and sighed. Exasperated, her manager asked me to coach her.

我曾有位客户，总在战略会议上挑同伴想法的毛病。她的领导说，在赢得同伴尊重前不会给她想要的晋升。他给了她清晰反馈，说明其行为影响及他期望的改变。她顺从了——在会上不再公开批评同伴，转而当同伴想法与她相左时翻白眼、叹气。无奈，她经理请我教练她。

She had attended some of the leadership classes I taught for the organization, so we had an established relationship. In our initial conversation, she said she wanted the promotion, was willing to change, and was keen on receiving coaching.

她上过我在组织内教的某些领导力课，所以我们有既定关系。初次对话中，她说想要晋升、愿意改变、也渴望接受教练。

We explored what annoyed her about her peers’ behavior and ideas. At one point, she said with a sarcastic tone, “Their behavior is so typical of leaders in this region. They don’t focus on the right things.” I asked her what she meant by “right.” She launched into the difference between the Latin American views of leadership and how the leaders in northern Europe are more effective and profitable. With agitation, she declared, “Leaders should make efficiency the priority.”

我们探索了她对同伴行为与想法有何不满。某刻，她带嘲讽语气说：“他们的行为典型就是这地区的领导作风。他们不聚焦正确的事。”我问她“正确”是什么意思。她大谈拉美与北欧领导观之别，以及北欧领导如何更高效、更盈利。带着激动，她宣称：“领导者应把效率放首位。”

My client had transferred from the Netherlands to Panama less than two years prior to her current position. I said, “Thank you for sharing your definition of good leadership. You spent your years rising up in your career in The Hague, correct?”

这位客户不到两年前从荷兰调往巴拿马。我说：“谢谢你分享你对好领导的定义。你职业生涯是在海牙一路升上来的，对吧？”

She gave me a wry smile and said, “Ya, ya, I get it, you want me to see where I learned about leadership.”

她苦笑说：“Ya ya，我懂，你想让我看见我在哪学到领导力的。”

“Yes,” I said, “and I want to clarify what seems to be a conflict in concepts of leadership. You told me you think efficiency should be a leader’s priority. What would your peers say should be the right priority for leadership?”

“是的，”我说，“而我想厘清领导概念中看似的冲突。你告诉我你认为效率应是领导优先项。你的同伴会说什么才应是领导的正确优先项？”

She conceded with a small roll of her eyes that her peers think employee engagement comes first, that people will work harder when they feel like we’re one big, happy family. She paused before she sighed and said, “Yes, there is a conflict of cultures that I don’t want to face but I know I have to!” We then unwrapped the beliefs and values she held in contrast to her peers. My client then said, “I’d like to at least find a compromise.”

她略翻白眼地承认，同伴认为员工投入第一——当人们感觉我们是一个快乐大家庭时会更努力。她停顿、叹气后说：“是的，确有我不想面对的文化冲突，但我知道必须面对！”我们接着拆开她与同伴相悖的信念与价值。客户于是说：“我至少想找个折中。”

Before I could say anything, she lit up with enthusiasm. She said, “I know. If I can involve them in a conversation about how they could use engagement to increase efficiency, we might find a way to integrate our priorities.” We then explored what she could do differently to mitigate the negative judgment her peers had of her so they might be open to brainstorm together.

我没及开口，她便热情迸发：“我知道了。若我能让他们参与一场关于如何用投入提升效率的对话，我们或许能找到整合优先项之法。”我们进而探索她能有何不同，以化解同伴对她的负面评判，使他们愿意一同头脑风暴。

When exploring further whether anything else could get in the way of her success, she admitted the biggest challenge was to re-earn their trust. She chose to set up one-on-one meetings with her peers to repair the relationships. They would meet over lunch since she knew they liked to share meals. She would genuinely ask what they thought she could do to be a better team member. She would bravely and patiently accept their ideas. She said she now knew this was the way to be seen as a leader by her peers.

进一步探索还有什么可能阻碍她成功时，她承认最大挑战是重新赢得信任。她选择安排与同伴的一对一会议修复关系。既然知道他们爱共餐，便共进午餐。她会真诚询问：他们认为她能做什么以成为更好的团队成员。她会勇敢而耐心地接纳他们的想法。她说，如今她知道这就是被同伴视为领导者的方式。

Feedback pushed her to pretend to behave differently. Coaching shifted her perspective, which allowed her to discover how to achieve her goals on her own. She recognized how differing values defined different ways to lead, which included the ability to adapt her style to make genuine connections with others.

反馈逼她假装表现得不同。教练转变了她的视角，让她自己发现如何达成目标。她认识到不同价值定义了不同领导方式，也包括调整风格以与他人建立真诚连接的能力。

when to coach and when to do something else

Many coaches tell you they do hybrid coaching that blends mentoring into the conversation. They say people want our opinions and insights from their experiences. They coach to understand the clients’ perception of the problem and then suggest options for going forward. They say they aren’t telling people what to do but are only offering ideas to consider.

许多教练告诉你，他们做“混合式教练”，把指导揉进对话。他们说人们想要我们来自经验的观点与洞见。他们教练以理解客户对问题的感知，然后建议前行选项。他们说这不是告知人们该做什么，只是提供可考虑的想法。

The result is a surface exploration of options that could solve the problem. This approach involves problem-solving mixed with advising. I cringe when they call it hybrid coaching. I believe there is no such thing as hybrid coaching.

结果是表面探索能解决问题的选项。这种方式把解决问题与建议混在一起。当他们称之为混合式教练，我直犯怵。我认为根本不存在混合式教练这回事。

When clients use your advice, you can’t be sure they won’t go back to doing things the way they have always done it before and then blame you for their ineffectiveness. You haven’t addressed what kept them from moving toward a solution on their own. You haven’t explored what doubts might arise, what beliefs could sabotage their resolve, or what other distractions could derail their progress. When you spend time discovering what could get in the way of success, clients have a better chance of navigating their routes when they encounter unplanned events and have to make quick decisions on their own.

当客户用了你的建议，你无法确知他们不会回头按老样子做，继而怪你无效。你没处理阻止他们自行走向方案的东西。你没探索可能浮现的疑虑、可能破坏决心的信念、或可能使进度脱轨的其他干扰。当你花时间发现什么可能挡住成功，客户在遭遇计划外事件、须独自快速决策时，更有机会走出自己的路。

I do think offering some ideas to consider at the end of a session could be useful if the suggestions support what actions the client committed to take. I don’t think asking a few questions at the start of the conversation and then giving advice saves time. Nurturing independent thinking is the best way to use your time efficiently in the long run.

我确实认为，在对话结束时提供些可考虑的想法或有用——若建议支撑客户承诺采取的行动。我不认为开场问几个问题然后给建议能省时间。长远看，培育独立思维才是高效用时的方式。

You are either coaching or you’re doing something else. Something else might be exactly what someone wants, but it doesn’t help them think for themselves in the future.

你要么在教练，要么在做别的事。别的事或许正是某人想要的，但无助于他们未来独立思考。

The acceptance of hybrid coaching dilutes the value of coaching. People will then expect you to tell them what to do. They will miss experiencing this powerful technology for creating breakthroughs, building confidence, and ensuring growth.

对混合式教练的接纳，稀释了教练的价值。人们随后会期待你告知该做什么。他们将错过体验这创造突破、建立自信、确保成长的有力技术。

Sometimes people don’t want or need coaching. Determine what they actually need before you assume they want to hear your stories and what you think is best to do.

有时人们不想要也不需要教练。在假设他们想听你的故事与你认为最佳做法前，先确定他们实际所需。

First, Establish If There Is a Desire for Coaching

Once, when a colleague tried to coach me on a situation when I wanted only to talk about the experience, I shut her down. “Stop coaching me. Right now, I just need a friend.” That incident made me look at the times when I butted in, thinking my friends or family needed my coaching.

曾有位同事想教练我某个情境，而我只想谈体验，我便回绝了她：“别教练我。现在我只是需要个朋友。”那件事让我审视自己插手的时候——以为朋友或家人需要我的教练。

Outside a formally declared coaching session, ask people if they would like some coaching before you start probing. You might ask, “What do you need from me right now?” They might just want to be heard, especially if they are outraged about a situation or grieving a loss.

在正式声明的教练对话之外，开始探究前先问人们是否想要些教练。你可以问：“此刻你需要我什么？”他们或许只想被听见，尤其当他们对某情境愤怒或正哀悼失落。

Even if they say they want coaching, make sure they are willing to engage with you. They must demonstrate a willingness for you to question their ideas and motivations, not just seek affirmation for their views. For example, they might want you to listen as they sort out a plan but aren’t open to being coached around the plan’s purpose, practicality, contingencies, or what had stopped them from creating the plan on their own without you.

即便他们说想要教练，也要确认他们愿意与你共事。他们必须展现出愿意让你质疑其想法与动机，而非只求对其观点肯定。例如，他们可能想让你听他们梳理计划，却不愿围绕计划的目的、可行性、偶发状况、或是什么阻止他们独自制定计划而被教练。

Some people are verbal processors, meaning they think best out loud with someone present. If they say they want to think through a situation, ask, “Do you need me to be your sounding board?” If you agree to this arrangement, which isn’t coaching, a little summarizing and paraphrasing might be welcome, with a few light questions that won’t interrupt their flow.

有些人是言语型处理者，意即有人在场时大声想最清楚。若他们说想理清某情境，问：“你需要我当你的共鸣板吗？”若你同意这安排（这不是教练），些许总结与转述或受欢迎，配几个不打断其流动的轻松问题。

However, don’t assume defensiveness means they are uncoachable. Ask what might be making them feel uncomfortable about being coached. If they aren’t sure about the value of being coached, ask what topics or situations they might explore with you that would be useful for them right now. Their willingness to be coached might increase once they experience the process.

然而，别假设防御就意味着不可教练。问是什么让他们对被教练感到不适。若他们不确定被教练的价值，问有哪些话题或情境可当下与你探索、对他们有用。一旦体验过程，他们被教练的意愿可能增加。

Next, Be Sure Coaching Is the Right Option

Sometimes, people lack any experience and knowledge to formulate a new perspective. Most times, the deficit is not absolute. Start by finding out what they do know that they think is not enough. When they say they have no idea what to do, ask if they considered any solutions even if they decided the approaches would not work.

有时，人们缺乏任何经验与知识来形成新视角。多数时候，这种缺失并非绝对。先弄清他们自认不足的是什么。当他们说不知该做什么，问他们是否考虑过任何方案——即便已断定那些方式行不通。

I often find people who say they don’t know what to do actually have an idea but are afraid they will fail or be judged negatively if they try. To determine if this is the case, ask, “Have you ever observed someone in a similar situation? What might you try that they did, or what might you do differently than what they did?” Or you might go further by asking, “If you had nothing to lose, what would you try?” If they still have no ideas, ask if they would like some suggestions to help them consider options, but trust they have ideas before you offer yours.

我常发现，说不知该做什么的人其实有想法，只是怕失败或被负面评价。要判断是否如此，问：“你可曾观察过类似处境的人？你会试他们做过的，或有什么会做得不同？”或可进一步问：“若你毫无损失，你会试什么？”若仍无想法，问他们是否想要些建议来帮助考量选项，但在你给建议前，先信任他们有想法。

Coaching works even when clients have only a little bit of experience to draw on but they aren’t sure what is best or they don’t understand why they are hesitating. You can help them discover options or dissect their fears.

即便客户只有一点经验可凭，只要他们不确定何为最佳、或不解自己为何犹豫，教练就有效。你能帮他们发现选项或剖析恐惧。

If they have a decision to make but are confused by different people telling them what they should do, you can coach them to discern what they think from what others say. Then, you can explore the possible losses and gains that could happen when they implement their preferred option.

若他们要做决定却被不同人告知该做什么而困惑，你可教练他们辨别何为自己所想、何为他人所言。然后你可探索其偏好方案实施时可能的得失。

Remember, the key word that defines what coaches do is partnering. Coaches are thinking partners. Coaches do not see clients as clueless or needing to be fixed. Clients are creative and resourceful enough to solve their own problems when you coach them to realize what has kept them from acknowledging what they know.

记住，定义教练所为的关键词是伙伴。教练是思维伙伴。教练不把客户看作一头雾水或需要被修理。当你教练他们认识到是什么阻止自己承认所知时，客户足以创造性地、有资源地解决自己的问题。

Start with coaching. You can discover together if they really need you to step out of coaching to offer suggestions.

从教练开始。你们可一同发现他们是否真需要你跳出教练去给建议。

When I teach coaching skills, I ask my students, “Are you willing to give up being the one who knows best?” You must step out of being the expert, helper, and rescuer to coach.

我教教练技能时问学生：“你愿意放弃做那个最懂的人吗？”要教练，你必须跳出专家、帮助者、拯救者的角色。

Learn When You Cannot Coach Someone

Do not coach if you can’t do the following:

若你无法做到以下，就别教练：

  • ✦ Let go of how you want the conversation to go. You want clients to resolve their problems, but you are attached to how the conversation will progress. If you can’t let go of knowing what the client should do, you will force the conversation in the direction you want it to go.
  • ✦ Believe in the client’s ability to figure out what to do. Do you judge your client’s ability to formulate an effective solution? If you doubt their competence to find a way forward, then offer to mentor instead. Otherwise, your impatience will corrupt your coaching.
  • ✦ Feel hopeful, curious, and care. If you are angry at, disappointed with, or bored by someone, they will react with fear, frustration, or disappointment. You will fulfill your own prophecy that the conversation won’t go well. Can you muster your belief in them, your commitment to their growth, and your care for building their confidence? If not, you may refer them to a coach who can.

- 放下你对对话走向的执念。 你想让客户解决自己的问题，却执着于对话将如何推进。若你放不下“客户该做什么”的已知，便会把对话往你想要的方向逼。 - 相信客户能搞清该做什么。 你评判客户制定有效方案的能力吗？若你怀疑他们找前路的能力，那就提供指导而非教练。否则你的不耐会腐蚀你的教练。 - 感到有希望、好奇与关怀。 若你对某人生气、失望或厌烦，他们会以恐惧、挫折或失望回应。你将实现自己“对话不会顺利”的预言。你能唤起对他们的信任、对成长的承诺、对建立自信的关怀吗？若不能，你可转介给能做到的教练。

Not all conversations can or should be coaching sessions. Figure out what people need and then choose to coach or do something else.

并非所有对话都能或该成为教练对话。弄清人们所需，再选择教练或做别的事。

ideal coaching scenarios

Opportunities to coach people often show up in these scenarios, personally and professionally:

教练人们的机会常出现在这些个人与职业场景中：

  • ✦ Exploring ways to improve communication and relationships
  • ✦ Decreasing a fear of conflict or emotional reactivity
  • ✦ Finding solutions for dealing with difficult people and situations
  • ✦ Strengthening relationships
  • ✦ Articulating purpose, vision, and commitments
  • ✦ Managing stress and improving well-being
  • ✦ Thriving with work and life changes
  • ✦ Leading others through change
  • ✦ Effectively taking on new leadership or career positions
  • ✦ Experiencing greater fulfillment and success

- 探索改善沟通与关系的方法 - 降低对冲突或情绪反应的恐惧 - 为应对难缠的人与处境找方案 - 强化关系 - 清晰表述目的、愿景与承诺 - 管理压力、改善福祉 - 在工作与人生转变中茁壮成长 - 带领他人穿越改变 - 有效承担新的领导或职业角色 - 体验更大的满足与成功

For coaching to be successful, clients should know what will occur in a typical coaching session with you. Conversations about the coaching process generally happen at the beginning of a coaching relationship, before the first full coaching session. Tell your clients they will experience the best outcomes if they are prepared to do the following:

要让教练成功，客户应知道与你典型教练对话会发生什么。关于教练过程的对话通常发生在教练关系开始时、首次完整教练对话之前。告诉客户，若他们准备好做以下事，将体验最佳成果：

  • ✦ Trust the coach even when the reflections and questions feel uncomfortable.
  • ✦ Be willing to explore what is confusing or uncomfortable.
  • ✦ Be open and honest, with themselves and with you, the coach.
  • ✦ Meet their own commitments for action between sessions.
  • ✦ Carve out time to reflect after a session and at least a few minutes before you both meet again.
  • ✦ Show up for scheduled sessions unless a real emergency comes up.

- 即便反映与问题令人不适，也信任教练。 - 愿意探索困惑或不适。 - 对自己、对作为教练的你，开放而诚实。 - 在两场对话之间履行自己的行动承诺。 - 在对话后留出反思时间，并在你们再次见面至少前几分钟也如此。 - 除非真有紧急，否则准时出席约定对话。

coaching shouldn’t be so hard

When you coach as a thinking partner instead of an expert, your job is to receive and offer back what your client expresses. You don’t have to produce a masterful question. You don’t have to have all the answers. You are a good coach if you provide a judgment-free space to talk, genuinely care about your client, share what you hear and see, and maybe offer what it seems like they mean with no attachment to being right.

当你作为思维伙伴而非专家来教练，你的工作是接收并回馈客户所表达。你不必抛出高明问题。你不必拥有所有答案。若你提供一个无评判的言说空间、真心关怀客户、分享所闻所见，并或许不带“我对”的执念地给出他们似乎意指之物，你就是好教练。

You will probably ask a question after you share what you heard and witnessed, but the question will come from your reflection, not your overused “best questions” list. Coaching should feel like a casual conversation. If you ease into the flow, not only will the experience be more effective and possibly life-changing, it will be more fulfilling and fun for you as well.

在你分享所闻所见后，你大概会问个问题，但问题出自你的反映，而非你用滥的“最佳问题”清单。教练应像场随意对话。若你从容进入流动，体验不仅更有效、甚或改变人生，对你也更充实有趣。

Chapter 4

CHAPTER 2 MISLEADING COACHING BELIEFS

Early on in my coaching career, I scratched my head at a number of standard practices and puzzled over some of the coaching “facts” that I had been taught.

在我教练生涯的早期，我曾对一些标准做法感到不解，也对所学到的某些教练“事实”感到困惑。

—R OBERT B ISWAS -D IENER

——罗伯特·比斯瓦斯-迪纳（ROBERT BISWAS-DIENER）

S OME COACHES, COACHING schools, and credentialing organizations don’t follow the ICF coaching competencies and still get amazing results. No matter what theoretical framework they draw from, if they predominantly use reflective inquiry as their foundational architecture, I support their approaches.

有些教练、教练院校和认证机构并不遵循 ICF（国际教练联合会）的教练能力标准，却依然取得惊人成果。无论他们借鉴哪种理论框架，只要主要以反思式探询作为基础架构，我都支持他们的做法。

If your practice encourages your clients to self-reflect and generate insights that spawn both possibilities and the motivation to commit to action, we are speaking the same language. We may disagree on the specific coaching competencies while agreeing on the effectiveness of good coaching.

如果你的实践能鼓励客户自我反思，并产生既能激发各种可能性、又能促使他们投入行动的洞见，那么我们说的就是同一种语言。我们也许在具体教练能力上存在分歧，却都认同优秀教练的有效性。

Conversely, certain beliefs and judgments about coaching belittle our work and hurt our profession. Some coaches oversimplify the application of coaching competencies to make them more accessible, understandable, and easier to assess when their students go for certification. The oversimplifications have made coaching more mechanical and rule based, decreasing instead of enhancing the potential impact of coaching.

相反，某些关于教练的信念和评判贬低了我们的工作，也伤害了这门职业。一些教练为了让能力标准更易理解、更易传授，也便于学生认证时评估，把它们过度简化。这种简化使教练变得更机械、更依赖规则，反而削弱而非增强了教练的潜在影响力。

These assumptions have caused many coaches to plateau in their development and have steered many leaders away from using a coaching approach in their conversations. When people think they have to adhere to rigid rules about how they ask questions, or they think they have to use a model with specific steps and formulaic questions to coach well, they lose their enthusiasm for coaching.

这些假设让许多教练在成长上陷入停滞，也让许多领导者不愿在对话中采用教练式方法。当人们以为自己必须遵守关于如何提问的僵化规则，或者以为必须套用带有特定步骤和公式化问题的模型才能做好教练时，他们便失去了对教练的热情。

I am sorry to say the misleading beliefs about coaching have changed only slightly since I wrote the first edition of this book. In this chapter, I will revisit and add more context to four of the beliefs from the first edition, and I added one belief about interrupting that keeps showing up in my work with coaches. Hopefully, my analysis will spark constructive dialogue and liberate coaches to use their skills more effectively.

我很遗憾地说，关于教练的误导性信念，自我写本书第一版以来，只发生了微小的改变。在本章中，我将重新审视第一版中的四条信念并补充更多背景，还新增了一条关于“打断”的信念——它在我与教练们的工作中反复出现。希望我的分析能引发建设性对话，并解放教练们，让他们更有效地运用自己的技能。

The unreasonable beliefs, judgments, and assumptions about coaching are ingrained in coaching mythology around the world.

这些关于教练的不合理信念、评判和假设，已深深植根于世界各地的教练迷思之中。

I will explain each belief, looking at why it may be true sometimes and at other times limit the effectiveness of coaching. I will also offer an alternative opinion for each belief, showing ways to loosen the rules the belief created to widen your perspective on how to successfully apply your skills.

我将逐一解释每条信念，探讨它为何有时为真、有时却会限制教练的效果。对每条信念，我也会提供一种替代观点，展示如何松动该信念所制造的规矩，从而拓宽你对于如何成功运用自己技能的认知。

misleading belief #1: the most important skill in coaching is asking powerful questions

误导性信念 #1：教练最重要的技能是提出有力的问题

Where the Belief Comes From

Although the use of reflective statements is taught by many coaching schools, the emphasis on what types of questions to ask leaves students thinking they must remember good questions or make sure they are formulating good questions while coaching.

尽管许多教练院校都教授反思性陈述，但由于过度强调该问哪类问题，学生们会以为自己必须记住好问题，或在教练过程中确保自己正在构思好问题。

Some schools teach that coaching is only a series of open-ended questions. Popular books tout the best questions for leaders and coaches to use. When a coach demonstrates coaching, observers listen for and gather what they think are great questions. They use their list of questions when they feel stuck to keep the conversation moving even though the question is out of context and confusing to the client.

有些院校教导说，教练不过是一连串开放式问题。畅销书也大肆宣扬领导者和教练该用的最佳问题。当一位教练示范教练过程时，观察者会竖起耳朵，收集他们认为绝佳的问题。即便问题脱离语境、让客户感到困惑，他们也会在卡住时搬出自己的问题清单，好让对话继续下去。

What Is True about the Belief

A good question can disturb a client’s ease of using past reasoning for behaviors while testing the accuracy of their thoughts and beliefs in the context of their current problem. Clients might even examine the thoughts that made them judge the situation as a problem. Instead of quickly considering options and actions, a question can prompt clients to stop and reflect on their beliefs and perceptions, which can change their view on what actions to take to achieve what they desire as well as to become the person they aspire to be.

一个好问题能够打破客户轻易沿用旧有推理来指导行为的状态，同时在当前问题的语境下，检验他们想法与信念的准确性。客户甚至可能检视那些让他们把局面判断为“问题”的念头。比起迅速考虑选项和行动，一个问题能促使客户停下来，反思自己的信念和认知，从而改变他们对于“该采取什么行动来实现渴望、并成为自己想要成为的人”的看法。

Every action you take has a reason. You might not be aware of your reasoning unless someone asks the question that causes you to look at what you have been telling yourself to believe.

你的每一个行动都有其理由。除非有人问出那个让你审视自己一直对自己说了什么才相信的问题，否则你可能根本意识不到自己的推理。

Questions help clients assess their beliefs, assumptions, and actions in relation to the presenting situation. The more emotions attached to the current dilemma, the less able clients can sort out their thoughts on their own. A good question prompts alternative ways of seeing the situation and possible approaches.

问题帮助客户在当下情境下评估自己的信念、假设与行动。当前困境牵扯的情绪越多，客户就越难以独立理清思路。一个好问题能引发看待局面的其他方式，以及可能的应对之道。

What Is Not True and Limiting

Only using questions thwarts attempts to make the interaction feel like a flowing conversation, and a barrage of questions can frustrate instead of facilitate the client’s thinking process.

只使用问题，会阻碍互动呈现出流动对话的感觉；一连串问题的轰炸，非但不能促进客户的思考过程，反而令人沮丧。

To create a conversational flow that invites exploration and breakthroughs, coaching should be more than asking a series of questions. Combining reflective summaries and possible paraphrases with questions is more likely to generate the critical thinking needed to discern gaps in logic, evaluate the value and limitations of beliefs, and clarify fears, doubts, and desires affecting the client’s choices of behavior going forward.

要营造一种能诱发探索与突破的对话流，教练不应只是一连串问题的叠加。将反思性总结、可能的释义与问题相结合，更有可能催生出批判性思考，让人分辨逻辑中的漏洞、评估信念的价值与局限，并厘清影响客户未来行为选择的各种恐惧、怀疑与渴望。

Some people think sticking to questions avoids the tendency to insert judgments and biased interpretations, but questions can be tainted by opinions that lead the client to the coach’s way of thinking. Summarizing the client’s key words and phrases and then basing a question on the reflective statement is more likely to be objective than leading.

有人认为，坚持只问问题能避免插入评判和偏见性解读，但问题也可能被导向教练自己思维方式的意见所污染。总结客户的关键词和短语，再基于反思性陈述提出一个问题，往往比引导性问题更客观。

Additionally, the cognitive process of composing an impactful question interrupts the coach’s presence. While the coach is formulating a question, presence is disrupted. I’ve always said “Thinking is the enemy of the coach.” The coach might miss important phrases while thinking. The questions then feel contrived and not a part of a trusting, supportive, and spontaneous conversation.

此外，构思一个有力问题的认知过程，会打断教练的临在。当教练在构思问题时，临在就被破坏了。我常说：“思考是教练的敌人。”教练在思考时，可能错过重要的短语。于是这些问题显得刻意，不再属于一段充满信任、支持且自发的对话。

Alternative Opinion

The opposite of giving advice is not just asking questions. The use of reflective statements, such as summarizing, offering an encapsulation of key phrases with a paraphrase or metaphor, and sharing observed emotional shifts can be powerful even without asking a follow-up question. People often are surprised when they hear their words restated, especially if they have been saying the same words over and over for years. The jolt could create an opening to thinking differently.

给予建议的对立面，并不只是提问。运用反思性陈述——例如总结、用释义或隐喻把关键短语封装起来、分享观察到的情绪转变——即便不追问后续问题，也极具力量。当人们听到自己的话被重述，尤其是当那些话他们已反复说了多年时，往往会感到惊讶。这一震动，可能开启一扇以不同方式思考的门。

When clients pause, give them a moment to think and then ask them what thought came to mind when they looked away or got silent midsentence. This step can be the vital prompt to articulating an emerging awareness. Yet you still may need to reflect what they share because the thought is not yet fully formed. Your reflection helps them crystallize the revelation as they speak it out loud with you.

当客户停顿，给他们一点时间思考，然后问他们：在移开视线或话说到一半沉默时，脑中浮现了什么念头。这一步，可能是帮助他们说出正在成形的觉察的关键触发。然而你可能仍需要反思他们所分享的内容，因为那个念头尚未完全成形。你的反思，能帮助他们在与你一起大声说出的过程中，将这一领悟清晰成形。

Providing a reflective statement before asking a question can also have the effect of making coaching flow more like a casual conversation than an intervention. When the coach first reflects words and expressions and then asks a question, the question is more likely to arise from spontaneous curiosity, not memory.

在提问之前先给出反思性陈述，还能让教练更像一次随意的对话，而非一次干预。当教练先反思客户的言辞与表达，再提出问题，这个问题更可能源于自发的的好奇，而非记忆。

In 2019, one of the objectives the ICF had for updating the coaching competencies was to take the focus off asking questions. They eliminated the competency called “Powerful questioning” and instead incorporated the behaviors into a new competency titled “Evokes awareness.” The new competency, which was updated in 2025, includes the requirement for coaches to “share observations, knowledge and feelings, without attachment, that have the potential to create new insights for the client.” Other behaviors that demonstrate this competency start with the words notices and invites. One of the indications of the competency, “Listens actively,” says, “reflects or summarizes what the client is communicating to ensure clarity and understanding.” Coaching was never meant to be synonymous with asking questions. 1

2019 年，ICF 更新教练能力时，其中一个目标就是不再聚焦于提问。他们取消了名为“有力提问”的能力项，转而将其行为并入一项名为“唤起觉察”的新能力。这项在 2025 年更新的新能力，要求教练“不带依附地分享观察、知识与感受，这些有可能为客户创造新的洞见”。体现该能力的其他行为，都以“注意到”和“邀请”开头。该能力的一项指示“积极倾听”写道：“反思或总结客户所传达的内容，以确保清晰与理解。”教练从来就不该与提问画上等号。¹

Staying present and out of your head is more important than asking the perfect question. The role of the coach is to be a thinking partner, using reflective approaches as well as questions to facilitate the client’s insights and learning.

保持临在、跳出头脑，比问出完美的问题更重要。教练的角色，是成为一位思维伙伴，运用反思性方法以及提问，来促进客户的洞见与学习。

misleading belief #2: don’t ask closed questions

误导性信念 #2：不要问封闭式问题

Where the Belief Comes From

Closed questions tend to generate one-word, yes or no answers. Whether learning how to coach, counsel, or do any other practice where you need a person to provide information, the texts and teaching tout the use of open-ended questions to get detailed answers. Closed questions are discouraged. When I publicly demonstrate coaching, there are always coaches who notice my use of closed questions when clarifying perceptions and desires and ask if I am breaking the rules.

封闭式问题往往只会换来一两个字的“是”或“否”式回答。无论是学习如何教练、咨询，还是任何需要对方提供信息的实践，教材和教学都鼓吹使用开放式问题来获取详尽回答。封闭式问题受到排斥。当我公开示范教练时，总有教练注意到我在澄清认知与渴望时使用了封闭式问题，并问我是不是在破坏规则。

What Is True about the Belief
