One-word answers to closed questions can shut down instead of open up the conversation. These moments can make the coach uncomfortable, feeling the conversation has nowhere to go. The coach then breaks the energetic connection while desperately thinking about what to say next.

对封闭式问题的一字回答，会掐断而非打开对话。这些时刻会让教练感到不适，觉得对话无路可走。于是教练打断了能量上的连接，同时拼命想着下一句该说什么。

The use of closed questions before trust is established can put a full stop to the conversation. The client answers curtly; they might even enjoy upsetting the coach if they are skeptical of the value of the conversation or fear feeling vulnerable. The client needs to feel safe, seen, understood, and comfortable with the coach before fully engaging in coaching.

在信任建立之前使用封闭式问题，可能让对话彻底停摆。客户简短地应答；如果他们本身对对话的价值存疑，或害怕显露脆弱，甚至可能乐于让教练难堪。客户需要感到安全、被看见、被理解，并与教练相处自在，才会全力投入教练过程。

Additionally, closed questions can be disguised attempts to force the client to accept what the coach thinks is most important. Examples of leading closed questions are “Have you tried . . .” or “Do you think you need to change how you are looking at your situation?” or “Is it possible your self-talk is the problem?” These questions limit the direction of the conversation. The client may lose trust in the coaching relationship.

此外，封闭式问题可能是伪装起来的企图，想迫使客户接受教练认为最重要的东西。带有引导性的封闭式问题例如：“你试过……了吗？”或“你是否认为需要改变你看待处境的方式？”或“有没有可能你自我对话本身才是问题？”这类问题限制了对话的方向。客户可能因此失去对教练关系的信任。

Open, exploratory questions encourage a deeper look at motivations for behavior and inaction. Questions that start with what, where, when, who, and even sometimes why provoke the client to be curious about their perceptions and not made to feel that what they see is wrong. Even reluctant clients might recognize the limits of their current perspective when asked an open question that challenges their point of view.

开放式、探索性的提问，能鼓励人们更深入地审视行为与不行动背后的动机。以 what、where、when、who，甚至有时以 why 开头的问题，会激发客户对自己的认知产生好奇，而不让他们觉得自己所看到的是错的。即便是抗拒的客户，在被问到一个挑战其观点的开放式问题时，也可能意识到自己当前视角的局限。

What Is Not True and Limiting

It is not true that all closed questions shut down thinking and inhibit the process of coaching. Closed questions can get more than a closed answer. When providing a concise summary of what the client explained and then asking if the summary is correct can provide clarity for both the coach and client. When asking if a stated belief is true for them can confirm or prompt exploration of the client’s framework. Closed questions can be thought-provoking.

并非所有封闭式问题都会扼杀思考、阻碍教练进程，这一点并不成立。封闭式问题带来的，可能不止一个封闭式的回答。当你简洁地总结客户所解释的内容，然后问这个总结是否正确时，教练与客户双方都能获得清晰。当问及某个被陈述的信念对他们是否属实时，可以确认或引发对客户框架的探索。封闭式问题同样能发人深省。

Case Study

案例研究

A client had left her job to recover from burnout. She enjoyed taking time off, but after six months, she became restless with the desire to go back to work. She hired a coach to help her decide if she should go back to working for a company or start her own business.

一位客户辞职以从职业倦怠中恢复。她很享受这段休假，但六个月后，她开始不安，渴望重返工作。她聘请了一位教练，帮她决定是该回公司上班，还是自己创业。

The coach started by asking her questions about what she most missed about her previous jobs and what she hoped she would never have to do again. The coach then summarized her answer by saying, “You love creating new things with a team of competent people. You don’t like typical management tasks, like doing performance reviews. Did I sum it up correctly?”

教练先问她，之前的工作中最令她怀念的是什么，以及她希望再也不必去做的是什么。然后教练总结她的回答：“你热爱与一队有能力的人一起创造新事物。你不喜欢常规的管理任务，比如做绩效评估。我总结得对吗？”

The client answered, “Exactly,” and went on to further describe what she loved most about her last position.

客户回答：“完全正确。”并进一步描述了她在上一份工作中最热爱之处。

The coach then asked, “You clearly know what you most like to do when you are working. Do you have an idea of what you want for your next job now?”

教练接着问：“你显然很清楚自己工作时最喜欢做什么。现在，对于下一份工作，你有没有想法？”

The client said, “Yes,” and went on to describe what an ideal job might look like.

客户说：“有。”并接着描述了理想工作可能的模样。

The coach then said, “So you said you want to work with a team on exciting new projects. You had said you could freelance, but when describing your ideal job, you kept putting yourself inside an organization. Do you think you have already decided to focus on finding a job working in a company, or is the freelancing idea still on the table?”

教练于是说：“所以你说过想和有团队一起做令人兴奋的新项目。你也说过可以当自由职业者，但描述理想工作时，你一直把自己放在某个组织内部。你是否已经决定专注于找一份公司里的工作，还是自由职业的想法仍在考虑之中？”

The client sighed, agreed that the reflection was correct, and explained why she didn’t really want to start her own business. The closed questions led to both clarification and further exploration of what she wanted her next chapter to look like even if it took a while to create.

客户叹了口气，认同这一反思是对的，并解释了为何她其实并不想自己创业。这些封闭式问题既带来了澄清，也引发了对她希望下一篇章如何展开（即便需要一段时间才能成形）的进一步探索。

The purpose of questions is to disrupt a pattern or flow of thinking and prompt clients to look at and question the limitations of what they have been telling themselves and others. When the coach creates a trusting bond with the client by using reflective statements so they feel seen, understood, and safe, closed questions can be just as provocative as open ones.

问题的目的，在于打断一种思考的模式或流向，促使客户去审视并质疑那些他们一直对自己和他人诉说的限制的真实性。当教练通过反思性陈述与客户建立起信任的纽带，让他们感到被看见、被理解、很安全时，封闭式问题就能和开放式问题一样具有挑衅性。

Alternative Opinion

Closed questions are useful in at least three situations:

封闭式问题至少在三种情境下很有用：

  • ✦ You want to clarify and confirm what clients would like to create or change that is not occurring at the moment. This becomes the result the coach and client agree will be the focus of the conversation.

- ✦ 你想澄清并确认客户此刻想要创造或改变、却尚未发生的事。这便成为教练与客户一致同意、将作为对话焦点的成果。

  • ✦ You want to affirm if a reflective statement is accurate or needs modification. The question is often “Did I get it right?” or “Did I miss anything?”

- ✦ 你想确认一条反思性陈述是否准确，或需要修正。问题通常是“我说对了吗？”或“我有没有漏掉什么？”

  • ✦ You want to prompt clients to articulate an insight when they exhibit a shift in emotions, gestures, posture, and moments of silence. The question might be “Has something shifted for you?” or “Would you share what just came to mind?” These closed questions motivate disclosure.

- ✦ 当客户在情绪、姿态、体态或沉默时刻出现转变时，你想促使他们说出自己的洞见。问题可能是“你有什么发生了变化吗？”或“你愿意分享刚才脑中浮现了什么吗？”这些封闭式问题能激发表露。

You might use many closed questions when applying the essential practices from chapter 8 . This skill starts with narrowing the focus of the conversation and then clarifying what the client wants to create to replace or resolve what is occurring. The coach first listens to the client’s story, listening for what they indicate is most important to change or create and then what they perceive is getting in the way of their desire.

在运用第 8 章的核心实践时，你可能会用到很多封闭式问题。这项技能始于收窄对话的焦点，然后澄清客户想要创造什么，以替代或解决当前正在发生的事。教练先倾听客户的故事，倾听他们暗示最想改变或创造之事，以及他们认为是什么阻碍了自己的渴望。

The coach might first use closed questions to affirm the topic or direction the client wants to focus on. Then, while telling the story, the client might reveal a number of issues to resolve. The coach summarizes the issues and asks which one is most urgent to focus on now. While exploring what needs to be achieved, resolved, or understood for the client to move forward, a new, more important desire might show up, such as building confidence to quiet self-talk, changing an internal or external habit, or accepting an uncomfortable reality. The coach then asks if the client wants to shift the outcome based on what was revealed or stick to the outcome they had agreed on before.

教练可能先使用封闭式问题来确认客户想聚焦的主题或方向。接着，在讲述故事时，客户可能透露出若干待解决的问题。教练总结这些问题，并询问此刻哪一个最紧迫、需要聚焦。在探索客户要向前迈进所必需达成、解决或理解之事时，一个更新、更重要的渴望可能会浮现，例如建立信心以平息自我对话、改变内在或外在习惯，或接受令人不适的现实。教练于是询问：基于所浮现的内容，客户是否想调整成果，还是坚持之前达成一致的成果。

Closed questions serve to clarify and confirm direction, desires, and perceived roadblocks.

封闭式问题用来澄清并确认方向、渴望与感知到的障碍。

Closed questions are useful to test the accuracy and importance of a reflective statement. For example, when you summarize what you think are the key points of what was said or identify an assumption that is creating the fear of taking action, you want to know if the client agrees with your summary or observation. If the client agrees with your statements, the client will likely provide more information.

封闭式问题也有助于检验一条反思性陈述的准确性与重要性。例如，当你总结出你认为对方话语中的要点，或指认出一个正在制造“不敢行动”之恐惧的假设时，你会想知道客户是否同意你的总结或观察。如果客户认同你的陈述，通常就会提供更多资讯。

When clients trust you are there to help them achieve something important to them, they will accept the discomfort of an edgy closed question. For example, if they realize their actions have been sabotaging their desires, you might ask, “Are you willing to act differently to get what you want?” or “Will you regret not committing to making a change a year from now?” You might follow up these closed questions with an open question about what they are now open to explore, but the closed question provided the push off the fence.

当客户相信你是在帮助他们实现对其重要的事，他们就会接受一道尖锐封闭式问题带来的不适感。例如，若他们意识到自己的行为一直在破坏自己的渴望，你可能会问：“你愿意采取不同的行动来得到想要的吗？”或“一年后，你会后悔没有承诺做出改变吗？”你可以在这些封闭式问题之后，跟上一个开放式问题，询问他们现在愿意探索什么，但那道封闭式问题已经把他们推离了骑墙观望的状态。

For closed questions to be effective, clients must know you believe they are smart and resourceful. They must know you are not trying to make them feel wrong or inadequate. Closed questions that follow reflections, such as “Do you want to change this pattern?” or “Have you already made up your mind?” can help them commit if asked with sincere interest and compassion.

要让封闭式问题有效，客户必须知道你相信他们聪明且足智多谋。他们必须知道你并非想让他们觉得自己错了或能力不足。在反思之后提出的封闭式问题，例如“你想改变这个模式吗？”或“你已经下定决心了吗？”只要带着真诚的兴趣与同理心来问，就能帮助他们做出承诺。

I hope coach trainers quit condemning closed questions. These tools are good clarifiers and can provoke examination. Let’s bring them back into our training, mentoring, and practice so all coaches know how to use them well.

我希望教练培训者停止谴责封闭式问题。这些工具是很好的澄清手段，也能激发审视。让我们把它们重新带回培训、辅导与实践中，让所有教练都知道如何善用它们。

misleading belief #3: you should never interrupt a client

误导性信念 #3：你绝不应该打断客户

Where the Belief Comes From

Children are often taught that it is rude to interrupt someone speaking. Although a few cultures see interrupting as a sign of engagement, most cultures declare interrupting is disrespectful and possibly confrontational.

孩子们常被教导，打断别人说话是失礼的。虽然少数文化视打断为投入的表现，但多数文化认定打断是失敬、甚至可能带有对抗意味的。

The common rule of waiting until the speaker indicates they are done speaking is taught by parents and teachers around the world and then carries into adult conversations. Therefore, when coaches are taught to apply this rule to coaching, most accept it without debate.

全世界父母与老师都教导“等说话者表示说完再开口”这条常见规则，它随之延续进成人对话。因此，当教练们被要求把这条规则用于教练时，多数人毫无异议地接受了。

The rule encourages coaches to allow clients to share their stories and perceptions without interruption. The coach must listen to what clients are saying to fully grasp what is on their mind. Interruptions can frustrate the client.

这条规则鼓励教练允许客户不带打断地分享他们的故事与认知。教练必须倾听客户在说什么，才能完全掌握他们的心思。打断可能让客户感到挫败。

What Is True about the Belief

Interrupting can feel intrusive. Clients feel you won’t hear everything you need to know and then won’t understand them. Their responses will be defensive. They might doubt the usefulness of the conversation and the competence of the coach.

打断可能让人觉得侵入。客户会觉得你无法听到需要知道的一切，进而无法理解他们。他们的回应会变得防备。他们可能怀疑对话的用处，以及教练的能力。

Some coaches have the habit of continually interrupting with vocal noise to indicate they are listening, such as “I see” or “mmhmm” or “yes.” Sometimes, these subtle reactions are comforting. They might encourage clients to express themselves more deeply than they would outside of the coaching. Yet sometimes, they disrupt the flow of the conversation. The coach should stay aware of how their habits impact their clients’ willingness to share their perspectives and concerns.

有些教练有不断用出声来打断的习惯，以此表示自己在倾听，比如“我明白”“嗯哼”“对”。有时，这些细微的反应令人安心。它们可能鼓励客户比在教练之外更深地表达自己。但有时，它们也会打断对话的流动。教练应时刻觉察自己的习惯如何影响客户分享观点与顾虑的意愿。

What Is Not True and Limiting

Interruptions are not always disruptive. They can help focus and maintain the direction of the conversation. It’s true that clients choose what they want to focus on. Then, it is the coach’s responsibility to adhere to the agenda set by the client or interrupt to ask about the agenda when it seems to be veering in a new direction. Asking to stop the conversation to clarify focus or to consider redirecting the conversation is useful when the client changes the topic or continually restates the problem without saying anything new.

打断并非总是具有破坏性。它们有助于聚焦并维持对话的方向。客户选择自己想聚焦什么，这固然没错；但随后，教练的责任就是遵循客户设定的议程，或在议程似乎转向新方向时进行打断，询问关于议程的事。当客户转换话题，或不断重复问题却毫无新意时，请求暂停对话以澄清焦点，或考虑重新引导对话，都是有用的。

When I do coaching demonstrations, in the debrief with observers I am often asked, “You interrupted the client. Isn’t that wrong?” or “When you interrupt, aren’t you then leading the conversation to go the way you think it should?” I love these questions because then I can explain when and how I use interruptions to move the conversation forward when it seems to be going in repetitive circles or keeps jumping in different directions. Some coaches are relieved to hear my explanation, giving them guidance on how they can interrupt. Others remain skeptical.

当我做教练示范时，在与观察者的复盘讨论中，常被问到：“你打断了客户。这难道不对吗？”或“你打断时，不就是在把对话引向你认为该走的方向吗？”我很喜欢这些问题，因为这样我就能解释：当对话似乎陷入循环重复，或不断跳向不同方向时，我会在何时、如何运用打断来推进它。有些教练听了解释如释重负，获得了如何打断的指引。另一些则仍持怀疑。

Without ever interrupting, the conversation will often bounce around and lose focus. The coach ends up chasing the client. A solution might be discovered, but it often has nothing to do with what they stated they wanted to focus on at the beginning of the session.

如果从不打断，对话往往会四处弹跳、失去焦点。教练最终在追着客户跑。也许会找到某个解决方案，但它常常与客户在开场时所说想聚焦的毫不相干。

Alternative Opinion

When interrupting a client has a clear purpose, it can keep the conversation on track to achieving the desired outcome or bring attention to something else that keeps reappearing that could be important to explore. Interrupting to reflect the bouncing focus of topics might shift the desired outcome of the coaching if the client realizes they need to address another issue before they can move forward.

当打断客户有明确目的时，它能让对话保持在通往期望成果的轨道上，或把注意力引向某个反复出现、或许值得探索的重要事物。如果客户意识到自己需要先处理另一个问题才能前进，那么打断以反映话题焦点的游移，可能会改变教练的期望成果。

The art of interrupting is a good skill to learn. When the coach notices the client has shifted focus or says something important outside of the current story but important enough to explore, the interruption is a good coaching tool.

打断的艺术，是值得学习的好技能。当教练注意到客户已转移焦点，或说出了当前故事之外、却重要到值得探索的内容时，这一打断就是很好的教练工具。

The following situations are some examples of opportunities to interrupt with purpose:

以下情境，是一些带有目的的打断机会示例：

  • ✦ When clients talk in circles, repeating what is frustrating, wrong, or troublesome, an interruption can feed their need to feel heard and understood. Asking to interrupt to summarize their perceived problem, paired with a question about what needs to change could move the conversation forward.

- ✦ 当客户绕圈子，重复那些令人沮丧、错误或麻烦之事时，一次打断能满足他们“被听见、被理解”的需求。请求打断以总结他们所感知的问题，并配上一个关于“需要改变什么”的问题，能推进对话。

  • ✦ When clients provide unnecessary details about past events or from their childhood history that is affecting their confidence, an interruption may bring them back into the present moment. The conversation should stay focused on what needs to be addressed or resolved in the present and future, not on what has occurred in the past. Interrupting to ask how their past experiences are impacting how they are dealing with their current situation can be the transition point. Then, refocus the conversation to what is possible for them to change today.

- ✦ 当客户提供关于过往事件或童年经历中、影响其自信的不必要细节时，一次打断能把他们拉回当下。对话应聚焦于当下与未来需要面对或解决之事，而非已经发生的过去。打断以询问“过去的经历如何影响他们处理当前处境”，可以成为一个过渡点。然后，把对话重新聚焦于他们今天有可能改变的事。

  • ✦ When clients say something important, quickly say “but,” and then justify their doubts, an interruption can throw a spotlight on these statements. They often don’t realize they stated their desire and then discounted it. An interruption that reinforces their statement of what they desire and a request to examine the likelihood of their fears coming true could shift clients’ thinking about what is possible, leading to an important breakthrough.

- ✦ 当客户说了重要的话，紧接着很快说“但是”，然后为自己的怀疑辩解时，一次打断能把聚光灯打在这些陈述上。他们往往没有意识到，自己刚说出渴望，又随即贬低了它。一次能强化其所陈述渴望、并请他们审视恐惧成真可能性的打断，可能转变客户对“何为可能”的思考，带来重要突破。

I have a formula for interrupting that invites clients to accept my interruption or not. When our conversations have a pattern of repeating what has been said, or they backtrack to a different issue, these steps can be critical to keep the momentum flowing:

我有一套邀请客户接受（或不接受）我打断的公式。当我们的对话出现重复已说内容、或退回到另一个问题的模式时，这些步骤对保持势头流动至关重要：

  • Ask for permission to interrupt.

- 请求允许打断。

  • State the purpose of the interruption.

- 说明打断的目的。

  • Briefly share what you heard or noticed that is prompting you to ask a question.

- 简要分享你听到或注意到的、促使你提问的内容。

  • Ask your question about what a word or statement they said means to them in this context, or ask them to share their response to the backtracking or wandering thoughts that you noticed. You may then ask what they want to focus on now.

- 提出你的问题：关于某个词或陈述在此语境下对他们意味着什么，或请他们分享对所注意到“退步或游离”念头所生出的回应。你随后可以问他们此刻想聚焦什么。

For example, you might start by saying, “Can I interrupt you to make sure I know where this conversation is going?” or “Can I stop you for a moment to share something you just said that could be important?” or “Can I ask you about a topic you keep bringing up that seems to impact your progress?”

例如，你可以这样开头：“我可以打断你一下，确认我知道这场对话要走向哪里吗？”或“我能先停你一下，分享你刚说的、可能很重要的内容吗？”或“我能问一个你一直提起、似乎影响你进展的话题吗？”

Robert Biswas-Diener, in his book Positive Provocation, says that using interruptions to manage the conversation or call out a critical statement for clarity or awareness is using strategic interruptions. 2 Biswas-Diener says coaches need to reflect on their own beliefs about intruding in a conversation to build their courage and proficiency to effectively use the art of interrupting.

Robert Biswas-Diener 在他的《Positive Provocation》一书中说，运用打断来管理对话、或点出某个关键陈述以求得清晰或觉察，就是在使用“策略性打断”。² Biswas-Diener 说，教练需要反思自己关于“介入对话”的信念，以建立起有效运用打断艺术的勇气与熟练度。

misleading belief #4: coaching must ensure clients have a clear vision of their desired future at the start of the conversation

误导性信念 #4：教练必须在对话一开始就确保客户对其理想未来有清晰的愿景

Where the Belief Comes From

I am not aware of any coach training that doesn’t include how to establish the goal of the coaching session, though some people think asking a simple question about what the focus should be is enough. The Coaching Research Laboratory at the Weatherhead School of Management at Case Western Reserve University says accepting what clients say they want from coaching in the first minute or two is not enough. Their research touts the power of taking time to envision a clear, positive outcome and suggests it should be a requirement in the coaching process. 3

据我所知，没有任何教练培训不包含“如何确立教练环节目标”的内容，尽管有人认为，问一个关于焦点应该是什么的简单问题就足够了。凯斯西储大学韦瑟黑德管理学院的教练研究实验室指出，在开头一两分钟就接受客户所说“想从教练中得到什么”并不够。他们的研究鼓吹“花时间设想一个清晰、积极的成果”的力量，并建议它应成为教练过程的一项要求。³

When you have a visible target in mind, instead of focusing on what is intolerable, the coaching focuses on moving toward what is possible. Focusing on the desired future scenario keeps the conversation supportive even while exploring issues that stir up emotions.

当你心中有一个可见的目标，而非聚焦于无法忍受之事时，教练就会专注于朝“可能之事”迈进。即便在探索搅动情绪的问题时，聚焦于期望的未来情境，也能让对话保持支持性。

What Is True about the Belief

One of the most important yet difficult aspects of coaching is keeping the conversation on track to a fulfilling resolution. Although the direction of coaching can change more than once in the conversation, if what is desired as an outcome is clear, progress will likely be made.

教练中最重要却也最困难的面向之一，是让对话保持在通往圆满解决的轨道上。尽管对话方向可能在过程中改变不止一次，但如果期望作为成果的东西是清晰的，就很可能会取得进展。

When the desired outcome to be achieved in the coaching session is vague, the conversation will often go in circles, cycling back to the details of the problem or the shoulds that need to be obeyed. Clients might declare next steps when closing out the session, but they might sort of do them or find a reason for not doing them immediately because they weren’t motivated to persist.

当教练环节中期望达成的成果模糊时，对话往往会绕圈子，循环回到问题的细节或那些必须服从的“应该”。客户在收尾时可能宣告下一步，但因为缺乏坚持的动力，他们可能“有点”去做，或找个理由不立即做。

I, too, believe taking time to formulate the observable desired outcome is ideal before exploring what is stopping my smart client from achieving their desire. I coach slow to discover what the client really wants that they don’t have at the moment. Then discovering what they will do next feels easier.

我也相信，在探索是什么阻挡了我那位聪明的客户去实现其渴望之前，先花时间明确可观察的期望成果是理想的。我慢慢地教练，去发现客户此刻真正想要、却尚未拥有的东西。然后，去发现他们下一步要做什么，就变得更容易。

What Is Not True and Limiting

It is not always true that the client must have a clear vision of a desired future at the start of the session. Clients are often unsure what they want from the outset. The best they can do is describe the circumstances that have them feeling uncertain or anxious or stuck. Pushing them to draw a picture of what they want can push them away.

客户必须在环节开始时对期望的未来有清晰愿景，这一点并不总是成立。客户往往从一开始就不确定自己想要什么。他们最多只能描述令自己感到不确定、焦虑或困住的情形。强迫他们描绘想要的画面，反而会把他们推远。

Case Study

案例研究

Once, when I was leading a group mentoring session, the coach was urging the client to create a clear, positive picture of the future she wanted to create. The client said she wasn’t ready to declare what she wanted her future to look like. She wanted to first talk about the options she had at the moment and have the coach help her see any biases, fears, or assumptions she may be making when she talked through her choices.

有一次，我带领一个团体辅导环节，那位教练一直催促客户去创造一个清晰、积极的未来画面。客户说，她还没准备好宣告自己想要未来是什么样子。她想先谈谈此刻手头的选项，并让教练帮她看清：在梳理自己的选择时，她可能抱持了哪些偏见、恐惧或假设。

The coach kept urging her to imagine her best future as an anchor for the coaching session. The client resisted. The coach reached toward her and beckoned her to stand up and walk toward her future to see what might become clear. The client started to cry. I stopped the session before more damage was done.

教练不断催促她把最好的未来想象出来，作为教练环节的锚点。客户抗拒。教练伸手示意她站起来，走向她的未来，看看什么会变得清晰。客户开始哭泣。我在更多伤害发生前叫停了环节。

The coach was taught this formulaic approach that had her overlook the client’s stated needs. It’s not that the client could not imagine a desired future before the session ended, but she wanted to process her thinking with her coach first to clear the fog of her uncertainty before she could see where she wanted her career path to go.

这位教练被教导了这套公式化做法，以至于忽视了客户明确表达的需求。并不是客户在环节结束前无法想象期望的未来，而是她想先与教练一起梳理自己的思考，清除不确定性的迷雾，然后才能看清自己的职业道路要走向何方。

Alternative Opinion

The outcome of a coaching conversation must be clear, but it often comes to light through coaching, not before. At the beginning of a session, clients might be able to only state a desire for clarity around what is stopping them from claiming what they want or what is at the root of their frustration. As the session progresses, the fears based on past experiences, the worst case scenarios clouding their view, and worries about how they will be judged by others emerge as the coach reflects and asks about what they are saying. From here, a desired outcome often emerges. This vision is often personal, such as “I want to develop the confidence when having difficult conversations.” As the coaching continues and clients further delve into their thoughts and emotions, what they actually want to happen in the future becomes crystal clear—or not. They still might need time to think about what they want to happen and who they want to be when the conversation feels difficult.

教练对话的成果必须清晰，但它往往是在教练过程中、而非之前，才浮现出来。在环节开始时，客户或许只能陈述一种渴望：想看清是什么阻挡了他们去主张自己想要的东西，或是什么构成了他们挫败感的根源。随着环节推进，基于过往经验的恐惧、遮蔽视野的最坏情境，以及“别人会如何评判自己”的担忧，会随教练的反思与追问而浮现。由此，一个期望成果往往应运而生。这个愿景往往是个人化的，例如“我想在困难对话中培养出自信”。随着教练的继续、客户更深入地探究自己的想法与情绪，他们未来真正想发生的事会变得清晰——也可能不会。他们可能仍需要时间，去思考在对话令人不适时，自己想让什么发生、想成为谁。

Clients often cannot articulate what they really want to happen until the coach helps them sort out their doubts, beliefs, and fears.

客户往往要等教练帮他们理清怀疑、信念与恐惧之后，才能说出自己真正想要发生的事。

Success in a session may not always include steps toward a tangible, realistic outcome. Committing to taking the time to think about what they discovered in the coaching session may be all they can agree to do at the moment. As they process their insights, what they want to create may become clear. They might even take a few steps toward what they want to change or create before the next session. Sometimes, the best learning happens between sessions.

一个环节的成功，未必总包含通向具体、现实成果的步骤。当下他们所能同意去做的，可能仅仅是承诺花时间去思考在教练环节中的发现。当他们消化这些洞见时，想创造的东西可能会清晰起来。他们甚至可能在下次环节前，就朝想改变或创造的方向迈出几步。有时，最好的学习发生在环节之间。

misleading belief #5: it takes a long time to be a good coach

误导性信念 #5：成为一名好教练需要很长时间

Where the Belief Comes From

New coaches love seeing demonstrations by experienced coaches. They enjoy the performance, but then often feel inadequate when they compare their own capabilities. Even if the coach breaks down pivotal moments in the coaching—the emotional shifts, the repeated key words, the clear beliefs significant to the challenges or desired outcome—the observers wonder if they will ever be as good as the coach doing the demo.

新教练喜欢看资深教练的示范。他们享受这场表演，但拿自己的能力与之比较时，常感到不足。即便教练拆解了教练中的关键节点——情绪转变、反复出现的关键词、对挑战或期望成果至关重要的清晰信念——观察者仍会怀疑自己是否能达到做示范者的水平。

The possibility of observers feeling both awe and doubt increases when the coach doing the demonstration doesn’t consider the developmental level of the audience. The demonstration shows off the coach’s skills more than providing a learning experience. Observers then feel it will take a long time, if ever, to be a good coach.

当做示范的教练不考虑观众的发展阶段时，观察者同时感到敬畏与怀疑的可能性就会增加。示范更多是在炫耀教练的技巧，而非提供学习体验。于是观察者觉得，要成为一名好教练，即便有可能，也得花很长时间。

There is also the common desire of humans to be perfect, or at least pretty good, before they risk stepping into a role that will be judged by others. Coaches think they must reach a particular level of proficiency before they become a real professional coach.

人类还有一种常见的欲望：在被他人评判的角色面前，总想先做到完美，或至少相当不错。教练们认为，自己必须先达到某种熟练程度，才能成为“真正的专业”教练。

What Is True about the Belief

Undoing the habits of telling and advising to fully embrace the curiosity and comfort of not knowing, which is needed to coach, takes time. Both new and experienced coaches want to feel helpful and useful and that they provide value. If they worry about being judged by their clients, they jump to talking about solutions before exploring the limiting stumbling blocks their clients have allowed to dominate their thinking.

要改掉“告知与建议”的习惯，全然拥抱教练所需的“好奇与对未知的自在”，是需要时间的。无论新教练还是资深教练，都想感到自己有帮助、有用，并提供了价值。如果他们担心被客户评判，就会在探索客户允许主导其思考的那些限制性绊脚石之前，急于谈论解决方案。

Developing trust in yourself as a coach and in the magic of coaching takes deliberate practice and patience. This is the reason it takes many coaching hours to earn a globally recognized credential.

对自己作为教练的信任，以及对教练魔力的信任，需要刻意的练习与耐心。这正是为何需要大量教练时长，才能取得全球公认的认证。

What Is Not True and Limiting

It is not true that you have to reach a certain level of proficiency before you seek clients. You are providing value when you give a busy person a moment to unreservedly talk about what they are facing, knowing they won’t be judged for what they disclose. The time with you might be the only moment in their day when they feel they can relax, breathe, and freely talk.

你不必达到某种熟练程度才能去寻找客户，这一点并不成立。当你给予一个忙碌的人片刻时光，让他毫无保留地谈论所面对的事，且知道所披露的内容不会被评判时，你就在提供价值。与你相处的这点时间，可能是他们一天中唯一能放松、呼吸、自由交谈的时刻。

When creating my first website after a year of coaching, I felt like a fraud. I was painfully aware of my inexperience and weaknesses. My designer set up a testimonial page. It prompted me to read the glowing testimonials from my clients. What they liked the most was the safe space I gave them to talk. They appreciated when I asked them clarifying questions that made them challenge their own thinking. They said they felt stronger about their choices and plans. They valued my imperfect coaching.

在教练一年后要创建我的第一个网站时，我觉得自己像个骗子。我痛苦地意识到自己的缺乏经验与弱点。我的设计师建了一个证言页。它促使我去读客户们那些热情的证言。他们最喜爱的，是我给予他们畅谈的安全空间。他们感激我在提问澄清时，让他们挑战了自己的思考。他们说，对自己做出的选择和计划感到更有力量。他们看重我不完美的教练。

Alternate Opinion

It doesn’t take a long time to be someone’s trusted thinking partner. If you are being fully present, listening wholeheartedly without judgment, and caring about them as humans trying to do their best, they probably aren’t judging your skills.

要成为某人信赖的思维伙伴，并不需要很长时间。如果你全然临在，不带评判地全心倾听，并关心他们这两个努力做到最好的人，他们大概并不会评判你的技巧。

Trust the coaching process will produce results when people feel safe, seen, and valued.

只要人们感到安全、被看见、被重视，就请信任教练过程会带来结果。

However, being a good listener is just a good start. You still need solid coach training from an accredited coach training school or recognized academic program so you get better and provide more value for your clients over time. Like any profession, you start with the basics and then move forward on your path of mastery with practice.

然而，做个好倾听者只是个好的开始。你仍然需要来自认证教练培训院校或受认可学术项目的扎实教练训练，才能随着时间进步，为客户创造更多价值。像任何职业一样，你从基础起步，然后借由练习，走上精通之路。

Coaching development is ongoing. When the COVID-19 pandemic had us staying home, I agreed to create a self-study online course using the foundational Breakthrough Coaching program I was offering in partnership with Coaching.com . 4 One of my tasks was to go through thirty coaching demonstrations we recorded over the three years I taught the program live. I could barely watch the first year. Even after coaching for twenty-five years, I cringed at my performance. Yes, everyone loved the demos, but I could clearly see how I grew over those three years. I threw out the first ten demos; I had plenty to choose from as I improved. I realized I got better every year. If you have to be perfect to coach, no one would ever experience the gift of coaching!

教练发展是持续的。当 COVID-19 疫情让我们居家时，我答应与 Coaching.com 合作，用我一直在讲授的基础 Breakthrough Coaching 课程，开发一个自学线上课程。⁴ 我的任务之一，是过一遍我在该课程现场讲授三年间录下的三十场教练示范。第一年我几乎看不下去。即便在教练了二十五年之后，我仍对自己的表现感到龇牙咧嘴。是的，人人都爱那些示范，但我能清楚地看到自己那三年间的成长。我扔掉了前十个示范；随着我进步，我有许多可选。我意识到自己每一年都在变好。如果非得完美才能教练，那谁都永远体验不到教练的礼物！

You can coach while you are learning. If you resist giving advice, you won’t cause harm. What makes coaching so powerful is the relationship between the coach and client, the human energetic bond I explained in the introduction. If clients feel safe with you, the conversation can spark insights even when you are just learning your skills. If you believe they are smart enough to think through their dilemmas, learn how to catch and release your judgments and fears as explained in the next section of this book, and feel patient and curious and sincerely care about your clients, they will find value in your coaching.

你可以在学习的同时就从事教练。如果你忍住不给建议，就不会造成伤害。教练之所以如此有力，在于教练与客户之间的关系，即我在引言中解释过的、人与人之间的能量纽带。如果客户与你在一起感到安全，即便你还在学习技巧，对话也能激发洞见。如果你相信他们足够聪明，能想通自己的困境，学会如本书下一节所述去捕捉并放下你的评判与恐惧，并感到耐心、好奇，且真诚关心你的客户，他们就会在你的教练中找见价值。

Part 5

Part II The Three Mental Habits for Co-Regulation

People are just as wonderful as sunsets if I can let them be.... I don’t try to control a sunset. I watch with awe as it unfolds.

若能任其本然，人与落日一样美妙……我不去控制落日。我带着敬畏，看它徐徐展开。

—C ARL R OGERS

— CARL ROGERS

T HE EFFECTIVENESS OF a coaching conversation depends on the energetic connection generated through the coach’s full-body presence. This presence not only establishes a sense of safety from the psychological harm of judgment, but the co-regulation of emotions generated by the coach also encourages the client’s willingness to share and learn by feeding the human needs of being seen, accepted, and valued.

一次教练对话的效力，取决于由教练全人临在所生成的能量联结。这种临在不仅确立了免受评判之心理伤害的安全感，而且由教练生成的情绪共同调节，也通过满足「被看见、被接纳、被重视」这些人类需求，催生客户分享与学习的意愿。

the biochemistry of coaching presence

Clinical psychologist Deb Dana writes in her book The Polyvagal Theory in Therapy about how connectedness that supports feelings of safety and trust is a “biological imperative” for stimulating the neurobiological foundation required for health, growth, and restoration. 1 Dana explores the role the central nervous system plays when talking and listening. When the interaction is accepting and respectful, nervous systems can co-regulate, stimulating a “range of responses including calm, happy, meditative, engaged, attentive, active, interested, excited, passionate, alert, ready, relaxed, savoring, and joyful.”

临床心理学家 Deb Dana 在她的《The Polyvagal Theory in Therapy》一书中写道，那种支撑安全感与信任的联结，是刺激健康、成长与修复所需的神经生物学基础的「生物性必然」¹。Dana 探讨了中枢神经系统在说与听时的作用。当互动是接纳且尊重的，神经系统便能共同调节，激发出「一系列反应，包括平静、快乐、冥想、投入、专注、活跃、感兴趣、兴奋、热情、警觉、就绪、放松、品味与喜悦」。

When coaching, this biological and emotional experience is also the foundation that facilitates clients to have breakthroughs in their thinking. The magic happens in this co-regulated connection between two people. There is an energetic field in the space between and around the coach and client I have often referred to as the safety bubble.

在教练中，这种生物与情绪体验，也是促成客户在思维上取得突破的基础。魔力就发生于两人之间这种共同调节的联结里。在教练与客户之间及其周遭的空间中，存在着一个能量场，我常称之为「安全泡」。

The energy exchange between coach and client is critical for the technical coaching skills to be effective. Your accepting, compassionate, and courageous presence quiets their stress response with the release of three neurotransmitters and hormones. Oxytocin stimulates feelings of acceptance, psychological safety, and social bonding, while serotonin and dopamine enhance their mood, memory, and the motivation to explore with you what is possible for them. Add in a touch of laughter that often occurs when clients have an aha moment, and the pleasure-inducing endorphins combine with the other neurochemicals to produce what David J. P. Phillips calls the “angel’s cocktail.” 2 The conversation becomes an emotional dance often ending with a firm commitment to take a step forward within days, if not hours.

教练与客户之间的能量交换，对技术性教练技巧能否奏效至关重要。你那接纳、慈悲而勇敢的临在，借着三种神经递质与荷尔蒙的释放，平复了他们的压力反应。催产素激发接纳感、心理安全感与社会联结，而血清素与多巴胺则提升他们的情绪、记忆，以及与你一同探索其可能性的动力。再加上客户顿悟时刻常伴有的那一丝欢笑，带来愉悦的内啡肽便与其他神经化学物质结合，生成 David J. P. Phillips 所谓的「天使鸡尾酒」²。这场对话变成一场情绪之舞，常常在数日、乃至数小时内，以一份坚定的前进一步的承诺作结。

I have found the impact of this co-regulated energy exchange has a head-and-heart opening effect even with resistant leaders and skeptical clients. Coaching then becomes a co-creative process where they willingly work with the coach to achieve their goals.

我发现，这种共同调节的能量交换，即便面对抗拒的领导者与多疑的客户，也具有一种开启头脑与心灵的效应。教练于是成为一种共创的过程，在其中他们心甘情愿地与教练协作，去实现自己的目标。

Dana also writes, “Although as we mature from babies to adults, our brains override patterns of connection with patterns of protection but our autonomic nervous system never stops needing, and longing for, co-regulation.” 3 The humans we work with yearn for this connection whether they know it or not. Coaching starts the moment they sense your open-minded curiosity, tender compassion, and purposeful courage before you utter a word.

Dana 还写道：「尽管当我们从婴孩长成成人，大脑会用保护模式覆盖联结模式，但我们的自主神经系统从未停止需要、也从未停止渴望共同调节。」³ 我们共事的人，无论是否知晓，都渴求这种联结。教练始于他们感知到你开放的好奇、温柔的慈悲，与 purposeful 的勇气——甚至在你开口之前。

Often, observers of my coaching demonstrations say clients come to me already open and trusting. I doubt they do, knowing they are being observed by many strangers, including me. Often, I have never met them before, but we make an immediate connection. The caring, nonjudgmental presence I have spent years developing calms their brain’s quest for protection. My open presence also gives me the ability to precisely attune to their thoughts and emotions so they feel seen and understood by my reflections and questions. The flow looks easy even when their emotions creep in because I want it to feel risk-free and timeless for us both.

常有人观看我的教练演示后说，客户是带着开放与信任来到我面前的。我对此表示怀疑，因为他们知道自己正被许多陌生人——包括我——观察。往往我此前从未见过他们，但我们却立刻建立了联结。我多年来修炼而成的那种关怀、不带评判的临在，平息了他们大脑对保护的渴求。我开放的临在，也赋予我精准契合他们思绪与情绪的能力，使他们在我的映照与提问中感到被看见、被理解。即便他们的情绪悄然渗入，这流动也显得轻松，因为我想让它对我们双方都无风险、无时限。

I believe we move forward on our paths of coaching mastery by deepening our compassionate, selfless presence. Reflections and questions that emerge from the spontaneous interaction when we co-regulate are more powerful than those we concoct from our memory.

我相信，我们正是通过深化那慈悲、忘我的临在，在教练精通之路上前行。从共同调节中自发涌现的映照与提问，比我们从记忆中拼凑的要更有力量。

why coaching skills overshadow presence

When working with DJ Mitsch to develop the curricula for her coaching school, the Healthcare Coaching Institute, we debated whether to start with teaching full-body, mind, and spiritual presence or start with the foundational skills. We decided that teaching self-awareness and coaching presence first would help the students shift from advising, mentoring, prescribing, and counseling to coaching.

在与 DJ Mitsch 合作开发其教练学校——医疗教练研究所（Healthcare Coaching Institute）——的课程时，我们曾辩论：该从教授全人、心智与精神的临在开始，还是从基础技巧开始。我们决定，先教自我觉察与教练临在，会帮助学生从劝告、指导、开处方与咨询，转向真正的教练。

The students reacted with frustration. They wanted to know how to do coaching first. They wanted to know what questions to ask, what model to follow, and what competencies they needed to demonstrate to earn their certification.

学生们却报以沮丧。他们想先知道如何做教练。他们想知道该问什么问题、该遵循什么模型、该展示哪些能力才能拿到证照。

I found this desire to first learn what to say as a coach in most countries where I have taught coaching skills, especially in Asia, where children are often required to excel in academics to win both the approval of their parents and success in society. Their cognitive skills are the source of praise or punishment.

我在多数教过教练技巧的国家，都发现这种先学「该说什么」的渴望，尤其在亚洲——那里孩子常被要求学业优异，以赢得父母的认可与社会的成功。他们的认知技能，是赞美或惩罚的来源。

Most coaching students seek to learn the right questions to ask. They need to know what they have to do to pass the tests. They coach with a sense of urgency to sort through possible solutions to give the client something to do.

多数教练学员寻求学习「正确」的提问。他们需要知道必须通过什么测试。他们带着一种紧迫感来教练，急于梳理出可行方案，好给客户一点事做。

When writing my book The Discomfort Zone, I searched for ways to train people to infuse an open, accepting, caring presence into their active coaching skills. The book was intended for leaders and coaches to get good results from difficult conversations. Teaching the importance of mental and emotional preparation before entering the room seemed like a logical first step for staying calm when others reacted.

在撰写我的《The Discomfort Zone》一书时，我寻找训练人们将开放、接纳、关怀的临在注入其主动教练技巧的方法。那本书面向领导者与教练，意在从艰难对话中取得好结果。在进房之前先做心智与情绪准备，作为在他人反应时保持冷静的合理第一步，看似顺理成章。

When faced with the uncertainty of coaching from presence, not memory, many coaches retreat behind their fear of failure and question their value as a coach unless they give advice.

当面对「从临在而非记忆出发去教练」的不确定性时，许多教练退回到对失败的恐惧之后，并质疑自己作为教练的价值——除非他们给出建议。

I began to teach mental preparation as the critical first step of the coaching conversation instead of separate prework. Students then accepted this skill as one of their technical competencies. They found the better they got at mental preparation, the more effective they were at coaching.

于是我开始把心智准备作为教练对话关键的第一步来教，而非单独的课前作业。学生们随后便把这技能接纳为他们的一项技术能力。他们发现，心智准备做得越好，教练就越有效。

The ICF added a new coaching competency in 2019 called “Embodies a coaching mindset,” which also stresses the importance of developing a coaching presence to ensure meaningful and lasting coaching results. Yet even when mental preparation is deemed a competency, I still find many coaches miss important phrases and reactions at the beginning of their coaching sessions as they try to remember how to get an agreement and then jump too quickly to problem-solving and action-planning mode.

ICF 在 2019 年新增了一项教练能力，名为「体现教练思维」，同样强调发展教练临在以确保有意义、可持续的教练成果的重要性。然而，即便心智准备被视为一项能力，我仍发现许多教练在对话伊始漏掉了重要的措辞与反应——他们急于记住如何达成共识，接着便过快跳向问题解决与行动计划模式。

I believe coach training needs to start with teaching the skills of co-regulation that inspire new coaches to energetically connect with clients to support the application of their verbal coaching skills. Seeing the results they get from practicing mental preparation and maintenance can motivate students to trust the process of coaching instead of shifting to advising and coaxing when they aren’t sure of what to say. This is the reason why this edition of Coach the Person, Not the Problem puts the relational skills—the three mental habits of co-regulation—before the section that focuses on the active skills of coaching.

我相信，教练培训需要从教授共同调节的技巧开始，激励新教练与客户建立能量上的联结，以支撑其言语教练技巧的运用。看到练习心智准备与维持所带来的结果，能促使学生信任教练的过程，而非在自己不知说什么时滑向劝告与诱导。这正是《Coach the Person, Not the Problem》这一版把关系性技巧——那三种共同调节的心智习惯——置于聚焦主动教练技巧的章节之前的原因。

co-regulation is the foundation of connection

When I teach leaders to use a coaching approach in their conversations, they often complain, “No matter what I say, they don’t open up to me.” My answer is always, “How are you listening?” Then, I explain that leaders must establish psychological safety with a clear other-focused intention and genuine care before their direct reports will trust they won’t be hurt when they share their thoughts and feelings.

当我教领导者在对话中运用教练方法时，他们常抱怨：「不管我说什么，他们都不对我敞开。」我的回答总是：「你是怎么听的？」接着我解释，领导者必须先确立心理安全感——带着清晰的、以他人为中心的意图与真切的关怀——其下属才会信任：当他们分享思绪与感受时不会受伤。

The late leadership coaching expert Doug Silsbee said, “In fact, our ability to facilitate lasting, sustainable development in others absolutely rests on the presence that we offer to the relationship.” 4 In his book Presence-Based Coaching, Silsbee defined the energy between two people as the relational field. The mood we sense in the field is often referred to as the atmosphere between two people. The strength of this energy can now be measured. 5

已故的领导力教练专家 Doug Silsbee 说：「事实上，我们促成他人持久、可持续发展能力，绝对依赖于我们带入这段关系的临在。」⁴ 在《Presence-Based Coaching》一书中，Silsbee 将两人之间的能量定义为关系场。我们在这个场中感知到的情绪，常被称为两人之间的「氛围」。如今这能量的强度已可测量⁵。

The energy you create with your emotions has substance. The relational field is palpable and powerful.

你以情绪创造的 energetic，是有实质的。关系场是可感知且有力的。

Even if you are adept at hiding your emotions, clients will be affected by even small shifts in your energy. Carl Jung said, “The meeting of two personalities is like the contact of two chemical substances.” 6 We either open up or shut down according to the emotions we pick up from the socially dominant person in the conversation. Unless the coach feels intimidated and the power balance shifts to the client, the client will respond to the emotions of the coach as being the socially dominant person by nature of the role.

即便你擅长隐藏情绪，客户仍会受到你能量中哪怕微小变化的影响。Carl Jung 说：「两个人格的相遇，犹如两种化学物质的接触。」⁶ 我们或因拾起对话中 socially dominant 之人的情绪而敞开，或因之封闭。除非教练感到被威慑、权力天平倒向客户，否则客户会依据教练作为角色天然的社会主导者所流露的情绪来反应。

Because clients often feel uncertain and cautious, you must consciously set and maintain your curious and compassionate emotional state. Then, you need to sustain this state to keep the conversation flowing no matter what happens in the session.

因为客户常感不确定与谨慎，你必须自觉地设定并维持你好奇、慈悲的情绪状态。接着，你需要让这状态持续，无论对话中发生什么，都保持流动。

psychological safety

The brain is always on alert for danger. It will stay in protective mode until it senses it is safe to engage. But it is up to the coach to let their client’s brain know their time together will not be mentally harmful. Employment engagement expert William Kahn said people feel safe when they do not fear there will be “negative consequences of self-image, status or career.” 7

Trust and safety are not the same. People must first feel safe from ridicule, retribution, or loss before they will give you their trust.

大脑始终在警觉危险。在感知到可以安全投入之前，它会停留在保护模式。但要让客户的大脑知道，共处的时间不会造成心理伤害，这要由教练来负责。员工敬业度专家 William Kahn 说，当人们不担心会有「自我形象、地位或生涯方面的负面后果」时，他们便感到安全⁷。

信任与安全并不相同。人们必须先感到免受嘲笑、报复或损失，才会把信任交给你。

When clients feel you care, accept without judgment their present perspective as valid for them right now, and believe they have the capability to learn and grow, they feel safe enough to trust you. Your good intentions will be lost if you slip into feeling impatient, judgmental, or hesitant.

The following chapters will help you implement the three mental habits that generate the co-regulation needed to establish both the psychological safety and the willingness to engage in the coaching process with you:

当客户感到你在乎、不加评判地接纳他们当下的视角（对他们此刻而言是有效的）、并相信他们具备学习与成长的能力时，他们便感到足够安全，从而信任你。若你滑入不耐烦、爱评判或犹疑，你的好意便会落空。

  • Align your nervous system.
  • Receive (don’t just listen).
  • Catch and release judgment.

接下来的章节将助你落实那三种心智习惯，它们生成所需的共同调节，以在你与客户之间建立心理安全感与投入的意愿：

- 调谐你的神经系统。

Integrating the routines suggested will help you embed the mindset that will enhance the impact of your coaching skills.

- 接收（不要只是倾听）。

- 捕捉并释放评判。

Chapter 6

CHAPTER 3 ALIGN YOUR NERVOUS SYSTEM

Real listening is a willingness to

let the other person change you.

真正的倾听，是一种让对方改变你的意愿。

—A LAN A LDA

——艾伦·阿尔达（ALAN ALDA）

A COMMONLY HELD belief is that practice makes perfect. Some people say you can achieve mastery with one thousand hours of practice. 1 While it’s true that practice can make any performance, including coaching, easier and more effective, practice does not put you on the path of mastery in coaching if you don’t include developing your full-body presence in your practice. Mastery in coaching comes from connection, not repetition.

一种普遍持有的信念是：练习造就完美。有人说，一千小时的练习就能达到精通。¹ 虽然练习确实能让包括教练在内的任何表现都更轻松、更有效，但如果你在练习中没有纳入发展全身临在，练习并不会把你带上教练精通之路。教练的精通来自连接，而非重复。

Full-body coaching presence has two benefits. First, your open presence creates the psychological safety needed to engage clients in an honest, exploratory conversation. Second, you are better able to tune in and receive the whole of what clients express in words, expressions, and inferences.

全身的教练临在有双重益处。首先，你开放的临在，创造了让客户投入一场诚实、探索性对话所需的心理安全。其次，你能更好地调频并接收客户以言语、表情和弦外之音所表达的全部。

When you listen with your body, opening your full self to the conversation, the results can lead to revolutionary change because you are more capable of receiving so much more information than if you were just thinking about what they tell you. You ease into the conversation knowing that the true desired outcome—what clients actually want to do or be—comes from taking time to explore how they see themselves and what is possible in the future.

当你用身体倾听，向对话敞开完整的自己时，结果可能带来革命性的改变，因为你能接收的信息，远多于仅仅思考他们告诉你什么。你从容进入对话，深知那真正的期望成果——客户真正想做或想成为的——来自花时间探索他们如何看待自己，以及未来有何可能。

You listen with a curious mind; an open, compassionate heart; and courageous gut that deepens your ability to perceive and dive into the beliefs holding them back from achieving what they want for themselves. Aligning your nervous system provides the presence and energy needed for co-regulation. You can then more comfortably coach from the unknown, speaking from your heart and gut, spontaneously and unrehearsed, naturally activating creativity and initiative.

你用好奇的头脑、开放而慈悲的心、以及勇敢的胆识（gut）来倾听，这深化了你感知并潜入那些阻挡他们实现自身渴望的信念的能力。校准你的神经系统，提供了共同调节所需的临在与能量。于是你能更自在地从未知中教练，从心与胆识出发，自发而不加排练地说话，自然地激活创造力与主动性。

the importance of aligning your nervous system

In the context of coaching, receiving and listening from your heart is a strength, not a weakness. When you open yourself to feel your clients’ experience, you not only better understand them but also create the energetic environment that encourages them to feel safe enough to open up to you. If at first they aren’t comfortable with you or with being coached, your compassionate presence “regulates down” their need to protect and defend themselves. Their discomfort may quickly dissipate, and their willingness to engage with you emerges. 2

在教练的语境中，用心倾听与接收，是一种力量，而非弱点。当你敞开自己去感受客户的体验时，你不仅更理解他们，也创造了鼓励他们在你面前感到安全、足以敞开的能量环境。即便起初他们对你或对被教练感到不自在，你慈悲的临在也会“调节下行”他们保护与防卫自己的需求。他们的不适可能迅速消散，与你投入的意愿随之浮现。²

When you listen only from your head, you focus on simple problem-solving instead of getting beneath the surface to discover what needs to be addressed that is making it difficult for clients to solve a problem on their own. When you think about only what they are saying, you tend to move the conversation quickly toward solutions and goals. You might hear and ask about a rationalization they are using to justify their inaction or an assumption about the future that feels like a risk too scary to take, which is still good coaching but will ensure a change only in how they approach their dilemma as they see it today. They might take a few positive steps forward, but without understanding the deeper motivations for their actions and the blocks caused by how they are defining their problem and themselves in relation to the situation, their old thinking patterns will likely show up again, interfering with their progress in the future.

当你只用头脑倾听，你聚焦于简单的问题解决，而非深入表层之下，去发现到底是什么需要被处理，才让客户难以独立解决一个问题。当你只思考他们说的话，你会倾向于把对话迅速推向解决方案与目标。你或许会听到并追问一个他们用来为不行动辩解的合理化说法，或一个关于未来的、感觉风险大到不敢尝试的假设——这仍是好的教练，但充其量只能改变他们看待当下困境的方式。他们可能向前迈出几步积极的行动，但若不理解驱动其行动的深层次动机，以及他们如何界定自身问题、界定与情境相关的自己，旧有的思维模式很可能再次出现，在未来干扰他们的进展。

Also, when you coach using only your analytical brain, they might not fully trust you. They stay guarded, willing to share only what doesn’t risk being judged.

而且，当你只用分析性大脑来教练，他们或许不会完全信任你。他们保持戒备，只愿意分享那些不会被评判的风险之外的内容。

When you listen with an open heart and gut, you are able to establish the co-regulated state where clients are willing to open up to you. They go more deeply into their thoughts. You are then able to catch the beliefs impacting their desires or fears of not getting their social needs met, such as love and loneliness, recognition and failure, respect and rejection, and hope and disappointment.

当你用开放的心与胆识倾听，你便能建立起共同调节的状态，让客户愿意向你敞开。他们更深入地进入自己的思绪。于是你能捕捉到影响他们渴望、或害怕社交需求（如爱与孤独、认可与失败、尊重与拒绝、希望与失望）无法被满足的那些信念。

You need to have all three centers open and ready to receive to be able to share what you think they expressed and to have them feel safe enough with you to respond thoughtfully and honestly. Even if you are wrong about what you believe you heard and sensed, your reflection and questions will help them think at deeper levels when they feel safe enough to correct your perception.

你需要三个中心全部敞开、准备好接收，才能分享你所认为他们表达的内容，也才能让他们对你感到足够安全，从而深思熟虑、诚实地回应。即便你对自己以为听到和感知到的内容判断有误，当你让他们感到足够安全、足以纠正你的认知时，你的反思与提问将帮助他们进行更深层的思考。

The practice is to open all three centers before any important conversation so you can both create safety with your energy and then receive openly and fully from your client. I’ve included an exercise to help you open all three processing centers of your nervous system at the end of this chapter.

练习在于：在任何重要对话之前，先敞开这三个处理中心，如此你们既能用能量共创安全，之后又能开放、完整地从客户那里接收。我在本章末尾附了一个练习，帮助你敞开神经系统的这三个处理中心。

Once these centers are open, you can catch moments you disrupt your presence, such as when you are thinking too much about what to say, you stopped yourself from sharing what you noticed out of fear, or you jumped in to make the person feel better. Notice, and then quickly reopen your entire nervous system by choosing how you want to feel.

一旦这些中心敞开，你便能捕捉到自己破坏临在的时刻，例如当你过度思考该说什么、因恐惧而止住了想分享的所见、或急于让对方感觉好些时。去觉察，然后通过选择你想要的感受，迅速重新敞开你的整个神经系统。

awakening your full presence

You must consciously choose to embody specific emotions to open the three centers of your nervous system. Follow these steps to establish your full-body presence:

你必须自觉地选择体现特定情绪，以敞开神经系统的三个中心。遵循以下步骤来建立你的全身临在：

  • Choose to feel curious, compassionate, and courageous.

- 选择去感受好奇、慈悲与勇敢。

  • Recall your intention for the coaching partnership.

- 回想你对教练伙伴关系的意图。

  • Believe your client can realize and act on insights.

- 相信你的客户能够实现并落实洞见。

When being fully present, you don’t need to think about your nonverbal expressions. When you activate your nervous system by opening your mind, heart, and gut, your expressions will be inviting and you will engage your clients regardless of how you are tilting your head. Also, when you are thinking about opening your arms, looking concerned, or giving the right eye contact, you are in your head and not present to the client.

当全然临在时，你无需思考自己的非语言表达。当你通过敞开头脑、心与胆识来激活神经系统，你的表情就会充满邀请感，无论你如何偏着头，都能与客户互动。同样，当你在思考如何张开双臂、露出关切的神情，或给出恰当的眼神接触时，你正陷在头脑里，并未临在于客户。

Choose to Feel Curious, Compassionate, and Courageous

Before any important conversation, you must consciously choose to feel the emotions that will engage others regardless of the level of resistance they bring to the conversation. If you are stressed, afraid, or disappointed, you must shift your emotional state by breathing your chosen emotions into your body until you fully feel you have embodied these sensations.

在任何重要对话之前，你必须自觉地选择去感受那些能调动他人的情绪，无论他们带着何种程度的抗拒来到对话。如果你感到压力、害怕或失望，你必须通过把所选的情绪呼吸进身体，来转换情绪状态，直到你完全感到自己已体现了这些感受。

Opening your nervous system before you start coaching requires you choose to feel curious, kindhearted, and courageous. Then, throughout the conversation, recalling one or two of these emotions with what else you want to feel becomes your anchor so you can stay steady with a quiet mind. You might choose to be curious and caring, calm and courageous, or grateful and optimistic. You choose the emotions for each conversation as you consider the person or group you will be engaging. You will find an exercise for returning to presence at the end of chapter 4 .

在开启教练之前敞开神经系统，要求你选择去感受好奇、仁善与勇敢。随后，在整个对话中，回想其中一两个情绪，连同你想感受的其他东西，成为你的锚，让你能以安静的头脑保持稳定。你或许选择好奇与关怀、平静与勇敢，或感恩与乐观。你为每场对话选择情绪，视你将互动的个人或团体而定。你会在第 4 章末尾找到一个回归临在的练习。

Recall Your Intention for the Coaching Partnership

Clients must sense that your intention is to partner with them to discover the best way forward throughout the conversation and that you are there for their best interest, not your own. As soon as you shift your intention to wanting them to go in a particular direction you think is best, their sense of safety might be impaired, if not lost. If they don’t actively resist your coaching, they might follow your lead without thinking the idea through for themselves.

客户必须察觉到，你的意图是与他们结为伙伴，在整个对话中探寻前进的最佳方式，并且你是为了他们的最佳利益而来，而非你自己的。一旦你把意图转向想让他们走向你认为最好的某个方向，他们的安全感即便没有被彻底破坏，也可能受损。即便他们不主动抗拒你的教练，也可能不假思索地跟随你的引导，而没有自己想透这个主意。

You may sense their blocks before they do and then find a way with your reflections and questions to point them out, but you cannot forget that how they define their discoveries and reach their conclusions is theirs to make without your sway. You are their thinking partner, not their mentor, adviser, or counselor. Your responsibility is to broaden their awareness to recognize their blocks and possibilities as a result of your coaching. Their insights define their choices.

你或许比他们更早察觉到他们的阻碍，然后借由你的反思与提问找到指出的方法；但你不能忘记，他们如何定义自己的发现、得出自己的结论，是他们的事，不容你左右。你是他们的思维伙伴，而非导师、顾问或咨询师。你的责任，是拓宽他们的觉察，使他们因你的教练而认出自己的阻碍与各种可能。他们的洞见，定义了他们的选择。

Even if they implore you to advise them, try to remain their thinking partner, knowing they have ideas hidden beneath their confusion and fears. They may feel uncomfortable when you don’t let them take the easy way out, but they also sense your trust in their resourcefulness and that you are there in service of their higher good, not your own. This intention helps them feel safe even when they feel vulnerable.

即便他们恳求你给建议，也要努力保持作为他们的思维伙伴，知道在困惑与恐惧之下，他们藏着自己的想法。当你不让他们走捷径时，他们可能感到不适，但他们也能感受到你对他们足智多谋的信任，以及你是为了他们更高的善而来，而非你自己的。这份意图，让他们即便脆弱也感到安全。

To maintain your intention of partnership, choose to stay open to and curious about what unfolds. Trust that even when the coaching feels uncomfortable as you disrupt their thinking, a new perspective will emerge. The process might feel painfully slow to you, but you need to stay there for them.

为维持你作为伙伴的意图，选择对展开的一切保持敞开与好奇。哪怕在教练打破他们思考时令人不适，也要信任新的视角终会浮现。这个过程对你而言可能慢得痛苦，但你需要为他们留在那里。

Stay curious about their assumptions without judging if their views are right or wrong. Stay grounded and compassionate as they jump around their thoughts, trying to explain the beliefs you question. They might be unsure and agitated as the foundation of their old beliefs starts to crumble while their brain reshapes their views of the reality relating to the presenting situation. Their emotions are signs their consciousness is expanding.

对他们假设保持好奇，而不评判其观点是对是错。当他们围绕自己的念头跳来跳去、试图解释你所质疑的信念时，保持扎根与慈悲。当旧信念的根基开始崩解，而他们的大脑正在重塑与当下情境相关的现实认知时，他们可能不确定且焦躁。他们的情绪，是意识正在扩展的征兆。

Believe your reflective statements, questions, and long moments of silence will lead to the insights that transform your clients’ thinking and decisions, even when you have no idea where the coaching is going.

相信你的反思性陈述、你的提问、以及长长的沉默时刻，终将带来转化客户思考与决定的洞见，即便你完全不知道教练正走向何方。

Remember, your intention isn’t to get somewhere, fix their problem, or make them be someone else. Since you are their thinking partner, your purpose is to use coaching to broaden their perspective to find the answers that were there for them all along.

记住，你的意图不是要抵达某处、修复他们的问题，或让他们变成另一个人。既然你是他们的思维伙伴，你的目的就是运用教练，拓宽他们的视角，去找到本就一直在那里、等着他们的答案。

Believe Your Client Can Realize and Act on Insights

Humanistic psychologist Abraham Maslow said feeling cared about and respected is necessary before humans can realize their full potential of consciousness and creativity. We must be seen and accepted by others before we can step into our best selves.

人本主义心理学家 Abraham Maslow 说过，在人类能够实现意识与创造力的全部潜能之前，必须先感到被关怀、被尊重。我们必须先被他人看见、接纳，才能步入最好的自己。

Typical habits of jumping in to fix a problem or asking what someone can do shortly after they define their problem overrides patience and acceptance. The client feels as if you are no longer interested in listening. When they express an emotion and you try to make them feel better or say they shouldn’t feel what they are genuinely feeling, you are being dismissive instead of helpful. If you jump in to share your similar experience because you think that will be helpful, you quit believing they can find a way forward without your wise guidance. The conversation remains shallow. The connection is not empowering.

Your habits of fixing, sharing your stories, or saving people demonstrate that you don’t believe in the person’s ability to discover a way forward. They are no longer a fully respected partner in the conversation.

Not believing in people’s potential to solve their own problems creates what journalist Johann Hari calls “parodies of connection,” where the humanity in people is invisible when we interact. 3 In coaching, when the coach becomes the one who knows, clients no longer feel safe from judgment. The breach in the partnership may leave clients feeling frustrated, or they just conform with the shift, letting the coach take the lead.

急于跳进去解决问题，或在客户刚界定问题后就问“他能做什么”，这些典型习惯压倒了耐心与接纳。客户会觉得你不再有兴趣倾听。当他们表达一种情绪，而你试图让他们感觉好些，或说他们不该有那份真实感受时，你是在轻慢，而非在帮忙。如果你跳进去分享自己相似的经历，以为那会有帮助，你其实就不再相信他们能在没有你高明指引的情况下找到前路。对话依然浅薄。这种连接并不能赋能。

Your clients must feel you believe they are smart, resourceful, and capable of creative thinking to open up and engage with you. The coach who is comfortable with not knowing what clients should do or feel is most effective in activating the client’s wisdom.

你那些“修复、分享自己的故事、或拯救他人”的习惯，表明你不相信对方有能力找到前路。他们不再是对话中受到充分尊重的伙伴。

When you summarize the key pieces that define their stories using their words, they feel seen and valued. Your coaching then fuels their motivation to resolve their difficult dilemmas. Your trust in your client’s ability to realize and act on the insights that will emerge is vital to achieving a satisfying outcome.

Personal power is sparked from within when people feel seen, cared about, and respected.

Practice knowing that each person you interact with has an amazing imagination and interesting ideas. No matter if you agree with what they are saying, know they are doing their best to survive with what they know. Hopefully, when they feel your genuine respect, you can use a coaching approach to gracefully expand what they know to see the world differently.

不相信人们有潜力解决自己的问题，会制造出记者 Johann Hari 所称的“连接的反讽”——在我们互动时，人的本性变得不可见。³ 在教练中，当教练变成“那个知道的人”，客户便不再感到免受评判的安全。这种对伙伴关系的破坏，可能让客户感到挫败，或干脆顺从这一转向，让教练来主导。

Tip Sheet

Being the Fully Present Coach

The following tips will help you align and coach with your open nervous system to create the emotional bond of co-regulation needed to effectively coach the person, not just the problem:

你的客户必须感到你相信他们聪明、足智多谋，且具备创造性思考的能力，才会敞开并与你投入。最能有效激活客户智慧的教练，正是那些对“客户该做什么、该感受什么”保持不知的从容者。

  • To mentally prepare for a coaching conversation so people quickly feel emotionally safe and willing to engage with you, take a moment to (1) choose to feel curious, compassionate, and courageous; (2) recall your intention for the coaching partnership; and (3) believe your client can realize and act on insights.
  • Practice recalling the emotions you chose to feel before the coaching conversations or whenever your thoughts or reactions disrupt your presence.
  • If your client appears to be uncomfortable, confused, or frustrated, do not jump in to save them. Share the physical shift you notice, and ask whether they would share what is on their mind. Remain open and curious to what unfolds.

当你用他们自己的话，总结出定义其故事的关键片段时，他们感到被看见、被重视。于是你的教练点燃了他们解决艰难困境的动力。你对客户“能实现并落实将会浮现的洞见”的信念，对达成令人满意的成果至关重要。

当人感到被看见、被关怀、被尊重时，个人力量便从内在被点燃。

  • Appreciate how your clients see their world even if it differs from your perspective. This acceptance inspires their willingness to examine and modify their thinking. Trust they will learn and grow.

Practice aligning your nervous system to be fully present regularly until this mental state becomes your natural way of being in the world. Align your head, heart, and gut before you start your day, in preparation for any conversation, before you sit down to answer emails, and as you prepare for a good night’s sleep. You will feel more in control of your emotional state and enjoy more of your daily interactions.

练习去知道：你互动的每一个人，都有惊人的想象力与有趣的想法。无论你同不同意他们所说的，都要知道他们正尽其所能，用已知的一切求生存。但愿当你流露出真诚的尊重时，你能用教练式方法，优雅地拓展他们所知，让他们以不同方式看世界。

exercise: opening your head, heart, and gut

The following visualization exercise will help you open your head, heart, and gut before your next conversation. You can find an audio version to practice the steps without reading them at Covisioning.com under Coach the Person Resources. 4

以下可视化练习，将帮助你在下一场对话前敞开头脑、心与胆识。你可以在 Covisioning.com 的“Coach the Person Resources”下找到音频版本，无需阅读即可练习这些步骤。⁴

  • Sit in a chair, and become a witness to your body. Become aware of how your body feels. Start with noticing the impact of your body with your chair, your feet on the ground, and the placement of your arms. Adjust yourself to be comfortable. Sit up to straighten your spine. Scan your body for tight spots, and release the tension.

- 坐在椅子上，成为自己身体的见证者。觉察身体的感受。从留意身体与椅子、双脚与地面的接触，以及手臂的摆放开始。调整自己到舒适状态。坐直以舒展脊柱。扫描身体寻找紧绷处，并释放张力。

  • Notice your emotional state. Do you feel sad? Calm? Tired? Stressed? Whatever you feel, see if you can relax and gratefully bring yourself into the present moment so you become open to the process you are about to step into.

- 留意你的情绪状态。你感到悲伤？平静？疲惫？压力？无论感受到什么，看看自己能否放松，并带着感恩把自己带入当下，从而向即将踏入的过程敞开。

  • Focus on your breathing. Feel the movement as your breath moves in and out. Feel the temperature of the air as you inhale it into your body. Slowly exhale to calm your heart.

- 专注于你的呼吸。感受气息进出时的流动。吸气入体时，感受空气的温度。缓缓呼气，让心平静。

  • Move your awareness into your brain. Picture an elevator resting in the middle of your brain. The door is open. Allow your thoughts, judgments, and opinions to float into the empty elevator. When they are safely inside, watch the door close, leaving your mind free of thoughts. Say the word “curious” to yourself. Feel your open, curious mind as you calmly breathe.

- 把觉察移入大脑。想象一部电梯停在你大脑中央，门开着。让你的思绪、评判与意见飘入这部空电梯。当它们安全进入后，看门关闭，让你的心智从思绪中解放出来。在心里对自己说“好奇”这个词。当你平静地呼吸时，感受你开放、好奇的头脑。

  • Watch the elevator float slowly down your body. Watch it go through your head and neck and then settle quietly in the center of your chest next to your heart. Recall a person, pet, or place that makes your heart smile with gratitude for having this person or pet in your life or special place you go to that calms your soul. As the elevator door opens, see this person, pet, or place safely nestled inside. Say to yourself the word you are feeling, such as “love” or “gratitude.” Feel your heart expand. Sit with an open, full heart for a few breaths.

- 看电梯缓缓顺着身体向下飘。它穿过头部与颈部，然后安静地停在你胸口中央、心脏旁。回想一个人、一只宠物或一个地方，让感恩的微笑浮上你的心——感恩生命中有这个人、宠物，或那个能安抚灵魂的特别去处。当电梯门打开，看见这个人、宠物或地方安然待在里面。对自己说出你正感受的那个词，例如“爱”或“感恩”。感受你的心扩展。带着开放、充盈的心静坐几次呼吸。

  • Say goodbye to the person, pet, or place as you watch the door close, keeping them safely inside. The elevator floats slowly down your body, your center, and your belly and comes to rest at the spot just below your navel. A warm glow comes from the elevator door. Watch the door slowly open. Nothing is inside, but the space is filled with warm glowing energy. Let the warmth fill your center from your front to your back. As your body warms with the power of the glow, recall a time in your life when you spoke up or did something despite feeling afraid. Remember the moment as you breathe in the word “courage.” Breathe with the sense of what your courage feels like.

- 看电梯门关闭，与他们道别，让他们安然留在里面。电梯缓缓顺身体、顺着你的中心与腹部向下飘，停在肚脐正下方。一道温暖的微光从电梯门透出。看门缓缓打开。里面空无一物，但空间充满了温暖的发光能量。让温暖从你的前胸贯通到后背。当身体被这光芒的力量温暖时，回想你生命中曾开口或行动、尽管害怕的时刻。在吸入“勇气”这个词时，记住那一刻。带着你所感受的勇气去呼吸。

  • Sit with your open head, heart, and gut for a few moments before you enter your conversation. Take your open nervous system with you to access and practice full-body receiving throughout your interaction.

- 在进入对话前，带着你开放的头脑、心与胆识静坐片刻。带着你敞开的神经系统，在整段互动中去调用并练习全身接收。

Consider if you have a difficult time accessing your heart or gut. If you have been a risk taker most of your life, you might find it awkward listening to your heart. On the other hand, if you were drawn to the coaching profession because you care deeply about helping people, you might find it difficult, even scary, to share what comes up from your gut.

想想看，如果你难以触及自己的心或胆识，会怎样。如果你大半生都是个冒险者，你可能会觉得倾听自己的心笨拙。另一方面，如果你是因深切关怀助人而投身教练职业，你可能会觉得难以、甚至害怕，去分享从胆识中升起的东西。

In your daily interactions, practice receiving from your most vulnerable center to better balance your open head, heart, and gut. The practice will help you align your nervous system to create a safe bond with your clients and receive their full expression.

在日常互动中，练习从你最脆弱的中心去接收，以更好地平衡你开放的头脑、心与胆识。这项练习将帮助你校准神经系统，与客户建立安全的纽带，并接收他们完整的表达。

Chapter 7

CHAPTER 4 RECEIVE [DON’T JUST LISTEN]

Attention consists of suspending our thought, leaving it detached, empty, and ready to be penetrated ... waiting, not seeking anything, but ready to receive.

注意力在于悬置我们的思想，让它抽离、空、并准备好被穿透……等待，不寻求任何东西，却准备好接收。

—S IMONE W EIL

——西蒙娜·薇依（SIMONE WEIL）

I FIRST HEARD the word receive used as a form of listening from Julian Treasure in his TED Talk “5 Ways to Listen Better.” 1 His formula for listening is RASA, which stands for receive, appreciate, summarize, and ask.

我第一次听到“接收（receive）”作为倾听的一种形式，是从 Julian Treasure 的 TED 演讲“5 Ways to Listen Better”中。¹ 他的倾听公式是 RASA，代表接收（receive）、欣赏（appreciate）、总结（summarize）与提问（ask）。

When I deliver coaching skills workshops for leaders, I teach them a version of Treasure’s RASA formula as a way to use a coaching approach in their leadership conversations. We first discuss and do exercises to experience each of the following steps:

当我为领导者开设教练技能工作坊时，我会教他们一个 Treasure 的 RASA 公式版本，作为在领导对话中运用教练式方法的一种方式。我们先讨论并做练习，来体验以下每一步：

  • Receive what others express while releasing any judgment of their perception.

- 接收他人所表达的内容，同时放下对他们认知的任何评判。

  • Accept they are speaking from their experience.

- 接纳他们是从自身经验出发在说话。

  • Reflect what you hear and notice as critical expressions.

- 反思你听到并注意到、作为关键表达的内容。

  • Explore their meaning of key words and phrases they use to describe their challenge or desire, beliefs you hear when they explain what is hindering their progress, and what emotions they express when you ask them to share what they want to have happen that is not yet occurring.

- 探索他们用来描述挑战或渴望的关键词与短语对你的意义、你在他们解释“是什么阻碍进展”时听到的信念，以及当你请他们分享“尚未发生、却想让其发生的事”时，他们所表达的情绪。

Then, the leaders work in pairs, coaching each other for five minutes with the instruction for the leader to use only the four steps. They must refrain from correcting their partner’s perception, jumping in to give advice, and telling their own story of a similar experience.

接着，领导者两两配对，用“领导者只能使用这四步”的指示，互相教练五分钟。他们必须克制自己，不去纠正搭档的认知、不急于给建议、不讲述自己相似经历的故事。

The leaders always come back with a full understanding and appreciation of coaching as an effective leadership tool. They admit they had to hold themselves back from giving their opinions and suggestions, but focusing on receiving without judgment and accepting a person’s perspective as true for them in the moment helped them stay present in the conversation. Then the requirement of reflecting and exploring what the person expressed had the immediate effect of broadening perspectives and possibilities in only five minutes! The person speaking also said how they felt seen and understood, which encouraged them to think about their thinking.

领导者们总是带着对教练作为一种有效领导工具的完整理解与欣赏回来。他们承认，必须克制自己不给意见与建议，但专注于不带评判地接收、并接纳一个人当下的视角为其真实，这帮助他们留在了对话的临在中。而随后“反思并探索对方所表达内容”的要求，在仅仅五分钟内就立刻产生了拓宽视角与可能性的效果！说话的人也说自己感到被看见、被理解，这鼓励他们思考自己的思考。

When you accept people for who they are and what they share from their perspective, they are likely to trust you enough to open up and explore their thinking with you.

当你接纳人们本来的样子、以及他们从自身视角分享的内容时，他们很可能足够信任你，从而敞开并与你探索其思考。

I use this exercise with both new and experienced coaches to demonstrate the best way to move into a coaching conversation in a more casual and engaging way instead of focusing on applying the coaching competencies or too quickly assuming they know what the client wants without asking. Even experienced coaches thank me for the important reminder.

无论对新教练还是资深教练，我都用这个练习来示范：如何以更随意、更具参与感的方式进入教练对话，而不是聚焦于套用教练能力，或过早地假设自己知道客户想要什么而不去询问。连资深教练都为此重要的提醒而感谢我。

Receiving clients with patience and full-body presence goes beyond just paying attention. From the start, clients will feel your intention is to invite them on a journey of discovery. They will willingly join you on the path.

带着耐心与全身临在去接收客户，远不止于仅仅“集中注意力”。从一开始，客户就会感到你的意图是邀请他们踏上一段发现的旅程。他们会乐意与你同行。

the intentions of listening

In your daily interactions, you most likely listen to gather information that will fulfill your needs, to hear enough to tell others how to solve their problem, or to find fault in their thinking. 2 You spend these moments in your head thinking about how to use what you hear or how to get out of the conversation if you have nothing to gain. Even if your desire is to collaborate with the people you are with, they may feel little emotional connection with you.

在日常互动中，你多半是为了满足自己的需求而倾听——听足够的信息去告诉别人如何解决他们的问题，或去挑他们思考中的毛病。² 你把这些时刻花在头脑里，盘算如何利用所听到的，或者，若你无利可图，如何脱身。即便你的欲望是与同处的人协作，他们也可能几乎感受不到与你的情感连接。

Common, habitual listening has these intentions:

习惯性倾听通常有这些意图：

  • ✦ To collect data. You listen to know what to say or do next. You might listen to formulate your argument or what you want to share from your own experiences. You stop listening once you think you have enough information to fulfill your purpose.

- ✦ 收集数据。你倾听，是为了知道接下来该说什么或做什么。你可能倾听，是为了构建自己的论点，或想从自身经验中分享什么。一旦觉得信息足够、能达成目的，你就停止倾听。

  • ✦ To give appropriate advice. You listen to a person’s problem long enough to know what advice to give. You may interrupt their story by saying, “I get it,” before you jump in to tell them what to do.

- ✦ 给出恰当的建议。你听一个人的问题，听到足以知道该给什么建议。你可能在跳进去告诉对方怎么做之前，就说“我懂了”来打断他们的故事。

  • ✦ To obey protocol. You listen because it is the right thing to do, generally for the minimum amount of time until they think you care about what they are talking about. Then, you find an excuse to move on.

- ✦ 服从既定规程。你倾听，是因为这是该做的事，通常只维持最短时间，直到他们以为你在乎他们在说什么。然后你找个借口抽身。

Habitual listening is a cognitive process. Your mind is active as you interpret what other people are saying. You might notice emotional shifts in their expression, but your tendency will be to analyze their reactions even though you often inaccurately decipher facial expressions. 3 Moments of curiosity are fleeting.

习惯性倾听是一个认知过程。你的心智在积极解读他人所说。你可能注意到他们表情中的情绪转变，但你倾向于分析他们的反应，尽管你常不准确解读面部表情。³ 好奇的片刻转瞬即逝。

If you take your habit of cognitive processing into your coaching, you will collect enough data to understand your client’s point of view so you can diagnose the problem and then leap to discover solutions, often saying, “Have you tried . . .” or “What options have you been considering?” The coaching may facilitate problem-solving, often confirming what the clients know but are afraid to act on. There is little change in how clients see situations. They are likely to have trouble solving similar problems in the future, which can erode any confidence they built solving the previous problem.

如果你把这种认知处理的习惯带入教练，你会收集足够数据来理解客户的观点，从而诊断问题，然后急于寻找解决方案，常常说“你试过……吗？”或“你一直在考虑哪些选项？”这种教练或许能促进问题解决，往往只是确认客户已知、却不敢行动的事。客户如何看待情境，几乎没有什么改变。他们未来很可能难以解决类似问题，这会侵蚀他们在解决上一个问题时建立起来的任何信心。

the intentions of receiving

The act of receiving is when you welcome and value what your clients share. You hear their words, notice their shifts in expression and posture, and sense when they are holding back from looking at something when they hesitate or jump to another topic. You come into the conversation with an open heart and gut, as well as an open mind. 4 The act of receiving creates the trust and safety needed for clients to willingly explore their thinking with you.

接收，是你欢迎并重视客户所分享内容的行为。你听到他们的言语，注意到他们表情与姿态的转变，并在他们犹豫或跳到另一个话题、从而克制自己不去看某件事时，感知到这一点。你带着开放的心与胆识，以及开放的头脑，进入对话。⁴ 接收的行为，创造了客户愿意与你一同探索其思考所需的信任与安全。

You receive what people offer in any conversation for these intentions:

你出于以下意图，在任何对话中接收人们所提供的东西：

  • ✦ To establish a positive connection with others. You engage to strengthen the relationship. You stay present throughout the conversation, resisting the urge to quickly know what they mean and need before understanding what they see and really want. You are comfortable with not knowing.

- ✦ 与他人建立积极的连接。你投入以强化关系。你在整个对话中保持临在，克制住“在理解他们看到并真正想要什么之前，就急于知道其意义与需求”的冲动。你对未知感到自在。

  • ✦ To let people know you value them. You listen so people feel you care enough to take the time to understand what they see and feel. You have empathy for their experience even when you don’t agree with their views.

- ✦ 让人们知道你重视他们。你倾听，让人感到你足够在乎，愿意花时间去理解他们所看与所感。即便你不同意他们的观点，你也对他们的经历怀有共情。

  • ✦ To explore, learn, and grow together. You listen with curiosity to learn from the amazing human in front of you who has lived a life different from yours. You enjoy when the conversation takes an unexpected turn. Sometimes, the dialogue can delight you in the same way as watching a beautiful sunrise or catching sight of a shooting star before it disappears into the black night sky. 5 Letting go of thinking can be magical, especially when you are coaching.

- ✦ 一同探索、学习与成长。你带着好奇倾听，向眼前这个与你过着不同人生的、了不起的人学习。当对话出现意外的转折时，你享受其中。有时，这段对话能像观赏美丽的日出，或捕捉到一颗闪过黑夜的流星般令你欣喜。⁵ 放下思考可以充满魔力，尤其在教练时。

Receiving is an active, not passive, act. To fully receive, you need to be aware of your sensory reactions in your body.

接收是一个主动的、而非被动的行为。要完整地接收，你需要觉察身体中的感官反应。

sensory awareness

Sensory awareness in a conversation includes the recognition of any physical sensations when talking to someone. You may be reacting to their words. You could also be reacting to shifts in their energy. When coaching, you might immediately and unconsciously shift your own emotions to match the shift clients make when talking.

对话中的感官觉察，包含对与人交谈时任何身体感觉的识别。你可能在回应他们的言辞。你也可能在回应他们能量的转变。教练时，你可能会立即且下意识地转换自己的情绪，以匹配客户说话时做出的转变。

You don’t just listen to your clients. You feel their desires and hesitation, their hopes and doubts, what they value most and how their self-doubt blocks achieving their desires. Sensory awareness requires you have open access to all three of your processing centers in the nervous system—your head, heart, and gut.

你不只是倾听客户。你感受他们的渴望与迟疑、希望与怀疑、最珍视之物，以及自我怀疑如何阻挡了渴望的实现。感官觉察要求你能开放地进入神经系统中全部三个处理中心——头脑、心与胆识。

You were likely taught to ignore signals from your heart and gut as a child. Were you ever told, “Don’t take things so personally” or “You need to toughen up”? You probably were rewarded more for how smart you were than your gift of empathy. To avoid feeling vulnerable and uncertain, you may have created a habit of evaluating what is being said instead of trusting what you receive when you are fully present.

你小时候很可能被教导去忽略来自心与胆识的信号。是否有人曾对你说“别那么把这事往自己身上揽”或“你得坚强点”？你大概因为聪明而非因为天赋的同理心获得更多奖赏。为避免感到脆弱与不确定，你可能养成了评估“正在说什么”的习惯，而非信任当你全然临在时所接收到的东西。

When you don’t allow people to get under your skin, you aren’t experiencing them fully. You are disconnected. You put up a wall between yourself and others, which blocks your ability to connect and co-regulate emotionally.

当你不允许别人触动你，你就未能完整地体验他们。你是断开连接的。你在自己与他人之间筑起一道墙，阻断了连接并共同调节情绪的能力。

When you allow yourself to be sensitive—to experience others enough to grasp even their small emotional shifts—your reflection of what you heard and noticed powerfully impacts their thinking.

当你允许自己敏感——去充分体验他人，乃至捕捉他们细微的情绪转变——你对所听到与注意到的反思，会强有力地影响他们的思考。

I am often asked if it is okay to venture into the emotional realm by leaders who see emotions as a sign of weakness but also by coaches who fear the conversation will slip into therapy when bringing up emotions. Ignoring the shifts in body language and vocal expressions means you are denying a part of what makes them human.

那些视情绪为软弱象征的领导者，以及那些害怕一提起情绪、对话就会滑向治疗的教练，常问我是否可以涉足情感领域。忽视肢体语言与声音表达中的转变，意味着你否认了使他们为人之一部分的东西。

When you are fully present and your clients have an intense emotional reaction, you pick up the emotional energy vibrating between you. 6 If you stay compassionately silent, you will experience the arc of their emotions. First, an emotional reaction is triggered in their body, then they may cry, feel ashamed, shut down, or have an angry outburst. If you don’t interrupt their emotional experience, the intensity will diminish. When you feel the force of the emotion subside, you can ask, “Would you be willing to share with me what just came up for you?”

当你全然临在，而客户有强烈的情绪反应时，你会接住在你们之间振动的情绪能量。⁶ 如果你保持慈悲的沉默，你会体验到他们情绪的弧线。首先，身体中被触发出一种情绪反应，接着他们可能哭泣、感到羞耻、关闭自己，或爆发愤怒。若你不打断他们的情绪体验，强度便会减弱。当你感到情绪的力量消退，你可以问：“你愿意和我分享刚才浮现的、关于你的什么吗？”

You take in the energy of their emotions, but you don’t let it sit in your body. Empathy is where you receive what others are feeling. You are able to sense their distress and then respond with compassion. You are also able to step back and consider their context and perspective. You must let the emotional experience you picked up move through you so you can stay calmly present as both of you process what is emerging in their thoughts.

你吸纳他们情绪的能量，但不让它滞留在你体内。共情，是你接收他人所感的地方。你能感知他们的苦痛，然后以慈悲回应。你也能退后一步，考量他们的语境与视角。你必须让所接住的情绪体验穿过你，这样你才能在我们双方共同处理其思绪中正在浮现之物时，保持平静的临在。

You aren’t coaching the whole person if you ignore the emotions expressed with their words.

如果你忽视与言语一同表达的情绪，你就不是在教练完整的人。

Spiritual teacher A. H. Almaas said, “Therefore, the more accustomed we are to our inner stillness and peacefulness, the more perceptive we become to the subtle dimensions. This can take our inquiry to deeper levels, to a newer kind of knowledge, to a different kind of experience.” 7 You are able to receive and accept the full expression of your clients with your steady, courageous presence. Your clients will then allow you to coach them through their blocks to move respectfully forward together.

灵性导师 A. H. Almaas 说过：“因此，我们越习惯于内在的静默与平和，对精微维度的感知力就越强。这能把我们的探询带到更深的层次，带向一种更新的知识、一种不同的体验。”⁷ 你能够用自己稳定、勇敢的临在，接收并接纳客户的完整表达。于是客户会允许你教练他们穿越阻碍，一同恭敬地向前。

regaining presence when emotions block the flow

In 1998, I was asked to create a program for professionals to find their zone of excellence based on interviewing top athletes to discover what they do to execute perfectly in clutch moments when the game depends on their performance. I spoke to well-known athletes in six sports about what they did to control their mind and emotions to stay calmly present.

I found the best competitors overrode their anxiety by choosing not to think about anything, not even winning. They said positive self-talk did not help in those moments. Instead, the champions cleared their minds, allowing their bodies to freely move with the joy of doing what they loved. 8

1998 年，我受委托为专业人士开发一个项目，去寻找他们的卓越之境（zone of excellence），其基础是采访顶级运动员，探究他们在比赛胜负取决于其表现的关键时刻，是如何做到完美执行的。我就六项运动中的知名运动员做了访谈，了解他们如何控制心智与情绪，以保持平静的临在。

The mental state they mastered allowed them to be in the zone where they were completely present, losing sense of time and the space outside of their focus, where nothing else mattered but moving their body fluidly in the moment. The psychologist Mihaly Csikszentmihalyi identified this state as flow. He called it the optimal state of consciousness, where people perform at their best without thinking. Csikszentmihalyi said that in this state, we know what is going on but we “react to it in a reflexive, instinctive way.” 9 We trust our senses. Thinking isn’t necessary.

我发现最优秀的竞争者，通过选择不去想任何事、连胜利也不想，来压制自己的焦虑。他们说，积极自我对话在那些时刻并无帮助。相反，冠军们清空心智，让身体随着做所爱之事的喜悦自由流动。⁸
