She held her hand up high, saying, “This is how much I want to go!” Then, she added, “I just can’t right now, but I can think about what I might want to do in the future without feeling guilty.” With her permission, we agreed to a new outcome for our conversation based on being better able to cope with staying in her current situation.

她把手举得高高的，说：「这就是我想走的程度！」接着又补充：「只是我现在还走不了，但我可以想想将来可能想做的事，而不必感到内疚。」经她同意，我们基于「更好地应对留在现状中」重新达成了对话的新结果。

The session didn’t end with a decision about when she might seek a different job. In addition to finding ways to release her stress and resentment, she committed to giving herself alone time to think that weekend.

这次会话没有以决定她何时可能另谋高就告终。除了找到释放压力与怨恨的办法，她还承诺那个周末给自己独处思考的时间。

Two hours later, she sent me an email declaring, “Thank you for my liberation day. I’ve done enough in my career that it shouldn’t be hard to find a more challenging position somewhere else. I am now free to leave whenever I choose to.”

两小时后，她发邮件给我说：「谢谢我的解放日。我在事业上已经做得够多，找一份更有挑战的职位不该很难。我现在随时可以离开，只要我愿意。」

The session was successful because it gave the client clarity about who was making choices for her. Knowing she would still be able to care for her family, she could better plan her next steps, no matter who thought she was wrong.

这次会话是成功的，因为它让客户看清了：究竟是谁在替她做选择。知道仍能够照顾好家庭，她就能更好地规划下一步，无论谁觉得她错了。

Notice whenever your clients use the word but when you point out an apparent conflict. You can explore if the reason given for not doing something is important enough to keep them in place. If their choice is to keep the status quo, honor it. Don’t push your clients to do something because you think it is in their best interest. Go with their decision. Ask what they need to be comfortable with the choice they have made for the time being. Trust that you opened them to new possibilities they will consider after the session ends.

每当客户在你看穿明显冲突时说出「但是」这个词，都要留意。你可以探究：那个不做某事给出的理由，是否重要到足以让他们维持现状。如果他们的选择是维持现状，就尊重它。不要因为你认为某事最符合他们利益，就逼客户去做。顺着他们的决定。问问他们需要什么，才能暂时安于自己做出的选择。相信你已为他们开启了会话结束后才会去考虑的新可能。

Paraphrasing

In the previous case study, the client worried she was wrong for wanting to leave her job; everyone told her she should be grateful for what she had. I said, “It sounds like your desires are in conflict with what you think you should do, that you are wrong, or even bad, for wanting to seek something else.” I added the word bad as I sensed guilt when she talked about her obligation to her family and frustration with not feeling grateful. She wasn’t just considering a career decision; she felt she would be harshly judged based on what she chose to do. I often find this same phenomenon with parents and leaders; they worry about being judged as a bad mom, dad, or leader if they choose to do what they think is best.

在前面的案例研究中，那位客户担心自己想要离职是错的；所有人都告诉她，她应该对自己拥有的一切心怀感激。我说：「听起来你的愿望和你认为自己应该做的事相互冲突——你觉得自己错了，甚至因为想要别的可能而觉得自己很糟。」我加上「糟」这个词，是因为当她谈到对家庭的责任、以及因无法感到感激而产生的挫败时，我感受到了她的愧疚。她不只是在考虑一个职业决定；她觉得，基于自己的选择，她会受到严厉的评判。我在父母和领导者身上经常发现同样的现象；他们担心，如果选择做自己认为最好的事，就会被认为是个糟糕的妈妈、爸爸或领导者。

Paraphrasing helps clients assess the meaning of their words and emotions. You restate what you hear in a slightly different form to surface and explore their beliefs.

释义能帮助客户评估自己言语和情绪的含义。你用略微不同的形式重述所听到的内容，以浮现并探究他们的信念。

Present your paraphrase as an offer; clients can accept your words or not. If they don’t agree, they will likely offer an alternative clarifier, such as “I don’t think they will say I’m bad. I think they will say I’m selfish.”

把你的释义当作一种「提议」来呈现；客户可以接受，也可以不接受。如果他们不认同，很可能会给出一个替代性的澄清，例如「我觉得他们不会说我糟。我想他们会说我自私。」

Because you are interpreting what was said, be careful to link your word to their definitions and expressions. It is difficult not to speculate based on your own experiences. Try to remain curious instead of assuming you know what they mean without asking.

由于你是在解读对方所说的话，务必小心地把你的用词关联到他们的定义和表达上。不去基于自身经验臆测是很难的。尽量保持好奇，而不是在没问清之前就假定自己知道他们的意思。

A good practice to help you develop your reflective capabilities is to ask your clients if you can record the session for your use only. Then, listen to your summations and paraphrases. Ask yourself what led you to your choice of words. Did your opinion get in the way of your objectivity? How did the client respond? It can be painful to listen to yourself coach, but as they say, no pain, no gain.

一个有助于培养你反思能力的好做法，是询问客户是否可以录制本次会话，仅供你自己使用。然后，去听你的概述和释义。问问自己，是什么引导你选择了那些词。你的看法是否妨碍了你的客观性？客户是如何回应的？听自己教练的过程可能很痛苦，但俗话说，不劳无获（no pain, no gain）。

Another form of paraphrasing is to use metaphors. It can help your client to offer a visual picture of their complex thoughts and emotions, such as “It feels like a tsunami just crashed into your life” or “These constant requests keep you pushing the boulder uphill” or “Sounds like they are asking you to cut the grass with scissors.” Metaphors can lead to instant insights. 1

释义的另一种形式是使用隐喻。这能帮助你的客户为其复杂的想法和情绪提供一幅视觉画面，例如「感觉像是一场海啸刚刚冲进了你的生活」，或「这些没完没了的请求让你一直推着石头上山」，或「听起来他们是在让你用剪刀割草」。隐喻能带来即时的洞见。1

You also want to listen and reflect when clients use common metaphors, such as “They don’t understand because they are living in a bubble” or “I’m the scapegoat for the team” or “I didn’t expect a heart of stone.” Ask what the metaphor means to them to identify specific behaviors that formed their belief.

你也要在客户使用常见隐喻时去倾听并映照，例如「他们不理解，因为他们活在泡泡里」，或「我是团队的替罪羊」，或「我没料到会是一颗石头心」。问问这个隐喻对他们意味着什么，以识别出形成其信念的具体行为。

Whether you offer your metaphor or you acknowledge one the client uses, metaphors are great clarifiers that lead to exploring what the client would like to do and feel instead of living as their metaphor suggests.

无论你提供自己的隐喻，还是认可客户所用的隐喻，隐喻都是极好的澄清工具，能引导客户去探索：比起活成隐喻所暗示的样子，他们更想做些什么、感受些什么。

Encapsulating

Sometimes, you can capture and package the major elements of a client’s story into a phrase or even one word to name the client’s experience. This practice includes labeling, bottom lining, and drawing distinctions.

有时，你可以把客户故事的主要元素提炼并打包成一句话，甚至一个词，来概括客户的体验。这种做法包括贴标签（labeling）、归纳要点（bottom lining）以及区分辨析（drawing distinctions）。

Labeling to Encapsulate the Experience

When labeling the client’s experience, you are offering a title for their story. Listen for the similar circumstances when they share different events that have led to their current dilemma. The label might be “Your support system is gone” or “The expectations are vague.” You might ask about a key concept, such as “It sounds like the issue is around trust” or “Your fear of getting a negative evaluation is paralyzing you.”

给客户的体验贴标签（labeling），就是为他们的故事提供一个标题。当他们分享导致当前困境的不同事件时，留意其中相似的情形。标签可以是「你的支持系统没了」，或「期望很模糊」。你或许可以就某个关键概念发问，例如「听起来问题出在信任上」，或「你对得到负面评价的恐惧正在麻痹你」。

Case Study

Case Study

I was coaching a leader who wanted to explore why he was being indecisive about accepting a job offer from a company in a country where he would like to live. His history included three jobs in different countries. He was hired by the three companies to come in and make big changes in the organizational structure to capture market share in new ways. As soon as his work was seeing successful returns, he would move on to the next company. His current job was in the same industry, but the CEO gave him a senior leadership position. For the first time in his career, he was leading a team. The CEO mentored him as he stepped into the leadership role and paid for a coach to help him grow as a leader. He spoke about the CEO with both admiration and gratitude.

我曾教练一位领导者，他想探究自己为何在是否接受一份工作邀约上犹豫不决——那家公司所在的国家正是他想生活的地方。他的履历包括在三个不同国家做过三份工作。那三家公司雇他进去，都是为了以全新方式大刀阔斧地调整组织结构以抢占市场份额。一旦他的工作见到成功回报，他就会转向下一家公司。他目前的工作在同一行业，但 CEO 给了他一个高级领导职位。这是他职业生涯中第一次带团队。他步入领导角色时，CEO 指导了他，并出资请了一位教练帮助他作为领导者成长。他谈起这位 CEO 时，既钦佩又感激。

Now, he had this amazing job offer for his talents in innovation and implementation. The job would be in a country where the schools for his kids were exceptional. His wife would have better work opportunities as well.

如今，他拿到了一份绝佳的邀约，正合他在创新与执行上的才华。那份工作所在的国家，孩子们能上极好的学校。他的妻子也会有更好的工作机会。

I summarized his job history and what he was doing differently than before. I shared how he showed both admiration and gratitude for his CEO and his excitement when he was telling me about the work he was doing with his team. I then said, “It sounds like your relationship with your CEO is the main factor for not accepting the new offer. You seem to have developed some loyalty—”

我概述了他的职业履历，以及他与前不同的做法。我分享了他如何对 CEO 既钦佩又感激，以及他在向我讲述和团队一起的工作时有多兴奋。然后我说：「听起来你和 CEO 的关系，是不接受这份新邀约的主要因素。你似乎产生了某种忠诚——」

He said, “Yes! That’s it! I have never felt loyal before.”

他说：「对！就是这样！我以前从没觉得过忠诚。」

The conversation shifted to his exploring whether loyalty was a block or benefit. He decided to stay longer in his position as a leader because he liked it. He would work with his family to meet their needs as well.

对话转向了他去探索：忠诚究竟是阻碍还是益处。他决定在领导岗位上多待一阵，因为他喜欢。他也会与家人协作，满足他们的需要。

The one word I offered— loyalty —summed up his dilemma. The label gave him the clarity he needed to evaluate his conflict and make the best decision for himself.

我给出的那个词——忠诚——概括了他的困境。这个标签给了他所需的清晰，让他得以评估自己的冲突，并为自己做出最好的决定。

Bottom Lining to Encapsulate the Experience

Bottom lining helps clients drill down and encapsulate what is the critical challenge they are facing or what they must decide on in the moment to end their procrastination. This approach is best used after the client has described and agreed on what they desire to achieve. Then, they fall into a spiral of reasons why they can’t get what they want. When you summarize their reasons, they add more or go deeper into explaining each one. Or they layer even more dramas and difficulties that they can’t control.

底线归纳（bottom lining）帮助客户深入核心，凝练出他们所面临的关键挑战，或此刻必须做出的决定，以终结拖延。这种方法最好在客户已描述并认同自己渴望达成的目标之后使用。随后，他们会陷入「为什么得不到想要之物」的理由漩涡。当你概述他们的理由，他们会补充更多，或更深地解释每一条。又或者，他们会层层叠加更多自己无法掌控的戏剧与困难。

As they list their reasons, listen for the word but. The word but signals that their brain is conjuring up excuses for not acting. Bring the conversation back to the statement they made before the but to see if, bottom line, what they said they truly wanted to achieve that is important enough to act on despite the risks they named after they said but. You might share the pattern of saying but every time they share what they want or can do, and then say, “Bottom line, do you really want to find what is in your control to achieve what you said you wanted?” Then, accept their decision even if they say they need to resolve or create something else.

当他们列举理由时，留意「但是」这个词。这个词表明，他们的大脑正在编造不行动的理由。把对话拉回到「但是」之前他们说的话，看看——说到底——他们所说真正想达成的东西，是否重要到足以让他们不顾在「但是」之后点出的那些风险去行动。你或许可以指出这个模式：每次他们说出想要或能做到什么，都会接一个「但是」，然后说：「说到底，你真的想去找自己能掌控的办法，去实现你说的想要之物吗？」然后，接受他们的决定，即便他们说需要先解决或创造别的东西。

Case Study

Case Study

A client hired me to coach him through his transition from business owner to philanthropist. He was close to selling the small business he had owned for years and had grown to thirty employees.

一位客户雇我教练他完成从企业主到慈善家的转型。他即将卖掉经营多年、已发展到三十名员工的小企业。

Weeks before closing the sale, he found reasons to delay the process. He came to each coaching session with a new company drama that had to be resolved before the sale could be made.

在成交前几周，他找理由拖延流程。每次教练会话，他都带着一个必须在新交易前解决的新「公司戏码」前来。

However, none of the problems he named were unre-solvable or had a big impact on the business. When he first announced the sale and what it would mean for the employees, there didn’t seem to be discontent. He assured them they would all be taken care of. When I asked him if the new problems were symptoms of their fear of changing owners. He said no, they weren’t resisting. He didn’t know why problems kept cropping up.

然而，他点出的问题没有一个是无法解决的，也对业务影响不大。当他最初宣布出售及其对员工意味着什么时，似乎并没有不满。他向他们保证都会得到妥善安置。当我问他，这些新问题是否源于他们对更换老板的恐惧。他说不是，他们并没有抗拒。他不知道为什么问题不断冒出来。

After four sessions starting with a new drama that had him delay the sale again, I asked, “With all these problems, do you still want to go forward with the sale?”

在连续四次都以一个新戏码开头、让他再次推迟出售的会话之后，我问：「有这么多问题，你还想继续推进出售吗？」

He said, “But I have to, don’t I? I’ve told so many people I’m doing this. There are expectations.”

他说：「但我必须卖，不是吗？我都跟那么多人说了我在做这件事。有期望在那儿。」

I said, “So you are moving forward with the sale because of other people’s expectations?”

我说：「所以你是因为别人的期望才继续推进出售的？」

There was a long pause before he said, “So are you saying I don’t have to sell if I don’t want to?”

长久的停顿后，他说：「所以你的意思是，如果我不想卖，我就不必卖？」

I said, “You always have a choice based on what you want. Bottom line, the question is, Do you want to proceed with the sale or not?”

我说：「你永远可以基于你自己想要的来做选择。说到底，问题是：你想继续出售，还是不想？」

He said, “Let me think about it. I will let you know my decision in our next session.” He called me two days later and said, “I’m not selling. This is my family. I look forward to being with them every day. Giving them a great place to work is meaningful for me. I don’t need to go find meaning elsewhere.”

他说：「让我想想。下次会议我告诉你我的决定。」两天后他打电话给我说：「我不卖了。这是我的家。我期待每天和他们在一起。给他们一个好的工作场所对我有意义。我不需要去别处找意义。」

His voice was resolute. It didn’t appear that he had found an excuse to avoid change; he clearly had made up his mind. I acknowledged his courage to choose for himself.

他的声音很坚决。看起来他并非找了个借口逃避改变；他显然已下定决心。我认可了他为自己做选择的勇气。

In our next session, we looked at how he could delegate some responsibilities to create more time for himself to do the activities he had thought he could do only if he sold his company.

在下一次会话中，我们探讨了他如何授权一些职责，为自己腾出时间，去做那些他原以为只有卖掉公司才能做的事。

I often use a bottom line statement or question when a pattern of behavior has stalled progress and the buts keep showing up to explain the delays. A good question to ask is “If you don’t choose to do something to create what you said you wanted, will you have any regrets a year from now?” The question helps clarify what they really want to do while reducing the impact of their fears.

当某种行为模式停滞了进展、而「但是」不断出现来解释拖延时，我常会用一个底线归纳式的陈述或问题。一个好问题是：「如果你不选择做点什么去创造你说的想要之物，一年后你会后悔吗？」这个问题有助于澄清他们真正想做的事，同时减弱恐惧的影响。

Drawing Distinctions

One of my favorite practices is drawing distinctions to help clarify what a person says they want or need. When you try to determine what a specific phrase means or clarify what they want, your question offers two possibilities to choose from based on what they said.

我最喜欢的练习之一，是用区分辨析来帮助澄清一个人所说想要或需要的东西。当你试图确定某个具体短语的含义，或澄清他们想要什么时，你的问题会基于他们说的话，提供两个选项供其选择。

Case Study

Case Study

I had a client who wanted to be more resilient but feared her brain was getting stale with age. She said she wasn’t boldly taking actions as quickly as she did when she started her career. She also feared she wasn’t being as creative with solutions as she used to be.

我曾有一位客户，想要变得更有韧性，却又害怕自己大脑随年龄变得迟钝。她说，自己不再像职业生涯初期那样迅速地果断行动。她也担心自己在想解决方案时不如从前有创意。

I said, “You mentioned two things that are different now, being bold and being creative. Which one do you think relates more to your decrease in resilience, that you don’t act quickly or you don’t see as many options to consider?”

我说：「你提到了两样如今不同的东西——果敢与有创意。你认为哪一样更关系到你韧性的下降：是你行动不够快，还是你看到的可考虑选项变少了？」

She said she didn’t feel that clever these days. If she could find the time and energy to consider more options, she might feel brave enough to act. Then, she said, “I’m just tired of it all.”

她说最近觉得自己没那么机灵了。如果能找到时间和精力去考虑更多选项，她或许会鼓起勇气去行动。接着她说：「我只是受够了这一切。」

I said, “Okay, so you don’t have the time and energy to be as creative as you once were. When you said you were tired of it all, are you tired of the work you are doing, or is it that you have so much to do, you are physically drained?” She chose the latter, saying she was tired by midday and exhausted each night.

我说：「好，所以你是没有时间和精力像从前那样有创意了。当你说受够了这一切时，你是厌倦了自己正在做的工作，还是因为要做的事太多而身体被掏空了？」她选了后者，说自己中午就累了，每晚都筋疲力尽。

I asked her if she had seen her doctor about her draining energy. She said she would, but my question made her realize that her lack of self-care was probably impacting her mental resiliency more than her age.

我问她是否就精力被耗尽的事看过医生。她说会去，而我的问题让她意识到，缺乏自我照顾对她心理韧性的影响，可能比年龄更大。

She then asked if we could focus on how she could better set boundaries to improve her self-care. I asked her what she would like her days to look like if she set boundaries. When she described her day, I asked, “Who are you in that picture?” She smiled and said, “A vibrant and resilient woman.”

她随后问，我们能否聚焦于她如何更好地设定边界来改善自我照顾。我问她，如果设定了边界，她希望自己的日子变成什么样。当她描述那一天时，我问：「在那幅画面里，你是谁？」她笑着说：「一个充满活力、有韧性的女人。」

In addition to exploring the meaning of your clients’ words, distinctions can help reveal a conflict of values or desires. Name two things you hear that conflict but seem to be important, and ask, “If there is no right or wrong decision, what is the key deciding factor that will help you decide what to do?” Some examples include the following:

除了探索客户话语的含义，区分辨析还能帮助揭示价值或愿望的冲突。说出你听到的两件相互冲突却又似乎都重要的事，然后问：「如果没有对错之分，那么帮助你决定怎么做的关键决定因素是什么？」以下是一些例子：

  • ✦ A client is offered a great job but is afraid it will be disruptive to his family.

- ✦ 一位客户得到了一份很棒的工作机会，却担心会打乱家庭生活。

  • ✦ A client has the opportunity to gain more recognition for her value at work but is worried she would have to work more hours, which could impact her health.

- ✦ 一位客户有机会在工作中获得更多对其价值的认可，却担心自己得加班，从而影响健康。

  • ✦ A client likes the stress of meeting commitments at the last minute but recognizes he might get better results if he starts the process earlier.

- ✦ 一位客户喜欢在最后一刻才兑现承诺带来的压力，却又意识到如果更早启动流程，或许会有更好结果。

Distinctions can help your client go deeper into their beliefs and desires. They might also see that a solution doesn’t have to be one or the other option, but maybe something can be done in the middle ground.

区分辨析能帮你的客户更深入自己的信念与愿望。他们也可能看到，解决方案不必非此即彼，也许可以在中间地带找到办法。

Use distinctions to cut through confusion. The conversation will move forward more quickly.

用区分辨析来拨开困惑。对话会推进得更快。

Pairing Summarizing with Questions

Once you summarize, recap, paraphrase, encapsulate, and draw distinctions, it helps to follow up with a curious question. Even a closed question such as “Is this how you see it?” can be a powerful clarifier. You don’t have to spend time remembering a question you used in a previous session. The questions emerge from the reflections you share.

一旦你进行了概述、重述、释义、凝练以及区分辨析，紧接着抛出一个带着好奇的问题会很有帮助。即便是一个封闭式问题，例如「你是这样看的吗？」，也能成为强有力的澄清工具。你不必费心去记住在之前某次会话中用过的问题。问题会从你分享的反思中自然涌现。

Tip Sheet

Tip Sheet

Best Practices for Active Replay

Best Practices for Active Replay

Your clients will often get lost trying to explain their thinking. The act of summarizing cuts through the fog so they have clarity around their blocks and options. Use the following tips to succinctly reflect your clients’ experiences to help them objectively observe their situations:

客户常常在试图解释自己的想法时迷失。概述这一举动能拨开迷雾，让他们对自己的障碍和选择变得清晰。运用以下技巧，简明地映照客户的体验，帮助他们客观地观察自己的处境：

  • Use your client’s words when recapping or encapsulating the dilemmas, desires, and beliefs holding them back. Then, ask what their key words mean. Key words are generally repeated or emotionally charged descriptors or phrases identifying what they don’t like or what they think they need.

- 在重述或凝练那些阻碍客户的困境、愿望和信念时，使用客户自己的话。然后，询问这些关键词的含义。关键词通常是被反复提及或带有情绪色彩的描述词或短语，指向他们不喜欢的东西，或他们认为自己需要的东西。

  • Use metaphors to paraphrase how clients see or feel about a situation. Paint a picture that represents what they are expressing and invite your client to expound on or change the image. You might start with “It feels like” or “It seems you are.” However they respond, ask what picture they would like to see instead.

- 用隐喻来释义客户对某一处境的看法或感受。描绘一幅画面，呈现他们正在表达的内容，并邀请客户展开或改写这个意象。你可以从「感觉像是……」或「看来你正在……」开始。无论他们如何回应，都问问他们希望看到的画面是什么。

  • Use distinctions to clarify for both you and the client by offering two different definitions of the words they use to describe what they want or need to resolve. The basic format of a distinction is “Do you mean this . . . or this . . . ?” or “Do you want (or fear most) this . . . or this . . . ?”

- 运用区分辨析来为你和客户双方澄清：针对客户用来描述自己想要或需要解决之事的词语，提供两个不同的定义。区分辨析的基本句式是「你指的是这一种……还是那一种……？」或「你想要（或最害怕）的是这个……还是那个……？」

  • After hearing and reflecting many excuses, highlight the options by asking, “Bottom line, are you willing to find a way to create your desired outcome or is this not a good time to try?” You can also ask if what they said they wanted is important enough to act on despite their fears or roadblocks. Then you can coach them to determine what they can to do to move toward their desires.

- 在倾听并映照了许多借口之后，通过发问来凸显选项：「说到底，你愿意想办法创造出自己想要的结果，还是现在不是尝试的好时机？」你也可以问，他们所说的想要的东西，是否重要到足以让他们不顾恐惧或阻碍去行动。然后，你可以教练他们去确定自己能做些什么，以朝愿望迈进。

reflecting physical and emotional shifts

When they don’t have the words or are reluctant to say what is running through their mind, sharing the gestures, hesitations, pauses, and eye movements you observe and asking if they would share what thoughts were coming up for them at that moment can prompt them to begin to articulate what they have yet to talk about.

当他们找不到词语，或不愿说出脑海中盘旋的东西时，分享你所观察到的手势、犹豫、停顿与眼神游移，并问他们是否愿意分享那一刻涌上心头之念，能促使他们开始说出尚未谈过的内容。

Gestures, posture, and emotional shifts are indications that a new thought is forming. Sharing the shifts you observe encourages them to talk about emerging ideas.

手势、姿态与情绪的变化，是新念头正在形成的迹象。分享你观察到的变化，会鼓励他们谈出正在浮现的想法。

When I summarize what people have said, they stop and think about their thinking. When I observe and share shifts in their body and energy, they stop and think about what they are experiencing. Then, I follow up the reflection of what I noticed with a request to share what is coming into their thoughts or what emotion just sent them somewhere else. They often reveal a belief, conflict, or fear that they resisted naming until now.

当我概述人们说过的话，他们会停下，思考自己的思考。当我观察并分享他们身体与能量的变化时，他们会停下，思考自己正在体验什么。然后，在我对自己所注意到之物的映照之后，我会紧接着请求他们分享：什么正进入他们的念头，或什么情绪刚刚把他们带去了别处。他们常常会揭示一个信念、冲突或恐惧——那是他们一直抗拒点名的，直到此刻。

Typical shifts you will notice include these behaviors:

你会注意到的典型变化包括这些行为：

  • ✦ Looking down or away with a pause, sigh, or gasp

- ✦ 低头或移开视线，伴以停顿、叹息或倒抽一口气

  • ✦ Hesitating or talking slower

- ✦ 犹豫，或说话变慢

  • ✦ Coming alive with more animation

- ✦ 变得生动、更有活力

  • ✦ Stressing the words always or never when describing other people’s behavior

- ✦ 描述他人行为时，强调「总是」或「从不」

  • ✦ Using the word really accompanied by a heightened tone that accentuates a declaration, such as “What I really want” or “What I really can’t stand”

- ✦ 使用「真的」一词，并配以加重的语调来强调一个宣告，例如「我真正想要的」或「我真的受不了的」

Recognize and share the emotional and physical shifts without attempting to make your client feel better. Then, use compassionate curiosity to explore what motivated this reaction or the meaning behind their shifts or words. If they stumble when explaining themselves, summarize or rephrase what they say to help them find the words for their new awareness. Eventually, you will ask how this relates to or changes what they said was their desired outcome for the coaching conversation.

识别并分享情绪与身体的变化，但不要试图让客户感觉好些。然后，运用悲悯的好奇去探索是什么引发了这个反应，或其变化与言辞背后的含义。如果他们在解释自己时卡住，就概述或改写他们说的话，帮他们找到词语来表达新的觉察。最终，你会问这如何关联或改变了他们所说的、本次教练对话的理想结果。

Some coaches are adept at sharing when clients express enthusiasm, passion, or relief but resist asking about what they see as negative emotions, such as anger, cynicism, or shame. Some coaches notice these expressions but think they have to jump in and calm the reaction. These actions are judgments that the emotion is negative. You are not allowing clients to work through their emotions so that they gain a better understanding of how they are changing how they see reality and themselves.

有些教练善于在客户表达热情、激情或释然时去分享，却抗拒追问他们视为负面的情绪，例如愤怒、愤世或羞耻。有些教练注意到了这些表达，却认为必须插手去安抚反应。这些做法都是把情绪判为负面的评判。你没有让客户去穿越自己的情绪，从而更理解自己如何正在改变对现实与自我的看法。

Your job isn’t to make clients feel better. You are there to coach them to see better.

你的工作不是让客户感觉好些。你在那里，是为了教练他们看得更清楚。

Clients can lose trust if you stifle their reactions. When you interrupt the coaching to render aid, they may no longer feel they can fully express themselves to you. They might even feel bad for making you uncomfortable.

如果你压抑他们的反应，客户会失去信任。当你中断教练去施以援手，他们或许不再觉得自己能对你全然表达。他们甚至可能因为让你不舒服而觉得糟糕。

Although most of us were brought up believing the expression of some emotions is bad, in coaching, all emotions are an expression of the human experience. Emotions provide vital information about clients’ perceptions. They might even be used to facilitate change, as when anger is channeled to activate courage and when grief can lead to gratitude. No growth can occur if some emotions are overlooked.

尽管我们大多在「某些情绪的表达是坏的」这一信念中长大，但在教练中，所有情绪都是人类体验的一种表达。情绪提供了关于客户感知的重要信息。它们甚至可被用来促成改变，比如愤怒被引导去激活勇气，而悲伤能通向感恩。若某些情绪被忽视，成长便无从发生。

Appreciating All Emotions

Maintaining trust and safety in coaching requires you to provide a safe, judgment-free space for clients to shed their tears, scream about injustice, and declare the end of all hope. You must accept doubts and disappointments to be able to explore their fears and expectations. Trying to make someone who is crying feel better negatively affects the coaching, no matter the value of your intention.

在教练中维系信任与安全，需要你提供一个安全、免于评判的空间，让客户能流下眼泪、为不公而呐喊、宣告所有希望的终结。你必须接纳怀疑与失望，才能去探索他们的恐惧与期望。试图让一个在哭泣的人感觉好些，无论你的初衷多好，都会对教练产生负面影响。

Clients don’t need you to cheer them up. They want you to acknowledge they are okay no matter what they feel.

客户不需要你给他们打气。他们想要你确认：无论感受到什么，他们都还好。

You can show you care without taking care of clients.

你可以表达你在乎，而不必照顾客户。

Using Nonreactive Empathy

As long as you don’t feel the risk of being harmed, your caring silence will take them through the arc of emotional reactions. This arc includes the moment the emotion is triggered, the peak of expression, and then the easing of the reaction. If you stay present, you can sense when the intensity of the emotion begins to diminish.

只要你不觉得自己有受到伤害的风险，你关怀的沉默就会带他们走完情绪反应的全过程。这个过程包括情绪被触发的那一刻、表达的高潮，以及反应逐渐平息。如果你保持临在，就能感知到情绪强度何时开始减弱。

The easing of their reaction is the best time to ask if they would be willing to share what thoughts are coming up for them in that moment. If they say they are sorry for reacting, just let the comment slip by and ask again if they would be willing to share what came up for them. The explanation that follows can lead to a deeper understanding of their feelings of loss, betrayal, disappointment, or doubt. As they come to better understand their emotional experiences, they are more empowered to discover what they need to do next.

他们反应平息时，是询问他们是否愿意分享那一刻涌上心头之念的最佳时机。如果他们为自己的反应道歉，就让这句话轻轻滑过，再次问他们是否愿意分享涌上心头的东西。随后的解释，能带来对他们失落、背叛、失望或怀疑等感受的更深理解。随着他们更理解自己的情绪体验，他们就更有能力去发现自己下一步需要做什么。

Using empathy in coaching means you sense their reaction, accept it, and then seek to understand what it means to your clients. You can’t help but interpret their stories through the lens of your own experiences, but be careful that you don’t think you know exactly what they are going through. You have not lived their life. The lens they use to interpret their situation is different from yours. You can never fully stand in their shoes, know what they know, and see what they see.

在教练中运用共情，意味着你感知他们的反应、接纳它，然后去理解这对你的客户意味着什么。你难免会通过自身经验的镜头去解读他们的故事，但要小心，别以为自己确切知道他们正经历着什么。你没过过他们的生活。他们用来解读自身处境的镜头，与你的不同。你永远无法完全设身处地，知道他们所知道的，看见他们所看见的。

Although the visceral reaction you have when sensing the emotions of others is real, your understanding of why they feel the way they do may or may not be accurate. You must detach from your experience to stay connected, curious, and compassionate to remain in the role of objective thinking partner.

尽管感知他人情绪时你产生的本能反应是真的，但你对「他们为何有此感受」的理解，可能准确，也可能不准确。你必须抽离自身经验，以保持联结、好奇与悲悯，从而留在客观思维伙伴的角色里。

Let your clients interpret their reactions. What made them feel sad, laugh at themselves, or hang their head as if they had regrets in that moment? If you have an inkling about the source of their reaction based on what they have said, you might offer an idea or distinction to choose from, such as “When you tensed up talking about work, are you more stressed by the piling up of assignments or the disrespect from your new boss?” or “Are you angry that the decision was made without you, or are you angry with yourself for not speaking up?” Your options will help them think more deeply about their thoughts and feelings even if their understanding differs from what you offer. You help them think more about their thinking even when you are wrong.

让你的客户去解读自己的反应。是什么让他们在那一刻感到悲伤、自嘲，或低下头仿佛心怀悔意？如果你根据他们说过的话，对他们的反应源头有所察觉，你可以提供一个想法或一项区分辨析供其选择，例如「你谈到工作时紧绷起来，是因为任务堆积更让你有压力，还是因为新老板的不尊重？」或「你是因为决定在没你参与下做出而生气，还是因为自己没发声而对自己生气？」即便他们的理解与你的提议不同，你的选项也会帮他们更深地思考自己的念头与感受。哪怕你错了，你也帮他们更多地思考了自己的思考。

A common question coaches ask me is “Wouldn’t it be helpful if I share my story and how I handled a similar situation?” Most of the time, the answer is no. It is common for humans to recall their own stories when making sense of the experience someone shares. When coaching, jumping in to tell your story is more likely a veiled attempt to give advice. You turn into a fixer, not a coach. If you swear your story would be helpful so they at least know someone else has walked a similar path, wait until the coaching session reaches a conclusion to ask whether they would like to hear what happened to you.

教练常问我一个问题是：「如果我分享自己的故事，以及我如何处理类似处境，难道没有帮助吗？」大多数时候，答案是不。人在理解他人分享的体验时，回想自己的故事是常事。但在教练中，贸然讲出你的故事，更像是在变相给建议。你就变成了「修理者」，而非教练。如果你确信自己的故事会有帮助，好歹让他们知道有人走过相似的路，那就等到教练会话接近尾声时，再问他们是否想听听你的经历。

Allow emotions to be a normal part of the coaching process. Appreciate their experience. Feel with them, and then let it go so you can return to being fully present. When they are ready to continue to look into their thoughts, proceed with compassionate curiosity on the path to their desired outcome.

让情绪成为教练过程中正常的一部分。欣赏他们的体验。与他们同感，然后放下，好让你能回到全然的临在。当他们准备好继续审视自己的念头时，带着悲悯的好奇，走上通往他们理想结果的路。

When Empathy Goes Too Far

Sara Hodges and Robert Biswas-Diener differentiate empathic concern, which is when you feel compassion and warmth for a person, and empathic distress, which occurs when you feel their emotions with them and maybe even mirror when they cry, show disgust, or clench their fists in anger. 2 Mirroring a slight facial expression may demonstrate understanding. Echoing their full emotional display goes too far.

With empathy, you will feel their stress, anxiety, and anger in your body. You might feel their pain emotionally and physically. If you let these emotions sit in your body, your body and mind can be emotionally hijacked. Your emotions will cloud your objectivity. You are more likely to jump to knowing what the client should do instead of relaxing into the unknown with curiosity.

Unbridled empathy can lead to concentrations of the stress hormone cortisol, making it difficult to release the emotions. 3 Taking on other people’s feelings so that you live their experience can make you susceptible to feelings of depression, which can lead to emotional burnout.

Most people long to feel seen, heard, and valued no matter what they express, but they don’t need you to feel sad, angry, or anxious with them. Your brain and body sense your clients’ emotional reactions instantaneously. It’s up to you to notice your reaction and then breathe and release the emotions so you can respond in an objective way.

Sara Hodges 与 Robert Biswas-Diener 区分了共情关切（empathic concern）与共情困扰（empathic distress）：前者是你对一个人感到悲悯与温暖，后者发生在你与他们一同感受其情绪，甚至在他们哭泣、露出厌恶或愤怒地攥紧拳头时去镜像。2 镜像一个细微的面部表情或许能展现理解。复述他们全部的情绪展露就过头了。

You embrace emotional expressions by observing physical and energy shifts, sensing a feeling in your body, and then breathing out the emotions so you can remain compassionately curious.

How you use your empathy lets clients fully express themselves without the fear of being judged. As the energy of their emotions subsides, they can think more clearly about their thinking. Coaching can then move forward.

Tip Sheet

Best Practices for Noticing Emotional Shifts

Expressing curiosity about physical and energetic shifts spurred by emotions can be powerful as well as intimidating for both you and your client. You need to manage your discomfort to objectively share what you witnessed. You also need to stay calm and connected when your clients have an intense reaction to your reflections because of what they are realizing about themselves or the situation that interferes with the narratives they had been believing. The following four tips will help you effectively share the shifts in energy and movements you witness as an offer for your clients to consider:

带着共情，你会在身体里感受到他们的压力、焦虑与愤怒。你可能在情绪上和身体上都感到他们的痛苦。如果你让这些情绪停留在体内，你的身心就会被情绪劫持。你的情绪会遮蔽你的客观性。你更可能跳到「知道客户该做什么」，而不是带着好奇放松地走入未知。

  • Notice shifts in clients’ posture, tone of voice, facial expressions, and eye movements. Start your sentences with “I noticed . . .” or “When you . . . , I am curious about what you experienced.” If they don’t respond directly, ask what might have motivated the gesture. Pause to let your clients process your words. Give them time to think as they sort out their response. Don’t push if they aren’t ready to talk about how they feel. They may need time to make sense of the new information as they let go of what they thought they knew.

无拘的共情会导致压力激素皮质醇的堆积，使情绪难以释放。3 承载他人的感受、以至活成他们的体验，会让你容易陷入抑郁情绪，进而导向情绪耗竭。

  • Be receptive to their experience, not judging an emotion as good or bad. Your clients need to feel safe to express themselves freely. If their emotions make you uncomfortable, unless you fear physical harm, breathe and release the tension so you can stay present, open, and curious. Remind yourself to warmly regard the human in front of you who is trusting you to help them move through a dilemma. Use your compassionate silence to maintain the strength in your bond of safety, trust, and respect.

大多数人无论表达什么，都渴望感到被看见、被听见、被重视，但他们并不需要你陪着他们感到悲伤、愤怒或焦虑。你的大脑和身体会瞬时感知客户的情绪反应。能否注意到自己的反应、然后呼吸并释放情绪，以客观的方式回应，取决于你。

  • Practice questioning with courageous curiosity. When their emotional energy subsides, help them understand what thoughts triggered their reaction. Do they know what they aren’t getting that is causing their frustration? What thoughts led them to seem so sad? Does their excitement represent something of value to them? If they say they don’t know what caused them to pause or react, offer a possibility as a question or distinction to choose from, such as “Could your anger be based on the increase of responsibilities or that your boss gave you the assignments without talking to you first?” or “When you went from frustration with your sister to a frowning silence, did something shift in your awareness about your sister or yourself?” Accept their response. Your options might help them think about their reactions, but you need to let them determine their own interpretation of their emotional reactions.

你通过观察身体与能量的变化、在身体里感知一种感受，再呼出这些情绪，来接纳情绪表达，从而保持悲悯的好奇。

  • Discover what the emotions of acceptance and curiosity feel like in your brain and body. Where do you feel the tension of discomfort? The quicker you catch this tension, the quicker you can breathe your chosen emotions into your body. Choosing emotions such as calm, caring, courageous, and curious will help you quickly return to being wholly with your client through their experience.

你如何运用共情，决定了客户能否在没有被评判的恐惧下充分表达自己。随着情绪能量消退，他们能更清晰地思考自己的念头。教练便可继续推进。

Chapter 12

CHAPTER 8 NAVIGATING DESIRE Clearing the Fog to See the Road

One’s destination is never a place but rather a new way of looking at things.

—H ENRY M ILLER

T HE BEST PRACTICE for using your skills of active replay is taking time to clarify the picture of what clients want that they don’t have at that moment in relation to the situation they want to discuss. This vision becomes the desired outcome of the session. Most clients have not conceptualized what they desire to have or create when you start the conversation. They may be able to describe their problem and what they don’t want or can’t have or doesn’t feel right to do. They may want to make a decision or plan, but what they ideally want as a result of the decision or plan is vague. Coaching them to create the mental image of what they truly want takes time.

一个人的目的地从来不是一个地方，而是一种看待事物的全新方式。 ——HENRY MILLER 运用“主动重播”（active replay）技能的最佳实践，是花时间厘清客户在当下尚未拥有、却与想要探讨的情境相关的渴望画面。这个愿景成为本次对话的期望成果（outcome）。当你开启对话时，大多数客户尚未将他们渴望拥有或创造的东西概念化。他们也许能描述自己的问题，以及他们不想要、无法拥有或觉得做起来不对劲的事。他们可能想做个决定或计划，但作为决定或计划结果，他们理想中真正想要的是什么，却很模糊。引导他们构建出真正渴望之物的心理意象，需要时间。

Starting a coaching session with the question “What do you want from our session today?” only elicits details of their dilemma. Your session will be more meaningful if you ask what is on their mind and then take in how they describe their challenge, how they feel about what is going on, and what is limiting their success. Your reflections and clarifying questions ensure they feel heard and understood. Then, you can seek to discover what is important for them to change, what they can imagine is possible, and then what needs to be addressed or resolved that is holding them back from moving in the direction of their aspiration.

以“你今天想从我们的对话中得到什么？”这个问题开启教练对话，只会引出他们困境的细节。如果你先问他们心里在想什么，进而接纳他们如何描述挑战、对正在发生之事的感受，以及是什么限制了他们的成功，你的对话会更有意义。你的反思与澄清式提问，确保他们感到被倾听、被理解。然后，你才能去发现：对他们而言什么改变是重要的、什么是他们能想象的可能，以及是什么需要被处理或解决、从而阻碍了他们朝渴望的方向前进。

Without clarifying the ideal scenario, the session often meanders with no clear goal or purpose. Even if their desired outcome expands or completely changes as you explore their beliefs, needs, values, and doubts, the picture you first agreed to gives you the storyline to work from so they can see what changes as their awareness expands.

若不厘清理想情境，对话往往会漫无目的地游走，没有明确的目标或意图。即便在探索他们的信念、需求、价值观和怀疑时，期望成果会扩展或彻底改变，你们最初约定好的那幅画面，也为你提供了可据以工作的故事线，让他们能看到随着觉察的扩展，什么在发生变化。

Then, when you move to end the session, if you didn’t have a clear picture of their desired outcome to coach from, the actions they might commit to may or may not bring them closer to the future they want. Even if your client enjoyed the conversation, they will experience little progress over time.

那么，当你准备结束对话时，如果当初没有一幅清晰的期望成果画面作为教练的依据，他们可能承诺的行动，未必能把他们带向想要的未来。即便客户享受了这次对话，长此以往他们也会感觉进展甚微。

Establishing the bookends of coaching—the desired outcome of the session and the commitment to taking the next step—is vital to ensure clients have a new story they are motivated to bring to life.

确立教练的“两端封板”——本次对话的期望成果，以及采取下一步行动的承诺——对于确保客户拥有一个他们有动力去实现的新故事（story），至关重要。

There are three important practices for coaches to create the structure of the coaching conversation: (1) unwrapping what clients want instead of what they have now; (2) tracking progress of the conversations toward the stated outcome or, if they discover something more important they need to create, inviting them to change the outcome; and (3) coaching clients to crystallize and declare their insights and commitments to the actions they will take to ensure progress toward their outcome (see figure 1 ).

教练要构建教练对话的结构，有三项重要实践：（1）拆开客户想要什么，而非他们现在拥有什么；（2）追踪对话朝既定成果推进的进度，或者，若他们发现更需要创造某样更重要的东西，就邀请他们改变成果；（3）教练客户去结晶化并宣告他们的洞见（insight），以及对将采取的行动做出承诺，以确保朝成果迈进（见图 1）。

This chapter will help you unwrap and clarify the client’s desired outcome for the session. Then, as you seek to discover what is getting in the way of your smart, resourceful clients achieving what they want, you will learn how to track the coaching session to stay focused on the envisioned outcome or recognize when the picture transforms and they may or may not choose to create a new picture of what they desire. Guidelines for closing the session with actions will be covered in chapter 10 .

本章将帮助你拆开并厘清客户对本次对话的期望成果。随后，当你努力去发现是什么阻碍了聪明、足智多谋的客户达成所愿时，你将学会如何追踪教练对话，以保持对愿景成果的专注，或识别出画面何时发生了转变——以及他们可能（或可能不）选择去创造一幅关于渴望的新画面。关于以行动收尾对话的指引，将在第 10 章探讨。

unwrapping the desired outcome

I’m often asked what mastery in coaching is. When I found a study that tracked the brains of rappers as they composed a freestyle rap, I thought it must be similar to what happens in the brain of a master coach. Neuroscientists at the National Institute on Deafness and Other Communication Disorders scanned the brains of twelve professional rappers with a functional magnetic resonance imaging machine (fMRI). The scientists discovered that although the brain’s executive functions were active at the start and end of the song, during freestyle, the parts of the brain responsible for self-monitoring, critiquing, and editing were deactivated. The researchers explained that the rappers were “freed from the conventional constraints of supervisory attention and executive control,” so insights could freely emerge.” 1

我常被人问到，教练的精通（mastery）究竟是什么。当我看到一项追踪说唱歌手即兴说唱（freestyle rap）时大脑活动的研究时，我想这必定与大师级教练大脑中发生的情况相似。美国国家耳聋与其他交流障碍研究所（National Institute on Deafness and Other Communication Disorders）的神经科学家用功能性磁共振成像（fMRI）扫描了十二位职业说唱歌手的大脑。科学家发现，尽管大脑的执行功能在歌曲开头和结尾时处于活跃状态，但在即兴说唱过程中，负责自我监控、批评和编辑的大脑区域却被关闭了。研究人员解释说，说唱歌手“从监督性注意和执行控制的常规约束中解放出来”，于是洞见得以自由涌现。¹

Figure 1. The path of coaching.

图 1. 教练的路径。

Leftmost box reads “What do you want?”; middle box reads “What do you really want?”; rightmost box reads “What will you do now?”

最左侧的方框写着“你想要什么？”；中间的方框写着“你真正想要什么？”；最右侧的方框写着“你现在要做什么？”

In other words, rappers used the executive function of their cognitive brains as they started rapping to deliberately set the intention of the composition. Once they had a sense of where they were going, they switched off their inner analyzer and critic. This allowed for more activity in the middle brain, where the eruption of new ideas, or creativity, takes place. As they moved to close out the song, their cognitive brains came back online to consciously complete the composition.

换句话说，说唱歌手在开始说唱时，会运用其认知大脑的执行功能，刻意设定作品的意图。一旦他们清楚了方向，便关掉了内在的分析者与批评者。这就让中间脑区有了更多活动，而新想法的迸发——亦即创造力——正是在那里发生。当他们接近歌曲结尾时，认知大脑重新上线，有意识地完成作品。

Like a rap, most of coaching should be spontaneous, creative interactions that use the clients’ experience to surface what they want and what is getting in the way of achieving this outcome. Effectiveness relies on the coach and client first discovering the desired destination of the coaching session. Then, the coach can flow with the client without cognitive interruptions until they move to wrap it up at the end.

与说唱一样，教练的大部分内容应当是自发的、富有创造性的互动，借助客户的经验，把他们的渴望，以及阻碍达成此成果（outcome）的因素呈现出来。成效有赖于教练与客户首先发现本次教练对话期望抵达的目的地。然后，教练便能与客户一同流动，不被认知打断，直到最后收尾。

In The 7 Habits of Highly Effective People, Stephen Covey listed “Begin with the end in mind” as his second habit. Covey wrote about the power of envisioning the best possible scenario to keep the context of the conversation positive when exploring the dangers of risks. 2 Researchers Richard Boyatzis and Kleio Akrivou wrote that without imagining the ideal scenario and then the ideal self in that picture, the focus will stay on “deficits, stories of short-comings, and the need to comply with social expectations, pressures, and controls.” 3 Clients need to see a possible future to courageously move through their fears and doubts.

在《高效能人士的七个习惯》（The 7 Habits of Highly Effective People）中，Stephen Covey 将“以终为始”（Begin with the end in mind）列为他的第二个习惯。Covey 写到，在探索风险的危险时，构想最理想情境的力量能让对话的脉络（context）保持积极。² 研究者 Richard Boyatzis 和 Kleio Akrivou 写道，若不先在脑海中构想理想情境，进而构想身处其中的理想自我，注意力就会停留在“缺失、关于不足的叙事，以及迎合社会期望、压力和控制的需要”上。³ 客户需要看到一个可能的未来，才能鼓起勇气穿越恐惧与怀疑。

Envisioning what the client wants is rarely easy. If you ask a client what they want to get out of the session at the beginning of the conversation, they will just talk about the problem, not the outcome. Or the client declares what they want and then launches into their view of both the circumstances responsible for the problem and their rationalizations for their inaction. They might even say they don’t know what they want.

构想客户想要什么，鲜少容易。如果你在对话开始时就问客户想从这次对话中得到什么，他们只会谈论问题，而不是成果（outcome）。或者客户会声明自己想要什么，随即展开他们对该问题成因的看法，以及对自己不作为的合理化解释。他们甚至可能会说不知道自己想要什么。

Coach slowly up front. Don’t push clients to see into the future. Relax and listen to their story. With reflections and a clarifying question, their emotional fog will dissipate as they begin to understand what they need to feel more content and confident with the outcome they design.

在开头放慢节奏。不要催着客户去眺望未来。放松下来，聆听他们的故事（story）。借助反思性陈述和一个澄清式问题，随着他们开始理解自己需要什么才能对亲手设计的成果感到更满足、更自信，他们情绪上的迷雾便会消散。

If clients are reluctant to designate an outcome, ask them to talk about what they want more of or less of compared to what they have that day. They may want a more fulfilling job or to have more energy or be less afraid. Then, you can ask them to describe what more or less might look like, including how they would define themselves in the picture. If they keep going back to their reasons for being stuck, you might invite the client to consider what life would look like if the obstacles they named did not exist.

如果客户不愿指明一个成果（outcome），就请他们谈谈，相较于当天的现状，他们想要“更多”什么或“更少”什么。他们可能想要一份更有成就感的工作，或拥有更多精力，或更少恐惧。接着，你可以请他们描述“更多”或“更少”可能是什么样子，包括他们会在那幅画面中如何定义自己。如果他们一直回到自己被困住的理由上，你可以邀请客户去设想：如果他们所说的那些障碍并不存在，生活会变成什么样子。

Distilling and getting clarity on what the clients want to create, change, or explore in the coaching session can take time. You will use your skills of presence, active listening, and evoking awareness with concise reflections and questions.

提炼并厘清客户想在教练对话中创造、改变或探索什么，需要时间。你将运用自己的临在（presence）、积极倾听和激发觉察的技能，配以简洁的反思与提问。

Case Study

案例研究

My client said her goal was to work with two leaders on her project team to create a solid action plan everyone would agree to. She started listing the activities she would like to see in the plan. After hearing a few, I asked, “Do you mind if I interrupt you? I want to make sure I understand what you need.”

我的客户说，她的目标是在项目团队中与两位领导者合作，制定一个所有人都会认同的扎实行动计划。她开始列举她希望出现在计划中的活动。听了几个之后，我问道：“你介意我打断你一下吗？我想确认一下我是否理解你的需要。”

She said, “Right. It’s easy for me to get lost in the details.”

她说：“对。我很容易陷进细节里。”

I said, “You seem to know what you want in the plan. What do you want your leaders to do with your suggestions once you present them?”

我说：“你似乎很清楚自己在计划里想要什么。当你把建议呈现出来后，你希望你的领导们怎么对待你的建议？”

“I am trying to present a middle ground so maybe they could agree to something. Maybe use some of or build on my ideas. They keep arguing about what is the right direction to take with no attempt to negotiate. Time is running out. Jobs are on the line. How do I make them see that?”

“我想提出一个折中方案，这样他们也许能就某件事达成一致。也许采纳我的一些想法，或在我的想法上继续发展。他们一直争论该朝哪个正确方向走，却毫无谈判的诚意。时间不多了。工作岌岌可危。我怎么才能让他们看清这一点？”

To clarify her role, I asked, “How responsible are you for delivering the plan?”

为了厘清她的角色，我问道：“你对于交付这份计划承担多大责任？”

“They don’t report to me, so I can’t make them do anything, but we all go down if we don’t deliver the plan. What if they ignore me? My job might be on the line too!”

“他们不向我汇报，所以我没法强迫他们做任何事，但如果我们交不出计划，大家就都完了。要是他们不理我怎么办？我的工作也可能不保！”

Her agitation was escalating. I said, “I see how frustrated you are with their conflict in light of the urgency of having a plan. And you don’t have the authority to make them—”

她的焦躁在不断升级。我说：“我看得出来，鉴于必须有份计划的紧迫性，你对他们的冲突有多么沮丧。而且你并没有权威去命令他们——”

She interrupted me. “Yes, I’m anxious, but I’m not their boss. I can’t really draw the line, can I?”

她打断了我。“是的，我很焦虑，但我不是他们的老板。我真的划不了那条线，对吧？”

I asked, “I don’t know, can you? What does drawing the line look like?”

我问道：“我不知道，你能吗？‘划清界限’会是什么样子？”

“I would clearly state that if we don’t come up with something, all our jobs, including theirs, are on the line.”

“我会明确说明：如果我们拿不出个方案，我们所有人的工作，包括他们的，都会受到威胁。”

“What is the worst that can happen if you clearly state this truth?”

“如果你清楚地陈述这个事实，最坏会发生什么？”

“They ignore me, which won’t change anything we have now.”

“他们不理我，这跟现在的情况没什么两样。”

I summarized: “So I hear your goal is to confront this situation head-on by clearly stating what absolutely must happen right now and why. Right?”

我总结道：“所以我听下来，你的目标就是直面这个局面——清楚说明眼下绝对必须发生什么、以及为什么。对吗？”

“Yes, I just need to say it—what needs to happen now and why, out loud, boldly, and firmly so they hear me.” Her resolve was much stronger than when we started. The conversation then moved toward what she needed to do, feel, and be to loudly, boldly, and firmly state what had to happen, immediately.

“对，我只是需要把它说出来——现在必须发生什么、为什么，要大声、勇敢而坚定地说出来，让他们听见我。”她的决心比我们开始时坚定得多。随后对话转向了她需要去做、去感受、去成为什么，才能立即大声、勇敢、坚定地说出必须发生的事。

As we moved to close out the session, she defined the steps she would take to get the leaders’ commitment to compromise. I asked her what she would do if her plan didn’t work. She said she would at least know she did her best. She could handle whatever came next. Maybe we could talk about her career plans in our next conversation.

当我们接近对话收尾时，她明确了自己将采取的步骤，以争取领导者对妥协的承诺。我问她，如果她的计划不管用会怎么办。她说她至少会知道自己尽了最大努力。接下来的任何结果她都能应对。也许我们可以在下次对话中谈谈她的职业规划。

An Outcome Is Not a Topic or Decision

Clients might have only a topic to discuss, such as how to improve their leadership presence, how to better accept change, or what they want next for themselves in their lives. Coaching doesn’t have to be about problem-solving; the focus may be on personal or professional development.

客户可能只有一个要探讨的主题（topic），比如如何提升领导力临在（presence）、如何更好地接纳变化，或者他们人生中下一步想要什么。教练不一定要围绕解决问题；焦点也可以放在个人或职业发展上。

Starting with a topic, you can coach clients toward an outcome by asking questions such as “What results are you hoping to get once we explore this topic?” or “How will you know you are creating the change you desire?” Coach them to describe one scenario they would like to define as their measure of success so you both understand what they want to create.

从一个主题出发，你可以通过提问把客户引向一个成果（outcome），例如“一旦我们探讨完这个主题，你希望得到什么结果？”或“你如何会知道，你正在创造自己渴望的改变？”教练他们去描述一个场景，作为他们成功的标准，让你们双方都理解他们想要创造什么。

Sometimes, topics are symptoms of personal needs, such as when clients claim they want more balance when they are actually feeling unfulfilled, unappreciated, or under pressure to perform better at work or home. If you accept their initial goal of balance, the outcome will focus on time or task management. Instead, if you dig deeper to find out what is actually missing for them and draining their energy, such as a lack of appreciation, meaningful challenges, or a sense of purpose, not only will the conversation be more meaningful but knowing they can envision how they will fill in what is missing for them will give them something to work on while making their present circumstances more palatable.

有时，主题只是个人需求的症状——比如客户声称想要更多平衡，实际上却感到不满足、不被赏识，或承受着在家庭或工作中表现更好的压力。如果你接受他们最初关于“平衡”的目标，成果就会聚焦在时间或任务管理上。相反，如果你深入挖掘，找出对他们而言真正缺失、并消耗他们能量的东西——比如缺乏赏识、有意义挑战，或目标感——那么对话不仅更有意义，而且，知道他们能构想出如何填补自己的缺失，会给他们一些可着手的事，同时让当下的处境更易忍受。

Clients often come to the conversation with a goal to make a decision when actually they have already made a decision that they are afraid to act on. This is a common scenario when someone wants to leave a job, turn down a project, or take an action that might harm a relationship. Their fears might be compounded by guilt.

客户常带着“做个决定”的目标来对话，但实际上他们已经做了决定，只是不敢付诸行动。当有人想离职、拒绝一个项目，或采取可能损害一段关系的行动时，这是常见情境。他们的恐惧可能因内疚而加剧。

You might start by looking at the likelihood of the consequences they fear will happen. It is also good to listen for the word should based on what other people might think if they acted based on only what they desire. Then, you can explore what they will regret in the future if they choose to keep everyone else happy instead of honoring their needs.

你可以从审视他们恐惧的后果发生的可能性入手。同时，留意“应该”（should）这个词也很有用——它基于：如果别人只按自己的渴望行事，他人会怎么想。接着，你可以探索：如果他们选择让其他人都开心、而非尊重自己的需求，未来会后悔什么。

Often, they will find that their fears are unfounded or exaggerated but had provided rationalizations to not take a risk. These realizations free their brains to envision what they want when they bravely choose for themselves while taking into consideration how they might handle the opinions of the people they care about.

通常，他们会发现自己的恐惧毫无根据或被夸大了，而此前这些恐惧为“不去冒险”提供了合理化。这些领悟解放了他们的大脑，让他们能构想自己想要的——当他们勇敢地为己选择，同时考虑如何应对自己在乎之人的看法时。

Another common scenario is when clients say they want to feel something they aren’t feeling now, such as to be more confident about speaking up. Confidence is the topic but not the outcome. If you ask them what is creating their lack of confidence, they may talk about their inexperience or old beliefs based on family or cultural rules. If you ask them if experience is the only thing keeping them from speaking up, they often say that even if they speak up, they worry they have nothing important to share.

另一个常见情境是，客户说他们想感受到此刻并未感受到的某种东西，比如对“发声”更有自信。自信是主题，却不是成果。如果你问是什么造成了他们的不自信，他们可能会谈到经验不足，或基于家庭、文化规则的旧信念。如果你问他们，经验是不是唯一阻止他们发声的东西，他们常常会说，即便发声了，也担心自己没什么重要的可分享。

Once you go deeper into exploring what they actually fear will happen when they embody the emotion they want, you can then shift the conversation to what is important enough or useful to others if they speak up despite their fears. For the goal of speaking up, you might explore what knowledge they have to offer, why people are asking them to be in the room, and who they see themselves as being when they speak up. Then, you can explore one of many paths, such as “Is it true that you will be ignored or disrespected if you speak up?” or “What would happen if someone disagreed with you?” or “What will it take for you to feel you are worthy enough and have good ideas to share?”

一旦你更深入地探索，当他们真正活出想要的情绪时，实际恐惧会发生什么，你便能把对话转向：即便带着恐惧，他们发声对他人而言足够重要或有何用处。针对“发声”这一目标，你可以探索他们能贡献什么知识、人们为何请他们到场、以及他们发声时如何看待自己。然后，你可以探索众多路径之一，例如“如果你发声，真的会被忽视或不尊重吗？”或“如果有人不同意你，会发生什么？”或“要让你感到自己足够有价值、有好想法可分享，需要什么？”

Once they are able to talk about their fears, needs, and possibilities, the coaching can shift to identifying what they want to do differently in the situations they fear. The picture of their desired outcome will emerge. Then, you can explore what they need to address and do to move toward their desires. You will learn how to coach around social needs more effectively in the next chapter.

一旦他们能谈论自己的恐惧、需求和可能，教练便可转向：识别在他们所恐惧的情境中，他们想做出什么不同的事。他们期望成果（outcome）的画面便会浮现。接着，你可以探索他们需要注意并处理什么，才能朝渴望迈进。关于如何更有效地围绕社会需求（social needs）进行教练，你将在下一章学到。

Remember, clients are often unsure of what they want or avoid speaking it out loud at the start of the conversation. Your job is to coach them to see and share what they really want. Once their desires are articulated, the actions they will proceed to take are easier to declare and commit to.

记住，客户常常不确定自己想要什么，或在对话开始时回避把它说出来。你的工作是教练他们去看见并分享自己真正想要的。一旦他们的渴望被阐明，他们接下来要采取的行动，便更容易去宣告并做出承诺。

Tip Sheet

提示单

Paths of Inquiry for Discovering the Desired Outcome

发现期望成果的探询路径

Once you get a clear understanding of the client’s story, spend time coaching to reveal answers to the following questions:

一旦你清晰理解了客户的故事（story），花时间教练，以揭示以下问题的答案：

  • What does your client want to happen, even if speaking the aspiration out loud creates discomfort? To facilitate picturing the ideal outcome, use your skills of summarizing, paraphrasing, and encapsulating what you hear they want or don’t want anymore. Ask what values and needs are important to them that might be missing. If the client keeps slipping back into the past to reiterate obstacles, ask again, “Knowing this happened in your past, what would you like to create differently now?” Even with compassion, your persistence can be frustrating for clients, but they eventually answer with a clear statement of their desire.

- 你的客户想要发生什么，即便大声说出这个渴望会带来不适？为助力勾勒理想成果，运用你的总结、复述和概括技能，呈现你听到的他们想要或不再想要的东西。问哪些对他们重要的价值观（values）和需求可能缺失了。如果客户不断滑回过去、重申障碍，再次问道：“既然这发生在你的过去，你现在想要创造什么不同？”即便带着同情，你的坚持也可能让客户恼火，但他们最终会以一句清晰的渴望声明作答。

  • What does your client feel is most important to begin creating now? Listen for what they say is irritating, confusing, uncomfortable, disappointing, or distressing. Reflect what you hear is the source of these emotions. Ask what is important about changing these feelings. Their response should lead to what they actually want to create if they could address the sources. When describing an important desired outcome, they often include a change in mindset that elevates their ability to generate new possibilities. After they envision their ideal scenario and mindset, ask who they are being in that picture, which should be both easy and inspiring for them to answer.

- 你的客户觉得现在最重要的是开始创造什么？留意他们说出的恼人、困惑、不适、失望或痛苦之事。把你听到的这些情绪的根源反射回去。问改变这些感受为何重要。他们的回答应当引向：如果根源得以处理，他们真正想创造什么。在描述一个重要的期望成果时，他们常会包含一种心态的改变，这能提升他们生成新可能的力。在他们构想出理想情境与心态之后，问他们在那幅画面中“正成为谁”——这对他们而言应当既容易又鼓舞人心。

  • What beliefs are causing your client’s frustration? When your client uses the word but and then states what is difficult or impossible to do, explore the reality of their excuse. Is change impossible or just difficult? Are they more concerned about failing or how other people will judge their efforts? What could they imagine if they took one step to begin shifting their frustration, belief, or fear? Exploring what little changes are in their control can be enough to envision a different possibility from what they are experiencing now. The first step is the most important one to make.

- 是什么信念引发了你客户的挫败感？当你的客户用了“但是”（but）这个词，接着陈述什么难以做到或不可能的，就去探索他们借口背后的现实。改变是“不可能”还是仅仅“困难”？他们更在意失败，还是更在意别人会怎么评判他们的努力？如果他们迈出一步，开始转化挫败、信念或恐惧，他们能想象什么？探索他们可控的那些微小改变，就足以构想出一个与当下体验不同的可能。第一步，是最该迈出的。

tracking the conversation based on the outcome

The agreement then keeps the coaching conversation on track. If the client wanders in a different direction, the coach reminds the client what they said they wanted to create and asks whether this is still the desired outcome or whether they’d like to focus on a new one.

这份协议（agreement）随后让教练对话保持在正轨上。如果客户走向了别的方向，教练会提醒客户他们曾说想要创造什么，并询问这是否仍是期望成果，还是他们想聚焦一个新的。

The process of discovering what clients actually want to create is often referred to as peeling the onion. As you remove layers defined by old beliefs and fears, the desires that lie underneath are revealed, which leads to a change in the envisioned outcome.

发现客户真正想创造什么的过程，常被称作“剥洋葱”。当你剥去由旧信念和恐惧所定义的层层外壳，底下潜藏的渴望便显露出来，从而引发愿景成果的改变。

Whether the visual of the outcome they yearn for expands or completely changes, you must notice what is emerging and invite clients to either accept a new direction for the coaching or discard it. Your job is to ensure the conversation is moving in a desired direction, whether they want to return from a side path to the main road or choose a new direction for the coaching that is more meaningful for them that day.

无论他们渴求的成果画面是扩展了还是彻底改变了，你都必须注意到正在浮现的东西，并邀请客户要么接受教练的一个新方向，要么舍弃它。你的工作是确保对话朝期望的方向推进——无论他们是想从岔路回到主路，还是选择对那天而言更有意义的教练新方向。

Changes in the desired outcome usually appear in one of two ways: (1) the client discovers a greater desire that they resisted seeing or speaking about before, which prompts the focus to shift to a new scenario the client wants to create, or (2) the client sees themselves differently when describing what the desired outcome looks like, which prompts the focus to shift to growing into a future self-image. These moments often occur when exploring frustrations or fears and they blurt out the true desire they want to realize. After reflecting the declaration and emotions expressed back to them, invite clients to concisely articulate the outcome they want to work on now. They can opt to look into the new desire they stated or stick with the initial outcome they defined as an important first step before taking on a greater challenge.

期望成果的改变通常以下列两种方式之一出现：（1）客户发现了一种更强烈的渴望，而此前他们一直抗拒看见或说出它，这促使焦点转向客户想要创造的新情境；或（2）客户在描述期望成果的样子时，以一种不同的方式看待自己，这促使焦点转向成长为未来的自我形象。这些时刻常发生在探索挫败或恐惧时——他们会脱口说出想要实现的真实渴望。在把他们的宣言和表达出的情绪反射回给他们之后，邀请客户简洁地阐明他们现在想要着手的成果。他们可以选择深入自己所说出的新渴望，也可以坚持最初界定的成果，将其作为迎接更大挑战前重要的第一步。

Horizontal versus Vertical Coaching to Define the Outcome

Horizontal coaching occurs when you use the initial client outcome as the definitive destination for the session. You may ask questions to explore the meaning of words used in the client’s description or inquire about the importance of achieving the outcome. Once the client responds, you might ask a few follow-up questions to ensure you agree on what they want that feels good to them, their reasoning behind that choice, and the challenges they need to resolve to achieve their aspirations. The conversation quickly moves to listing actions (see figure 2 ).

当你把客户最初的成果（outcome）当作本次对话的确定终点时，就发生了横向教练（horizontal coaching）。你可能会提问，来探索客户描述中所用词语的含义，或询问达成该成果的重要性。一旦客户回应，你也许会再追问几个问题，确保你们就“他们想要什么（这让他们感觉良好）、选择背后的理由，以及他们需解决哪些挑战才能实现渴望”达成一致。对话很快转向罗列行动（见图 2）。

The objective of horizontal coaching is to formulate plans to move forward. When looking at their challenges, you will probably ask what they might do to work through or eliminate the obstacle. You might help them sort through ideas, risks, and possible gains. You might ask what has stopped them from working on this plan. They may share an obstacle that they need to overcome. Coaching can include exploring internal and external motivations, but the focus is still on what to do next instead of going deeper into the beliefs, doubts, and concerns that might alter their desire as well as their action plan.

横向教练的目标是制定向前推进的计划。审视他们的挑战时，你大概会问：他们能做些什么来克服或消除障碍。你也许帮他们梳理想法、风险和可能的收益。你也许会问是什么让他们迟迟没有推进这个计划。他们可能分享一个需要克服的障碍。教练可以包括探索内在与外在动机，但焦点仍落在“下一步做什么”，而非深入那些可能改变其渴望与行动计划的信念、怀疑和顾虑。

Figure 2. Horizontal coaching.

图 2. 横向教练。

Leftmost box reads “What do you want? What will this give you?”; middle box reads “What do you need to resolve to move forward?”; rightmost box reads “What will you commit to do now?”

最左侧方框写着“你想要什么？这会带给你什么？”；中间方框写着“你需要解决什么才能前进？”；最右侧方框写着“你现在承诺做什么？”

With vertical coaching, the initial outcome is just the starting point of coaching. You then spend time coaching clients to consider what is important or fulfilling about their desire and what has hindered their movement. The outcome tends to evolve as clients reveal deeper desires that were hidden under old beliefs and fears.

而在纵向教练（vertical coaching）中，最初的成果只是教练的起点。你随后花时间教练客户去考量：关于他们的渴望，什么重要或令人满足，又是什么阻碍了他们的行动。随着客户揭示出藏在旧信念和恐惧之下的更深渴望，成果往往会演进。

Vertical coaching has more opportunities to coach at the identity level as well. You may go beyond behavior and motivations and examine the desire to develop “who I am.” The ideal time to explore the future self a person would like to become is immediately after the visual representation of the desired outcome has been clarified and agreed to by both coach and client. When the vision is clear, you can ask, “How would you define who you are in this picture?” The question often leads to the awareness of how self-definition plays into defining what has to happen to produce the changes the client envisions regardless if they think the transformation will be difficult or surprisingly easy.

纵向教练也有更多在“身份”层面教练的机会。你可以超越行为与动机，去审视发展“我是谁”的渴望。探索一个人想成为的“未来自我”的理想时机，是在教练与客户双方都已厘清并认同期望成果的可视表征之后。当愿景清晰时，你可以问：“你会如何定义在这幅画面中的自己？”这个问题常会导向一种觉察：自我定义如何影响“必须发生什么，才能产生客户所构想的改变”——无论他们认为转变会很难，还是出奇地容易。

Shifts made in identity—who I will be when I realize my desired outcome— increase confidence and commitment to growth. This is coaching the person, not the problem, at its best.

在身份上发生的转变——当我实现期望成果时，我将是谁——会增强信心，以及对成长的承诺。这正是“教练这个人，而非问题”的最佳体现。

Although horizontal and vertical coaching may both start with clarifying what clients want as an outcome of your time together and what they could gain that is important to them, vertical coaching takes time to explore the layers of their thinking more deeply. Instead of going from vision to action horizontally, with vertical exploration, you take time to flesh out what they truly want to happen. Then, you listen for beliefs clients state when describing why they question whether the outcome can be achieved or what has kept them from taking action up until that point (see figure 3 ).

尽管横向与纵向教练都可能始于厘清：客户想要什么，作为你们共度时光的成果；以及什么对他们重要的收获，但纵向教练会花时间更深入地探索他们思维的层层结构。纵向探索不是横向地从愿景走到行动，而是花时间充实他们真正想要发生的事。接着，你倾听客户在描述“为何质疑成果能否达成”或“是什么让他们至今未能行动”时所陈述的信念（见 图 3）。

This awareness-focused coaching approach might lead to identifying fears that they won’t get what they need from other people or that others will negatively judge and react to their choices. In this process of this exploration, they might discover that they need to create something else first so they can more easily move toward their bigger vision when they are ready.

这种以觉察（awareness）为核心的教练方式，可能导向识别出这样的恐惧：他们无法从他人那里得到所需，或他人会对他们的选择做出负面评判和反应。在这一探索过程中，他们也许会发现，需要先创造别的东西，这样当准备就绪时，才能更轻松地迈向更大的愿景。

Vertical coaching starts with a specific aim to clarify the focus and desired outcome of the session. Then, you move into the unknown or what has previously been unspoken. Your reflections and inquiries come from what they offer, not from standard and reliable questions you have used before. Trust the process to discover what they have been yearning for and what is most important for them now.

纵向教练始于一个明确的目标：厘清本次对话的焦点与期望成果。然后，你进入未知，或此前未被言说之物。你的反思与探询来自他们所提供的，而非来自你以往用过的那些标准而可靠的问题。信任这个过程，去发现他们一直渴求什么、以及此刻对他们最重要的是什么。

Figure 3. Vertical coaching—examples of what to explore.

图 3. 纵向教练——可探索内容的示例。

Topmost box is titled “Outcome” and contains the questions “What do you want?” and “What will this give you?” Middle box is titled “Personal Relationship to Outcome” and contains the questions “Why is this important to you now?” and “What is missing for you or stopping you from moving forward?” Bottom box is titled “Beliefs, Fears, Assumptions Affecting Actions” and contains the questions “How real are the consequences you fear?” followed by “Is the gain worth the risk?” and “Who will judge your actions?” followed by “What will you regret not doing a year from now?”

最上方方框题为“成果”（Outcome），包含问题“你想要什么？”和“这会带给你什么？”中间方框题为“与成果的个人关系”（Personal Relationship to Outcome），包含问题“为何这对现在的你很重要？”和“对你而言缺失了什么，或是什么阻止你前进？”最下方方框题为“影响行动的信念、恐惧、假设”（Beliefs, Fears, Assumptions Affecting Actions），包含问题“你恐惧的后果有多真实？”、继而“收益值得冒这个险吗？”、以及“谁会评判你的行动？”、继而“一年之后，你会因没做哪件事而后悔？”

Sometimes, clients need a safe place to talk through options horizontally, but if they struggle making a decision or finding a solution, awareness-focused vertical coaching is more effective at identifying what they actually want and then resolving what is getting in the way than solution- or action-focused horizontal coaching.

有时，客户需要一个安全的场所，来横向地梳理选项；但如果他们在做决定或找解决方案上挣扎，那么以觉察为核心的纵向教练，比以方案或行动为核心的横向教练，更能有效地识别出他们真正想要什么、进而解决阻碍。

For example, if you coach leaders, you will often have a session focused on how the leader might approach a difficult conversation. You may ask what a successful conversation would look like. The leader will describe a positive interchange. Horizontal coaching would look at what might happen to derail the desired outcome and what the leader might do to handle these distractions.

例如，如果你教练领导者，常常会有一场对话聚焦：这位领导者可能如何开展一场艰难的谈话。你可能会问，一场成功的谈话会是什么样子。领导者会描述一次积极的交流。横向教练会去审视：什么可能让期望成果脱轨，以及领导者可能做什么来应对这些干扰。

Even with an action plan, when the leader attempts to hold the conversation as promised, if emotions arise during the conversation, the plan is often forgotten. The leader either retreats, letting the current situation continue, or ends up being directive, giving the person an ultimatum to act the way the leader says is right.

即便有了行动计划，当领导者试图按承诺开展谈话时，如果谈话中情绪浮现，计划常常被忘掉。领导者要么退缩、任由现状继续，要么最终变得指令式，给对方下最后通牒，要其按领导者说的“正确”方式行事。

If you were to instead vertically coach the leader after you agree to what the conversation could look like, you might ask what could make the conversation difficult. The leader might describe a fear of handling the emotions the person will show or the assumption that these conversations never go well. After you both explore what “never go well” means, the focus might shift to examining the beliefs about emotional displays or what the leader needs to be able to work through during difficult moments in conversations.

如果你在双方认同谈话可能的样子之后，改为对领导者进行纵向教练，你也许会问：什么可能让这场谈话变得困难。领导者可能会描述一种恐惧——害怕应对对方将表现出的情绪，或抱有一个假设：这类谈话永远不会顺利。在你们共同探索“永远不会顺利”意味着什么之后，焦点可能转向审视关于情绪表达的信念，或领导者在谈话艰难时刻需要能够穿越的东西。

When I coach leaders, what often emerges is a fear of being wrong or judged as a bad leader. With further exploration, they might reveal another, more personal belief, such as “Just having this conversation means I failed at being a leader.” The unpacking of the layers of beliefs leads to a conversation that shifts from focusing on problem-solving actions to looking deeper at what the leader defines as good leadership. The desired outcome of your conversation will shift as well.

当我教练领导者时，常常浮现的是一种恐惧：怕犯错，或被评判为糟糕的领导者。随着进一步探索，他们可能揭示出另一个更个人的信念，比如“仅仅有这场谈话，就意味着我作为领导者失败了。”层层信念的拆解，会把对话从聚焦“解决问题的行动”，转向更深入地审视领导者如何定义“好的领导”。你对话的期望成果也会随之改变。

Vertical coaching is needed for anyone to facilitate talking through why they are avoiding conversations and actions that feel difficult. Brené Brown says most people have been taught to believe that vulnerability is a weakness. “It’s hard, and it’s awkward. And we don’t want to do it because we feel if we put ourselves out there, we’re going to get hurt. We’re going to fail.” 4 Keeping these thoughts inside restricts possibilities.

任何人若要促成一场对话，谈清楚自己为什么在回避那些感觉困难的谈话与行动，都需要纵向教练。Brené Brown 说，大多数人被教导去相信：脆弱（vulnerability）是一种弱点。“它艰难，且尴尬。我们不想去做，因为觉得如果把自己暴露出去，就会受伤。就会失败。”⁴ 把这些想法闷在心里，会限制可能。

Speaking fears out loud reduces the choke hold emotions have when deliberating what is real and possible. With coaching, clients’ outcomes often shift to the personal development needed to embrace the challenge.

大声说出恐惧，能减弱情绪在斟酌“何为真实、何为可能”时的扼制力。有了教练，客户的成果常会转向：接纳挑战所需的个人发展。

What’s Getting in the Way?

Tony Latimer, my colleague and brilliant leadership coach and trainer in Singapore, says his simple coaching model is to find out what clients want and then coach them to discover what is getting in the way of what they want. Once the blocks in progress are revealed, clients know what they want to do next.

Tony Latimer 是我在新加坡的同事，一位出色的领导力教练与培训师。他说，他简单的教练模型就是：先弄清楚客户想要什么，然后教练他们去发现，是什么阻碍了自己想要的。一旦进展中的阻碍被揭示，客户便知道下一步该做什么。

I add a few layers to Tony’s model to include the evolving outcome. Each time the outcome shifts, I ask my clients, “What is getting in the way of what you want?” (see figure 4 ).

我在 Tony 的模型上叠加了几层，把不断演进的成果（outcome）纳入其中。每次成果发生偏移，我都会问客户：“是什么阻碍了你想要的？”（见图 4）。

Exploring what is getting in the way of your smart, creative clients means you are curious about the beliefs, social needs, and values that hold their story together. We will explore these aspects in more detail in chapter 9 .

探索是什么阻碍了你那些聪明、富有创造力的客户，意味着你对支撑他们故事（story）的信念、社会需求（social needs）和价值观（values）充满好奇。我们将在第 9 章更详细地探讨这些面向。

Start with reflecting the beliefs and assumptions you hear as they describe their challenge. Then, you can affirm the importance of addressing both what is making the situation a challenge and what a new reality would look like, which opens the door for a deeper look at their fears and self-doubts.

从反思你在他们描述挑战时所听到的信念与假设（assumption）开始。接着，你可以肯定：既要处理让情境成为挑战的因素，也要处理新现实会是什么样子，这二者都很重要——这便打开了更深入审视他们恐惧与自我怀疑的大门。

For example, I coached a leader who said she wanted to find a way to announce a decision she had made that would inspire instead of discourage her team. When I asked what would be discouraging, she said she wasn’t sure they would want to drop their current projects to take on the new goals. But then, she said, “I’m not sure it’s about the projects. I don’t want to sound too demanding or too weak because I’m not sure I like the decision.” The decision had come from upper management, but she wasn’t sure it was the right thing to do. She thought the higher-ups didn’t realize the impact the decision would make on both morale and long-term results.

例如，我曾教练一位领导者，她说想找到一种方式，去宣布一个自己已做出的决定，既能鼓舞团队，又不至于让他们泄气。当我问什么会令人泄气时，她说不确定团队成员是否愿意放下当前项目去承接新目标。但接着她说：“我不确定问题出在项目上。我不想听起来太苛求，也不想显得太软弱，因为我不确定自己喜欢这个决定。”这个决定来自上层管理，而她不确定它是否正确。她认为高层没有意识到这个决定对团队士气和长期结果的影响。

Figure 4. Discovering the desired outcome.

图 4. 发现期望成果。

In the following order: Box 1 reads “What do you want?”; box 2 reads “What’s getting in the way?”; box 3 reads “So what I hear you really want is . . .”; box 4 reads “What’s getting in the way?”; box 5 reads “So what I hear you really really want is . . .”; and box 6 reads “What are you willing to commit to do?”

按以下顺序：方框 1 写着“你想要什么？”；方框 2 写着“是什么在阻碍你？”；方框 3 写着“所以我听下来，你真正想要的是……”；方框 4 写着“是什么在阻碍你？”；方框 5 写着“所以我听下来，你真正真正想要的是……”；方框 6 写着“你愿意承诺去做什么？”

Then, she said, “Honestly, it’s not who I am.” Forcing the decision impacted her sense of integrity as a leader. The coaching then shifted to her describing what she felt she needed to do and who she needed to be as she went back to the leadership team to express her concerns and alternative plans.

接着她说：“老实说，这根本不是我。”强行推行这个决定，冲击了她作为领导者的廉耻感。教练于是转向：她描述自己觉得需要去做什么、需要成为谁，以便回到领导团队面前表达她的担忧和替代方案。

Some clients might state an outcome they think they should want but either don’t believe it can happen or aren’t actually committed to it. Coaching requires you not only explore the picture of what they say they want but also reflect the emotions that may contradict what they are saying.

有些客户可能会说出一个他们认为“应该”想要的成果，却要么不相信它能实现，要么实际上并未对此做出承诺。教练要求你不只探索他们口中所说想要之物的画面，还要反思那些可能与他们所言相矛盾的感受。

Case Study

案例研究

I had a client who wanted to find a way to make one of his employees work better with the rest of his team. I asked what he meant by working better. He said she looked at everything negatively and rudely judged any idea to fix things. She was poisoning team motivation. He then started outlining his plan for her development when I asked if we could stop for a moment so I could share something I noticed. He agreed. I said, “You jumped into a plan you’ve obviously spent time creating, but your energy dropped. It sounds like you are giving me your grocery list.”

我有一位客户，想找到办法让手下一名员工更好地与团队其余成员协作。我问“协作更好”是什么意思。他说，她看一切都带着消极眼光，对任何改进的想法都粗鲁地评判。她在毒害团队的积极性。接着他开始勾勒为她制定的发展计划，这时我问他能否停一下，好让我分享一个我注意到的东西。他同意了。我说：“你一头扎进了一个你显然花了很多时间制定的计划，但你的能量却掉了下去。听起来你是在给我念你的购物清单。”

He let out a loud sigh and said, “Honestly, I’m just grasping at anything, but I don’t really think she’s going to change.”

他长长地叹了口气，说：“老实说，我只是在抓救命稻草，但我真的不觉得她会改变。”

We then explored the evidence he had for his belief. He had tried conversations with her focused on her future and how performing differently could help her. He sent her to classes she chose. He tied team behavior to her performance review. I said, “It sounds like you have done a number of things to solve this problem, that you’ve done a lot of work with no results. If you believe she is not going to change, what keeps driving you to find a way to change her?”

我们随后探索了他支撑这一信念的证据。他曾尝试与她谈话，聚焦她的未来，以及不同的表现如何能帮到她。他送她去上她自己选的课。他把团队行为纳入了她的绩效考核。我说：“听起来你为了这个问题已经做了不少事，花了很大力气却毫无结果。如果你相信她不会改变，是什么一直在驱使你去找办法改变她？”

He said, “Am I a bad leader for giving up on her?”

他说：“放弃她，我算不算一个糟糕的领导者？”

I said, “Would you flip that by telling me what a good leader would do in this situation?”

我说：“你能反过来吗——告诉我，在这种情况下，一个好的领导者会怎么做？”

After a long pause, he said, “I have to limit, or stop, how she is disrupting my team. She has so much potential, I just wish . . . I thought you might give me one more thing to try.”

长久的停顿后，他说：“我必须限制、或者制止她扰乱我团队的行为。她潜力很大，我只是希望……我以为你也许能再给我一样可以试的东西。”

I said, “The conflict causing your frustration is clear. You see potential even though she hasn’t shown interest in improving. In the meantime, your team is suffering. Is there a way you can show you aren’t giving up on her while taking care of your team?”

我说：“引发你挫败感的冲突很清楚。你看到了她的潜力，尽管她还没表现出改进的意愿。与此同时，你的团队在受苦。有没有一种方式，让你既能表明自己没有放弃她，又能照顾好你的团队？”

He smiled before he said, “Okay, so I’ve been trying to make it work here, but maybe we could find a place in the organization where she would be a better fit, maybe even enjoy her work more. A good leader wouldn’t ignore his obligation to his team.”

他微笑着说：“好吧，所以我一直想在这里把她安顿好，但也许我们可以在组织里找个她更合适的位置，甚至让她更享受自己的工作。一个好的领导者不会忽视自己对团队的义务。”

I said, “What has changed about your approach with her?” We looked at what he felt he would do and how he would handle any reaction she had, which included letting her go if they reached a dead end. I asked him, “How would you define who you are as a leader now?”

我说：“你对她的做法发生了什么改变？”我们审视了他觉得自己会做什么、以及他将如何应对她可能有的任何反应，包括在走到死胡同时让她离开。我问他：“你现在会如何定义自己作为领导者的身份？”

He said, “Okay, I’m not a bad guy. I’m a more courageous leader who is focused on the bigger picture. Now I’d like some coaching on what I can do to repair any damage done to my team.”

他说：“好吧，我不是个坏人。我是一个更有勇气、着眼于大局的领导者。现在我想就一件事接受教练：我能做些什么来修复对团队造成的任何伤害。”

If your client defines a desired outcome they don’t sound committed to, share the hesitation or lack of enthusiasm you noticed. They might reveal the thoughts they had been avoiding. Let them process their emotions, and ask how this revelation changes their options.

如果你的客户界定了一个期望成果，听上去却并未真正投入，就分享你所注意到的迟疑或缺乏热情。他们可能会吐露此前一直在回避的想法。让他们处理自己的情绪，并询问这一揭示如何改变了他们的选项。

When you accept what they say with no judgment, they will begin to accept themselves as well. Self-awareness leads to other possibilities.

当你不带评判地接纳他们所说的话，他们也会开始接纳自己。自我觉察会通向其他可能。

Inviting and Reinviting

While coaching, you want to keep in mind “Where are we going with this conversation?” If you coached the client to clearly define what they want to create or change that resolves the challenge they described, you should be able to spot when another challenge emerges that the client says is important to address. This is the time to invite the client to choose to either refine the original goal of the conversation or agree to go in a new direction. If they choose to explore the new challenge that emerged, then you will coach them to clearly state what a new outcome will look like.

在教练过程中，你要始终记着“我们这场对话要去往哪里？”如果你已教练客户清晰地界定了他们想要创造或改变什么，以解决他们所描述的挑战，你就应当能捕捉到：当客户说另一个挑战很重要、需要应对时，它何时浮现。这正是邀请客户去选择——要么精炼对话的初衷，要么同意转向一个新方向。如果他们选择探索浮现的新挑战，你就要教练他们清晰地陈述，一个新成果会是什么样子。

Sometimes, clients are so emotionally attached to their stories, they envision what they want but keep jumping back to restate what was so annoying, hurtful, or disappointing about the past. To avoid following them backward, share the pattern they keep repeating and invite them to stay focused on their future possibility or explore what they may need to release or resolve about their past so they can free themselves to move forward.

有时，客户在情绪上过于依附于自己的故事（story），他们虽能设想自己想要什么，却不断跳回去重申过去那些多么恼人、伤人、或令人失望的事。为避免跟着他们向后走，分享他们不断重复的模式，并邀请他们专注于自己未来的可能性，或去探索关于过去、他们可能需要释放或解决什么，好让自己获得向前迈进的自由。

Case Study

案例研究

A client was starting a new job after taking a year off due to health issues. Her doctor told her she had fully recovered and cleared her to take a full-time position. She wanted to be coached on creating a self-care plan to stay healthy while working.

一位客户在因健康问题休整一年之后，即将开始一份新工作。她的医生告诉她已完全康复，批准她担任全职职位。她想接受教练，制定一份自我关怀计划，以便在工作时保持健康。

In our first session, it became clear she already had a plan, so I asked her if she wanted to explore how to stay with her plan while working. She said that would be a good focus because her typical pattern was to start a job being mindful of self-care until her fears of being judged as inadequate led her to overwork.

在我们的第一次对话中，很明显她已经有了一份计划，于是我问她，是否想探索如何在工作时坚持这份计划。她说这会是个不错的焦点，因为她典型的模式是：开始一份工作时还留意自我关怀，直到“怕被评价为不够好”的恐惧导致她过度工作。

I asked her if she wanted to stay with the outcome of sticking with her self-care plan while working or to address her fears of being judged for her choices. She chose to address her fear by building her confidence in her work skills. She said she wanted to feel “tall and strong.”

我问她，是想坚持“工作时维持自我关怀计划”这个成果，还是想处理“因自己的选择而被评判”的恐惧。她选择通过建立在工作中技能上的自信，来应对那份恐惧。她说她想感觉“高大而强壮”。

As we explored her fears of being judged, she went down a side path of describing her history of illnesses. I asked her if her desire to feel “tall and strong” was more about staying healthy or building confidence in her work skills. She said she probably shouldn’t live in the past, but she knew that when her confidence falters and she overworks, it hurts her health, so we should probably focus first on building her confidence. When I asked her what her best days at work would be like if she felt confident about her abilities, she fell back into talking about her fear of not being physically strong enough to do her best because she had not worked for so long.

当我们探索她对被评判的恐惧时，她拐上了一条岔路，去描述自己的病史。我问她，想要感觉“高大而强壮”，更多是关于保持健康，还是关于建立工作技能上的自信。她说自己大概不该活在过去，但她知道，当自信动摇、她过度工作时，会损害健康，所以我们大概应该先聚焦建立她的自信。当我问她，如果对自己的能力有信心，工作中最好的日子会是什么样时，她又退回到谈论自己的恐惧——因为太久没工作，怕自己身体不够强壮，无法做到最好。

I asked her, “You said your doctor cleared you to work. How real is the possibility that you will not have the physical stamina for the job?”

我问她：“你说过，你的医生已经批准你工作。你工作中会缺乏体力耐力的可能性，到底有多大？”

She said, “I don’t know, I’m just worried.”

她说：“我不知道，我只是担心。”

I said, “You said, ‘I’m just worried’ so decisively. Is that your real challenge, that you are just worried?”

我说：“你说‘我只是担心’，说得那么果断。这就是你真正的挑战吗——你只是担心？”

The client laughed. “Yes, I create a storm in my head. I always do this to myself. I envision everything that could go wrong before I take one step.”

客户笑了。“是的，我在脑子里制造一场风暴。我总是这样对待自己。在迈出一步之前，我把所有可能出错的都设想一遍。”
