I said, “So is your vision still to be tall and strong every day at work?” She said she believed she could be that person. I said, “You started by saying you wanted to sustain your self-care plan but then moved to a desire to build your confidence. But you now see that what really gets in your way of being tall and strong is your habit of worrying too much. Do I see this correctly?”

我说：“所以你的愿景仍然是在工作中每天都高大而强壮吗？”她说相信自己能成为那样的人。我说：“你一开始说想维持自我关怀计划，后来转成了想要建立自信。但你现在看清了：真正阻碍你高大而强壮的，是你过度担忧的习惯。我理解得对吗？”

“Yes, I make it up until it’s real. I make myself sick with worry.”

“对，我把事情编出来，直到它变成真的。我让自己被担忧弄得生病。”

I said, “What a great insight, that your many illnesses might be related to your excessive worrying, which also causes you to overwork. I think you are saying that what weakens both your physical and mental strength is your habit of worrying. Is that what you would like our coaching to focus on, who you will be when you decrease your worrying?”

我说：“多棒的洞见（insight）——你诸多病痛也许与你过度的担忧有关，而担忧也导致你过度工作。我想你说的是：削弱你身心力量的，正是你担忧的习惯。这难道不是你希望我们的教练所聚焦的——当你减少担忧时，你会成为谁？”

She gave me a resounding “Yes, that’s it.” I coached her to describe what it would be like to worry less. She said her desire was still to be tall and strong at work but now knew she needed to focus on her mindset and trust that her skills were good enough. In the end, she said she knew she could go to work with more confidence with her new goal in mind.

她响亮地回了我一句“对，就是它”。我教练她去描述少一些担忧会是什么样子。她说自己的渴望仍是在工作中高大而强壮，但如今明白自己需要聚焦心态，并相信自己的技能足够好。最后，她说她知道，带着这个新目标，自己能更自信地去工作。

Coaching is supportive and encouraging; it can also be uncomfortable and disconcerting when you need to firmly hold the focus on their present experience and their desired future scenario. Once, after I did a coaching demonstration for a group, an observer asked my coachee if she was annoyed that I asked her three times to state what she really wanted and what that would look like. The coachee replied, “Yes, it was annoying. And it was exactly what I needed.” Keep inviting them to look into what they want to create now based on what they share, what they are becoming aware of, and what is changing for them as they explore their thoughts with you.

教练既支持、鼓励，也可能令人不适和不安——当你需要坚定地把焦点保持在他们当下的体验和期望的未来情境上时。有一次，我为一群人做教练演示后，一位观察员问我的客户（coachee），被我问了三次“你真正想要什么、那会是什么样子”，她是否感到厌烦。客户回答：“是的，很烦。而这恰恰是我需要的。”持续地邀请他们去审视自己现在想要创造什么——基于他们分享的、他们正觉察到的、以及他们在与你一起探索想法时正在改变的东西。

Be Careful of Your Assumptions

When defining and agreeing to the client’s desired outcome, be careful not to fall into the common habit of assuming what clients experience and want without getting confirmation. The assumption of knowing comes into play when coaches think they have had the same experience as their client. Author David Brooks said, “Events happen in our lives, but each person processes and experiences any given event in their own way.” 5 When coaches assume an understanding without adequate exploration and confirmation, they quit listening. They can’t fully understand how clients interpret their reality and what they want to create instead.

在界定并认同客户的期望成果（outcome）时，要小心，别掉进一个常见习惯：在没有获得确认的情况下，就假定客户体验了什么、想要什么。“自以为知道”的假设（assumption of knowing），在教练认为自己有过与客户相同经历时便会起作用。作家 David Brooks 说：“事件发生在我们的生活中，但每个人都以自己的方式加工和体验任何一个既定事件。”⁵ 当教练在没有充分探索和确认的情况下就假定自己理解了，他们便停止了倾听。他们无法完全理解客户如何诠释自己的现实，以及客户想转而创造什么。

Also, if what the client says they want from coaching is vague, ambiguous, and imprecise, there won’t be a solid direction for the coaching conversation to take. The agreement frames the conversation.

此外，如果客户说想从教练中得到的东西模糊、含混、不精确，教练对话就没有坚实的方向可循。协议（agreement）框定了对话。

To clarify vague desires, consider asking these questions if clients say they want to be clearer on the following choices:

为厘清模糊的渴望，若客户说想在以下选择上更清晰，可考虑问这些问题：

  • ✦ A decision or plan. “What are you hoping to have as a result of making the right decision or laying out a plan?”

- ✦ 一个决定或计划。“做出正确决定或列出一份计划，你希望得到什么结果？”

  • ✦ A general topic, such as leadership, better relationships, or confidence. “If you had clarity on what to do to be a better leader, connect with others better, or handle yourself more confidently, what would you achieve? Who would you be in that picture?”

- ✦ 一个笼统主题，如领导力、更好的人际关系，或自信。“如果你清楚该怎么做才能成为更好的领导者、更好地与他人联结，或更自信地掌控自己，你会取得什么？在那幅画面中，你会是谁？”

  • ✦ A better understanding of what happened or is occurring. “What are you hoping to get from better understanding the situation, clearing up your mental confusion, or identifying how you want to react?”

- ✦ 更好地理解已发生或正在发生的事。“通过更好地理解情境、厘清你头脑中的困惑，或确认你想如何反应，你希望得到什么？”

The answers will help define both the direction of the coaching conversation and what a meaningful closure might look like when they commit to moving toward their desired state at the end of the session. Stay curious until you and the client are clear about what the client actually wants.

这些答案将既有助于界定教练对话的方向，也有助于界定：当他们在对话结束时承诺朝期望状态迈进时，什么样的收尾才是有意义的。保持好奇，直到你和客户都清楚客户真正想要什么。

Tip Sheet

提示单

Three Tactics to Keep the Conversation on Track

让对话保持在正轨上的三种策略

Because problems often have many layers, outcomes can morph and change during the session. Use the following tips to enhance how you navigate the movement of the coaching session:

因为问题常有多个层面，成果可能在对话中变形、改变。运用以下提示，提升你引导教练对话流动的方式：

  • When clients list a number of problems, sum up the options using their key words and invite them to choose what outcome they want to work toward first. An example is “What do you want to focus on first, your relationship with your boss, your ability to inspire your team, or your desire to think more clearly under pressure?” Inviting them to choose the most important dilemma and outcome to work on gives clients both a clear destination and an affirmation of what would most make them feel better about themselves in their current life situation.

- 当客户列出若干问题，用他们的关键词总结选项，并邀请他们选择想首先着手的成果。例如：“你首先想聚焦什么——你与老板的关系、激励团队的能力，还是在压力下更清晰思考的渴望？”邀请他们选择最重要的困境与成果去处理，既给了客户一个清晰的目的地，也肯定了什么最能让他们在当下生活情境中对自己感觉更好。

  • Recognize when the outcome shifts, and reflect this to the client, inviting them to choose what direction they want to take. Shifts in desires can be big if the coaching moves from solving an external problem to resolving an internal conflict or inhibition. Or the shift might be a small adjustment in direction or priorities after the client has a clearer view of the situation. Regardless of the magnitude of the shift, make sure the client clearly describes the new vision of the desired outcome to ensure you both have the same target in mind.

- 识别出成果何时发生了偏移，并把这一点反射给客户，邀请他们选择想采取的方向。如果教练从解决外部问题转向化解内部冲突或抑制，渴望的偏移可能很大。或者，在客户更清楚地看清情境后，偏移可能只是方向或优先级的微小调整。无论偏移幅度多大，都要确保客户清晰地描述出期望成果的新愿景，让你们双方心中目标一致。

  • Listen for trigger points that spark anger, excuses, or blame when looking at what is stopping them from moving toward their desired outcome. Reflect what you hear as their judgments of themselves or others as the reason why they can’t get what they want. Is their reason insurmountable to address, or are they afraid to do what feels risky? Reaffirm what they said they wanted to create, and then ask if they feel something else is more achievable to work on. Imagining, refining, and redefining outcomes helps keep conversations moving forward instead of going in circles.

- 在审视是什么阻止他们朝期望成果迈进时，留意引发愤怒、借口或指责的触发点。把你听到的、他们对自己或他人的评判，作为“为何得不到想要的”的原因反射回去。他们的理由是无法逾越，还是他们害怕去做那件感觉冒险的事？重申他们曾说自己想创造什么，然后问他们是否觉得别的什么更容易着手。构想、精炼和重新定义成果，有助于让对话向前推进，而非原地打转。

Chapter 13

CHAPTER 9 BRAIN HACKING What Are You Listening For?

Human beings are poor examiners, subject to superstition, bias, prejudice, and a profound tendency to see what they want to see rather than what is really there.

人类是糟糕的检验者，受迷信、偏见（bias）、成见所支配，并且深刻地倾向于看见自己想看见的，而非真正存在的。

—M. S COTT P ECK

——M. SCOTT PECK

I N THE MIDDLE of my brain, in its long-term memory storage, is a box of stories I use to navigate each moment of my life (see figure 5 ). My stories are a mosaic of my life experiences and learnings, forming the meaning I attach to each moment (reality) and how I define myself (identity). The largest stories in my mosaic are composed of events that triggered strong emotions, structured by my parents, religion, education, social pressures, and unexpected experiences. These events form the context that influences how I define what I see and who I am each moment.

在我大脑的中部，长期记忆存储区里，有一个“故事盒子”，我用它来导航我生命的每一刻（见图 5）。我的故事是我生活经验与学习的马赛克，构成了我赋予每一刻的意义（现实），以及我如何定义自己（身份）。我这幅马赛克中最大的故事，由那些触发强烈情绪的事件组成，并以我的父母、宗教、教育、社会压力和意外经历为结构。这些事件构成了情境（context），影响着我在每一刻如何定义所见与我是谁。

Figure 5. Box of stories.

图 5. 故事盒子。

These memories planted the seeds of the beliefs, motivations, and desires that figure into my interpretation of what I consider is true and real. New experiences lodged in my memory may impact my translation of reality depending on the thickness of the shell encasing each story. The shells protect my stories from disruption, though some shells are thinner and more vulnerable to change than others.

这些记忆播下了信念、动机和渴望的种子，它们融入了我对“何为真实”的诠释。新经验若停留在我的记忆中，可能会根据包裹每个故事的“外壳”厚度，影响我对现实的翻译。这些外壳保护我的故事不被扰乱，尽管有些外壳比其他更薄、更易改变。

From the moment I wake up, I know “I am a responsible woman with a lot to do, but if I am at home, first I have to feed the cat.” This story complements my “I always wake up early” story inside a strong shell I can’t seem to crack even when I travel. The casing around my “morning exercise routines” has stiffened with age. I love to learn and always say I don’t know everything, yet I wrote this book with the view that “I know a lot more than many people about coaching.” I continually research to support my “expert” story. What is happening in the world right now disturbs my story about how we should live together, but I have many stories that support my knowing I will find a way to thrive in the chaos and help others flourish as well.

从我醒来的那一刻起，我就知道“我是一个负责任的女人，有很多事要做，但如果我在家，首先得喂猫。”这个故事，与我那个“我总是早起”的故事相得益彰，后者被包裹在一个坚固的外壳里，即便旅行时我也似乎无法打破。“晨间锻炼习惯”的外壳随着年龄变得僵硬。我热爱学习，总说自己并非无所不知，但我写这本书时却带着这样的看法：“关于教练，我比许多人懂得多得多。”我不断做研究，以支撑我的“专家”故事。当下世界上正在发生的事，扰乱了我关于“我们该如何共同生活”的故事，但我有许多故事支撑着我的认知：我会在混乱中找到蓬勃之道，并帮助他人同样繁荣。

My stories direct my daily tasks and interactions, assign meaning, and prompt emotional reactions in response to each incident. I rarely consider the need to verify what I think is accurate or wonder why I do something. Political philosopher Hannah Arendt said our reasons for acting are “not inspired by the quest for truth but by the quest for meaning. And truth and meaning are not the same.” 1

我的故事指引我的日常事务与互动，赋予意义，并对每一件事引发情绪反应。我很少去考虑是否有必要验证我所认为的准确，也很少好奇自己为何做某件事。政治哲学家 Hannah Arendt 说，我们行动的理由“并非受求真驱动，而是受求意义驱动。而真理与意义并不相同。”¹

Your brain has a box of stories that guides your thinking and actions too. Your stories differ from mine, even if we share similar experiences.

你的大脑也有一个指引你思维与行动的故事盒子。即便我们有相似经历，你的故事也与我的不同。

Because we need a sense of who we think we are and how we see the world around us the moment we wake up, most of the shells that encase our stories are stable. In daily conversations, we defend our stories as right for us.

因为我们在醒来的那一刻就需要“自己是谁、如何看待周遭世界”的感觉，包裹我们故事的大部分外壳是稳定的。在日常对话中，我们捍卫自己的故事，认为它们对我们而言是正确的。

I have learned that when I have an important decision to make or I’m having difficulty understanding why my emotions are hindering my actions, I call a coach. I can’t see outside of my story until I first see the story.

我学到，当我要做一个重要决定，或难以理解自己的情绪为何阻碍行动时，我会呼叫一位教练。在我先看见故事之前，我无法看见故事之外的东西。

The stories we staunchly live by keep us safe and stuck. The brain prefers self-preservation over self-actualization.

我们执着存活于其中的故事，既让我们安全，也让我们困住。大脑偏好自我保存，胜过自我实现。

The coach’s reflections and questions give me access to my stories in a way I can’t get for myself. It’s the best approach I know to examine my reasoning while keeping my protective brain out of the way.

教练的反思与提问，让我以一种自己无法做到的方式，触达我的故事。这是我所知最好的方法：审视自己的推理，同时让那保护性的大脑退到一边。

As stories pile up, context forms

The field that surrounds our stories and the frame around it form our context (see figure 6 ). Our contextual field formed as we accumulated stories. What emerged from our stories that created the field are our values—what we believe is most important to live by—and our social needs—what we hope to get from others that supports our feelings of safety and success. Our values and needs define what we perceive as right, wrong, good, and bad in the world.

环绕我们故事的场域，以及包裹它的框架，构成了我们的情境（context）（见图 6）。我们的情境场域，是随着故事积累而形成的。从故事中浮现、并创造出场域的，是我们的价值观（values）——我们相信最值得依此生活的东西——以及我们的社会需求（social needs）——我们希望从他人那里得到、以支撑安全感与成功感的东西。我们的价值观与需求，定义了我们在世界中视为对错、好坏的东西。

The brain’s operating system runs continuously in the background, directing our thoughts and emotions. Coaching delivers needed revisions and critical upgrades to the system.

大脑的操作系统持续在后台运行，指挥着我们的思想与情绪。教练为这套系统交付所需的修订与关键升级。

Our context shapes the rules we live by, with some being more important and obvious than others. The rules are either violated or honored in our stories. They show up in our conversations when we agree with or end up in conflict with others.

我们的情境塑造着我们赖以生活的规则，其中一些比另一些更重要、更明显。这些规则在我们的故事（story）中，要么被违背，要么被恪守。当我们与他人意见一致或最终发生冲突时，它们便出现在对话里。

The value of coaching is how we use our skills so clients can view the stories that give meaning to the challenge they are facing; to look inside the stories to see the beliefs, fears, and desires impacting their perception of the situation and to explore the values and needs that hold their stories in place. As a result, clients create new stories to replace the ones that no longer serve them.

教练的价值，在于我们如何运用技能，让客户得以审视那些赋予其所面临挑战以意义的故事；得以深入故事内部，看见影响他们对情境感知的信念、恐惧与渴望，并探索让故事得以稳固的价值观与需求。结果，客户创造出新故事，来取代那些不再服务于他们的旧故事。

When you explore stories and contexts inside your clients’ mental frames, you are coaching from the inside out. You start by asking what they would like to explore with you, and then listen to their stories. As they share, you seek to clarify what they mean with their descriptive words, what they hope to do or experience differently as a result of the coaching, what makes this change important, and what they think is getting in the way of realizing their desires. Then, you can coach them to examine the beliefs and assumptions holding their story together. Your reflections and questions can open their eyes to new ways of thinking about the situation. When their awareness expands, their storylines shift. They can then more confidently plan, make decisions, and commit to taking action.

当你在客户心智框架内部探索故事与情境时，你是在由内而外地教练。你先问他们想和你一起探索什么，然后聆听他们的故事。随着他们分享，你寻求厘清：他们用描述性词语意指什么、他们希望通过教练以不同方式去做或体验什么、什么让这个改变重要，以及他们认为是什么阻碍了自己实现渴望。接着，你可以教练他们去审视支撑其故事的信念与假设（assumption）。你的反思与提问，能让他们睁开眼，以思考情境的新方式去看。当他们的觉察扩展，故事线便发生转移。他们便能更自信地规划、决策，并承诺采取行动。

Figure 6. The contextual field supporting and protecting our stories.

图 6. 支撑并保护我们故事的情境场域。

This indicates the aspects that create the context holding the story balloons in place. Inside the balloons are the words “Beliefs,” “Biases,” and “Assumptions.”

这指出了构成情境、让故事气球各就其位的各个面向。气球内部写着“信念”（Beliefs）、“偏见”（Biases）和“假设”（Assumptions）。

If your clients still feel stuck or hesitant after exploring beliefs, dig deeper to find what makes it personally important to them to resolve their dilemmas and achieve their desires. What values are they compromising if they don’t move forward? What personal needs do they yearn for others to give them, such as respect, appreciation, or belonging? Is it worthwhile enough for them to rise above their fears to get their needs met?

如果你的客户在探索信念后仍感到困住或犹豫，就挖得更深，找出对他们个人而言，解决困境、达成渴望为何重要。如果他们不前进，是在妥协哪些价值观？他们渴望他人给予自己哪些个人需求，比如尊重、欣赏或归属感？值得他们超越恐惧、去满足自己的需求吗？

Coaching can surface conflicts of values, worries, and deep yet unspoken wants. The revelations crack the shells of old stories and modify the meaning of personal values and needs, which then change definitions of self and reality. The creative insights that cause these rifts, shifts, and changes are what we call breakthroughs.

教练能浮现价值观的冲突、担忧，以及深刻却未说出口的渴望。这些揭示（revelations）裂开了旧故事的外壳，改写了个人价值观与需求的意义，进而改变了自我与现实的定义。造成这些断裂、转移与改变的创造性洞见（insight），我们称之为突破性时刻（breakthroughs）。

coaching the story

Clients unconsciously depend on their old stories to provide meaning to current situations. Jonathan Gottschall, author of The Storytelling Animal, says, “For humans, story is like gravity: an inescapable field of force that influences everything, but is so omnipresent that we hardly notice it.” 2 Unexamined interpretations of a situation are clouded by old beliefs, outdated values, and tenacious fears.

客户在无意识中依赖自己的旧故事，为当下的情境赋予意义。《故事动物》（The Storytelling Animal）的作者 Jonathan Gottschall 说：“对人类而言，故事就像重力：一个无法逃脱的力场，影响一切，却又无处不在，以至于我们几乎注意不到它。”² 对情境未经审视的诠释，会被旧信念、过时的价值观和顽固的恐惧所遮蔽。

Coaching the client’s narrative is a good place to start. When listening to the client’s story, you can pinpoint key beliefs holding the story together. Reflecting words that indicate doubt and risks can surface unsubstantiated fears and unspoken desires. Clients begin to see more options for action than they did based on what they didn’t see before or had avoided due to the discomfort they thought they would face.

教练客户的故事（story）/叙事，是个好的起点。聆听客户的故事时，你能精准定位支撑故事的关键信念。反射出那些暗示怀疑与风险的词语，能浮现出毫无根据的恐惧和未说出口的渴望。客户开始看到比此前更多的行动选项——那些他们先前没看见、或因预期会面对的不适而回避的东西。

Examining how beliefs and assumptions are holding a story together is the most accessible part of the client’s narrative. They describe their story based on beliefs about what they see and fear. When you reflect their statements, they are more likely to modify or scrap an obstructive belief than defend it. They often show a sign of relief when they embrace a more supportive view that opens the door to creating what they desire. They see a way forward with more peace of mind.

审视信念与假设（assumption）如何支撑着一个故事，是客户叙事中最易入手的部分。他们基于“关于所见与所惧”的信念来描述自己的故事。当你反射回他们的陈述，他们更可能修正或抛弃一个阻碍性的信念，而非为它辩护。当他们接纳一个更具支持性的视角——那扇通向创造所欲之物的门——时，常会流露出如释重负的神情。他们以更平和的心境，看见前进之路。

Case Study

案例研究

A client had recently been promoted to be on the leadership team of a global pharmaceutical company. She wanted to be seen as a respected partner by the other leaders. Her goal was to speak up more in their team meetings. I asked her what was stopping her from speaking up. She said, “These men have worked together for a long time. I’m the new girl in the room — actually, the only girl. I think I need to listen for a while so I can make sure that what I offer meets their standards.”

一位客户最近被提拔进一家全球制药公司的领导团队。她希望被其他领导者视为受尊重的合作伙伴。她的目标是在团队会议中更多发声。我问她是什么阻止她发声。她说：“这些男士共事很久了。我是房间里的新女孩——实际上，是唯一的女孩。我想我需要先听一阵子，好确保我提供的东西符合他们的标准。”

I asked her what she meant by standards. The conversation led her to say, “I don’t want to offer silly ideas.” I asked her if the vision of her being respected by her peers in meetings included speaking up more or only speaking when she knew she had good ideas. She said, “My ideas are good. I think I’m scared they might not take me seriously.”

我问她“标准”是什么意思。对话引导她说：“我不想提出愚蠢的想法。”我问她：在她于会议中被同侪尊重的愿景里，是包含更多发声，还是只在确定有好想法时才说话。她说：“我的想法很好。我想我是怕他们不把我当回事。”

I asked her why she was promoted to be a part of the leadership team. She said she was the expert on marketing in Asia. I said, “So you are an expert, not just a new girl. What makes you believe they won’t take your ideas seriously?”

我问她为何会被提拔进领导团队。她说自己是亚洲市场的营销专家。我说：“所以你是专家，而不只是个新来的女孩。是什么让你相信他们不会把你的想法当回事？”

She smiled. “I am the expert. They need my ideas to make the best decisions.” I asked what would be different when she showed up as the expert instead of the new girl. With confidence, she described how she would listen for the opportunities to share and then ask if she could provide some ideas. At least she would start by asking until they got used to her contribution.

她笑了。“我是专家。他们需要我的想法来做最好的决定。”我问，当她以专家而非新女孩的姿态出现时，会有什么不同。她自信地描述：自己会留意分享的机会，然后询问能否提供一些想法。至少她会先以提问开始，直到他们习惯她的贡献。

I asked who she was in that picture. She said, “I’m a respected expert but also a good team member.” The rest of the coaching focused on what she needed to prepare to show up as she imagined she could.

我问她在那幅画面中是谁。她说：“我是一位受尊重的专家，也是一位好的团队成员。”余下的教练聚焦于：她需要准备什么，才能以她想象中可能的样子出现。

The quickest way to shift your clients’ perceptions of situations is to reflect their defining statements so they hear their beliefs and assumptions spoken by you. If you need to, you can follow up with a question about how they know what they shared is true and what else could be true. Coaching your client to state what they want to happen and then comparing that with the inhibiting beliefs they stated when describing the problem provides the clarity and motivation they need to figure out how to move toward their desire.

转变客户对情境感知的最快方式，就是反射回他们的界定性陈述，让他们从你口中听见自己的信念与假设。如有必要，你可以追问：他们如何知道所分享的是真的，以及还有什么可能是真的。教练客户去陈述他们想要发生什么，再将其与他们在描述问题时所说出的、具抑制性的信念相比较，便提供了他们所需的清晰度与动力，去想清楚如何朝渴望迈进。

Case Study

案例研究

My client was the vice president preparing her team for their division to be sold and merged into a new company. One of her managers was not meeting deadlines for reports important to the transition process. Peers reported that this manager was very negative in meetings. My client listed everything she had done to persuade her employee to get on board with the transition.

我的客户是一位副总裁，正带领团队为所在部门被出售、并入一家新公司做准备。她手下一位经理未能按时提交对转型流程很重要的报告。同侪反映，这位经理在会议上非常消极。我的客户列举了她为说服这名下属跟上转型所做的一切。

I said, “It sounds like you have done all you can to save her.”

我说：“听起来你已经尽了全力去救她。”

She paused, sighed, and said, “I hoped she would step up. She was once my top performer. I hoped to groom her for leadership. I think I’m holding on to that, but my gut says I should let her go. It’s the responsible choice I’ve been avoiding.”

她停顿、叹气，说：“我曾希望她能站出来。她曾是我的头号 performer。我曾希望把她培养成领导。我想我还在抓着那个不放，但我的直觉告诉我应该让她走。这是我一直在回避的负责任的选择。”

I asked her what had held her back from accepting that she had to let her go. She frowned and said, “She had been my favorite. I kept thinking I could fix this, that we could go back to the great relationship we had before. But she’s not happy here now, I know it. And the executives of the new company want to see if I can make tough decisions. My division will be a cornerstone for the new organization. We might even have our own budget and CEO. I need to bring the right team. Maybe I’m the one who needs to step up and do what is right for everyone.”

我问她，是什么让她迟迟不肯接受“必须让她走”。她皱眉说：“她曾是我最偏爱的。我总想着我能修好这一切，我们能回到从前那段好关系。但她现在在这里不快乐，我知道的。而新公司的管理层想看看我能否做出艰难决定。我的部门将是新组织的基石。我们甚至可能拥有自己的预算和 CEO。我需要带对团队。也许，需要站出来、为所有人做正确之事的人，是我自己。”

I said, “It sounds like what you want to do now is make sure you bring the right team with you in the transition, not look at how to fix one employee. Is that what you want to focus our conversation on now?” When she agreed, I asked, “What kind of leader do you see yourself being in this process?”

我说：“听起来你现在想做的，是确保在转型中带上对的团队，而不是去研究怎么修好一名员工。这难道就是你想让我们现在聚焦的吗？”她同意后，我问：“在这个过程中，你如何看待自己作为领导者的样子？”

She blurted, “Well I’m not the super leader who can save anyone.” We both laughed. She said, “I just want to be brave enough to do the right thing for my team members who want to move forward. So yes, I want to be a brave, effective leader.” She committed to acting on her decision to let the woman go that week and then put her energy into prepping her team for the transition. Within a year, the new company named her CEO of her very successful division.

她脱口而出：“好吧，我又不是能拯救任何人的超级领导者。”我们都笑了。她说：“我只是想有足够的勇气，为那些想前进的团队成员做正确的事。所以是的，我想成为一个勇敢、有效的领导者。”她承诺在那一周就执行让她离开的决定，然后把精力投入到为团队转型做准备中。不到一年，新公司任命她为她那极为成功部门的 CEO。

Even a small insight can reframe perspective and unlock what is possible to create. Reflecting their beliefs can lead to facing what was holding them back so they can see themselves in a new story that changes their lives.

即便一个小小的洞见（insight），也能重构视角，解锁可创造的可能。反射回他们的信念，能引导他们直面曾经阻碍自己的东西，从而让他们在一个改变其人生的新故事中看见自己。

Biases

Beliefs that lead to stereotyping and judging people or things can also be called biases. Biases can shield us from harm. They also distance us from others and short-circuit our empathy.

导致刻板印象、并去评判人或事的信念，也可称作偏见（biases）。偏见能保护我们免受伤害。它们也让我们与他人疏远，并短路了我们的同理心。

Sometimes, we are conscious of our biases. We staunchly defend them, believing we are right with or without evidence. If a bias is tied to a life value, such as how we hold the importance of having a work ethic or the value of religious observance in making decisions, we often expect others to believe as we do without considering a contrasting value important to them. 3

有时，我们意识到自己的偏见。我们顽固地捍卫它们，相信无论有无证据，自己都是对的。如果一种偏见与人生价值观（value）绑在一起——比如我们如何看重职业道德的重要性，或如何在决策中看重宗教践行的价值——我们常期望他人也像我们一样相信，而不考虑对他们而言重要的、相对立的价值。³

With coaching, we hope to bring client biases to light if they are hindering our clients from achieving their goals. Clients who aspire to be good leaders, parents, or humans are often open to examining the validity of their biases. They may change their minds, or they may just become more tolerant of how others see the world.

通过教练，我们希望在客户的偏见妨碍他们达成目标时，将其带到光亮处。立志成为好领导者、好父母或好人的客户，常愿意开放地审视自己偏见的有效性。他们可能改变想法，也可能只是对他人看待世界的方式变得更包容。

Unconscious biases are often referred to as blind spots. Typical unconscious biases relate to attitudes about age, race, nationality, gender, religion, and lifestyle choices. The impact of biases ranges from annoying others with our opinions to fueling the desire to harm others. If we hold a position of power over others, we may limit their chances and choices based on the judgments created from our biases.

无意识的偏见常被称作盲点。典型的无意识偏见，涉及对年龄、种族、国籍、性别、宗教和生活方式选择的态度。偏见的影响，小到用我们的意见惹恼他人，大到助长伤害他人的欲望。如果我们对他人拥有权力地位，我们可能会基于偏见所产生的评判，限制他们的机会与选择。

Bringing an unconscious bias to light by reflecting the judgments you hear and asking how their beliefs impact their relationships or their desired outcome might lead them to examine their thoughts behind the bias. They might retain their bias but change the narrative of how their bias shows up in their situations.

通过反思你所听到的评判，并询问他们的信念如何影响其与他人的关系或期望成果（outcome），把一种无意识偏见带到光亮处，可能引导他们审视偏见背后的想法。他们可能保留偏见，却改变“偏见如何在自己情境中显现”的叙事。

A good shift for clients is when they release the expectation that others think as they do. Hopefully, they will be more open to learning how others see things differently. Also, it can be transformational for clients to realize they are not their beliefs; that they recognize their biases as opinions, not truths; and that they have the power to change their minds.

对客户而言，一个良好的转变是：当他们放下“他人会像自己一样思考”的期待。但愿他们会更开放地学习他人如何以不同方式看世界。此外，当客户意识到“他们不等于自己的信念”、意识到自己把偏见认作“看法”而非“真理”、并意识到自己有力量改变想法时，这可能带来转化性的改变。

coaching the context

As mentioned earlier in this chapter, we hold a box of stories in our long-term memory that directs our behavior in the present. The field inside the box that surrounds and penetrates our stories is our context. Our contextual field holds what we need to feel good about ourselves and relationships (social needs) and what we believe is most important to live by (values). Our needs and values define the rules and standards we live by, dictating what we perceive as right and wrong in how the world should work and how people behave.

如本章前文所述，我们在长期记忆中持有一个故事盒子，它指引着我们当下的行为。盒子内部、环绕并渗透着我们故事的那个场域，就是我们的情境（context）。我们的情境场域，承载着我们需要在自我与关系上感觉良好之物（社会需求 social needs），以及我们认为最值得依此生活之物（价值观 values）。我们的需求与价值观，定义着我们赖以生活的规则与标准，规定了我们认为世界应当如何运转、人应当如何行事的“对错”。

When you coach clients to examine the beliefs shaping their stories, they might reveal the social needs or life values at the core of their beliefs. What makes them happy, proud, content, and significant shapes what they believe or desire to have. Needs and values can also keep them locked in their frustration when they doubt they will ever get their needs met or when their values conflict with what others value.

当你教练客户去审视塑造其故事（story）的信念时，他们可能会揭示出处于其信念核心的社会需求或人生价值观。让他们快乐、自豪、满足和有价值感的东西，塑造了他们所相信或渴望拥有的东西。当他们怀疑自己的需求永远不会被满足，或当他们的价值观与他人的价值观冲突时，需求与价值观也会把他们锁在挫败里。

Needs and values are central to how people define who they are. Questioning the validity of what is important in a specific situation can feel uncomfortable, even scary, especially in conversations at work when relationships are not deeply rooted or strong. Coaching helps override these fears because our noncon-frontational reflections and questions bring the elements shaping their stories to light. When clients feel safe and cared about, they more readily explore the usefulness of their beliefs, needs, and values. Clients can detach from the uncertainty felt when they question their views of reality, social needs, values, and ultimately their identity, increasing their willingness to learn and grow.

需求与价值观，是人们定义“自己是谁”的核心。质疑在特定情境中“何为重要”的有效性，可能让人感到不适，甚至害怕——尤其是在职场对话中，当关系尚未深厚或牢固时。教练有助于克服这些恐惧，因为我们非对抗性的反思与提问，能把塑造其故事的元素带到光亮处。当客户感到安全、被在意，他们便更乐于探索自己信念、需求与价值观的用处。客户能从“质疑自己对现实、社会需求、价值观、乃至最终对身份的看法”时所感到的不确定中抽离出来，从而提升学习与成长的意愿。

Social Needs

I laugh to myself whenever I hear someone say, “I don’t like needy people.” We are wired to be social, needing contact with other humans to thrive. But how we connect with others is impacted by the social needs we developed as children.

每当我听到有人说“我不喜欢黏人的人”时，我都会在心里笑一笑。我们天生是社会性的，需要与他人接触才能蓬勃发展。但我们如何与他人联结，受到童年时期形成的社会需求（social needs）所影响。

On the positive side, social needs are drivers of success. My need for attention helps me succeed as a writer, teacher, and public speaker. My need for recognition drives my desire to do good work. My need for control helps me run a successful business.

从积极的一面看，社会需求是成功的驱动力。我对关注的需求，帮助我成为成功的作家、教师和演说家。我对认可的需求，驱动着我把工作做好的渴望。我对掌控的需求，帮助我经营一家成功的企业。

You discovered early in life what helps you thrive. You found what enables you to be seen or what you can do to keep from standing out if being seen feels unsafe. You learned what you are good at doing and what makes you feel worthwhile. Your current identity includes what you learned you need from other people, such as respect, recognition, a sense of order, being liked, or independence. As you matured, you identified what boundaries make your work and life feel comfortable, whether you hold people to honor your boundaries or not.

You may think you are using logical reasoning to make a decision when your unconscious motive is to get a social need met.

你在生命早期就发现了什么能让你蓬勃发展。你找到了能让自己被看见的方式，或——当“被看见”感觉不安全时——你能做些什么来避免引人注目。你学会了自己擅长什么，以及什么让你感到有价值。你当下的身份，包含着你所学到的、需要从他人那里得到的东西，比如尊重、认可、秩序感、被喜欢，或独立。随着你成熟，你明确了哪些边界让你的工作与生活感到舒适——无论你是否要求他人尊重你的边界。

你可能以为自己是在用逻辑推理做决定，而你的潜意识动机其实是想满足一项社会需求。

On the shadow side, the rejection or violation of a social need may trigger a range of emotions, including fear, anger, vengefulness, disappointment, frustration, and sadness. Needs become expectations; we react emotionally when we don’t get what we want from others. Coaching can help people examine what expectations in a situation are valid or futile. Clients can then determine if requesting a need be met or letting it go is their best next step.

从阴影的一面看，一项社会需求被拒绝或违背，可能引发一系列情绪，包括恐惧、愤怒、报复心、失望、挫败和悲伤。需求变成了期望；当我们从他人那里得不到想要的，就会情绪化地反应。教练能帮助人们审视：在特定情境中，哪些期望是合理的、哪些是徒劳的。客户随后便能判断：要求满足一项需求，还是放下它，才是自己最好的下一步。

When you notice your clients blaming or belittling others when describing their situation, seek to understand what social needs they expected to be fulfilled by these people but weren’t. They might be judging people’s behavior as negative without evidence because they feel disrespected or left out. Or they might give in, saying, “Nothing is going to change. I just have to live with it” or “Screw them. I’ll get what I need elsewhere.” Ask them what it would look like if circumstances were different, if they felt accepted, respected, or safe to be themselves. Their description of their ideal scenario will reveal what social needs are important to them.

当你注意到客户在描述处境时指责或贬低他人，要去理解：他们曾期望从这些人那里得到满足、却未能如愿的，是什么社会需求。他们可能在毫无证据的情况下，就把人们的行为评判为负面，因为他们感到不被尊重或被冷落。或者他们可能屈服，说“什么都不会改变。我只能忍着”，或“去他们的。我到别处去满足我的需求。”问问他们：如果境况不同，如果他们感到被接纳、被尊重、或可以安全地做自己，那会是什么样子。他们对理想情境的描述，会揭示出哪些社会需求对他们重要。

Needs are not bad. They affirm our identity and strengths. Success might be a result of a need to make a difference or a drive to learn something so what you say is intelligent and correct. We tend to be happy and productive when family and colleagues acknowledge and support our needs.

需求并非坏事。它们确证我们的身份与优势。成功也许是“想有所作为”这一需求的结果，或是“想学到东西、好让自己的话显得聪明而正确”这一驱动力的结果。当家人和同事承认并支持我们的需求时，我们往往更快乐、更富成效。

However, the greater the need to feel safe or seen, the more we expect others to give us what we need. We judge them negatively when they don’t, thinking they are clueless or self-centered. We often put up with their behavior because asking for what we need might be ridiculed, but we still have reactions and form judgments just thinking about these people.

然而，越是需要感到安全或被看见，我们就越期待他人给予我们所需要的东西。当他们不给时，我们便负面地评判他们，觉得他们无知或自我中心。我们常常容忍他们的行为，因为开口要自己所需可能会被嘲笑，但光是想到这些人，我们仍有反应、仍会形成评判。

The following list includes some of the most common social needs people identify that cause them to react when a need is not met. Remember, social needs stem from what we discovered gives us happiness, comfort, or success, so people will have different needs at the top of their lists. 4

Be accepted

Feel respected

Be liked

Be understood

以下清单列出了人们公认的一些最常见的社会需求——当需求未被满足时，会引发他们的反应。记住，社会需求源于我们发现能给自己带来幸福、舒适或成功的东西，所以不同的人，清单顶端的需求会不同。⁴

Be needed

Be valued

Be given control

Have consistency

Maintain order

Be recognized

Feel loved

Experience safety

Experience predictability

Be right

Be treated fairly

Be given attention

Be seen

Experience freedom

Feel in harmony

Feel balanced

Be included or appreciated

Have fun

Have variety

Be seen as smart

Experience new challenges

被接纳 感到被尊重 被喜欢 被理解 被需要 被重视 被赋予掌控权 拥有一致性 维持秩序 被认可 感到被爱 体验安全 体验可预测性 是对的（正确） 被公平对待 被给予关注 被看见 体验自由 感到和谐 感到平衡 被包含或被欣赏 拥有乐趣 拥有变化 被视为聪明 体验新挑战

When coaching a person who demonstrates anger or hurt when talking about others in the present or past, reflect the behavior you notice and ask what need wasn’t met that is causing the reaction. You might offer what need you heard that wasn’t met and then ask whether your client knows the other person’s intentions, such as “Do you think they intended to make you angry (or disappointed, dismissed)?” The answer could be yes or no, but either way, the next question can help redirect the conversation to how to get their needs met in the future.

当教练一个在谈论现在或过去他人时表现出愤怒或受伤的人，把你注意到的行为反射回去，并问：是什么未被满足的需求引发了这种反应。你可以提出你听到却未被满足的需求，然后问客户是否了解对方的意图，例如“你认为他们是故意让你生气（或失望、被忽视）的吗？”答案可能是是，也可能是否，但无论哪种，下一个问题都能帮助把对话引向：未来如何让需求得到满足。

For coaches as well as clients, without consciously acknowledging the needs that trigger emotional reactions, our actions are controlled by the quest to get our needs met. However, when we honestly declare our needs—that we expect people to treat us in a particular way—we can choose how to courageously act in our best interest. We can ask for what we need or choose a better place to focus our energy by breathing and letting the needs go.

对教练和客户而言，若不自觉承认那些触发情绪反应的需求，我们的行动就会被“满足需求”的渴望所控制。然而，当我们诚实地宣告自己的需求——即我们期望他人以某种方式对待我们——我们就能选择如何勇敢地、为自身最大利益而行动。我们可以开口要自己所需的，也可以选择更好的地方去投放能量：深呼吸，然后放下这些需求。

Recognizing social needs frees people up to be the master, not the victim, of their reactions.

识别社会需求，能让人们成为自己反应的主人，而非受害者。

Don’t be afraid to discuss social needs. Past rejections and fears of not getting needs met in the future will impact willingness and courage. Coaching clients to claim their needs will help them better manage what is in their control to do.

不要害怕讨论社会需求。过去的拒绝，以及对未来需求无法被满足的恐惧，会影响意愿与勇气。教练客户去主张自己的需求，能帮助他们更好地管理自己可控之事。

Be cautious about assuming a client has a need that is the same as yours. Stay curious. Invite them to consider what you think is missing when they describe feeling betrayed, devalued, or annoyed. Let them tell you what they did not get even if it is not what you think.

要谨慎，别假定客户拥有与你相同的需求。保持好奇。当他们描述感到被背叛、被贬低或恼火时，邀请他们考量你认为缺失的东西。让他们告诉你他们没得到什么，即便那不是你所想的。

If they state a need you don’t have, be careful not to judge their need as trivial. Appreciate their needs as a part of who they are at their best so you can coach them to getting their needs met now or in the future.

如果他们说出了一种你并没有的需求，小心别把他们的需求评判为微不足道。把他们需求视为“他们最佳状态时的一部分”来欣赏，这样你才能教练他们去满足自己现在或未来的需求。

Life Values

Values give us a sense of purpose and appreciation for living. When our relationships, home life, and work support our highest values, we feel happiness, fulfillment, and contentment. They are the most secure elements in the contextual field that influence our perceptions and how we craft our stories. We make life choices based on our values even though the choices don’t always live up to what we hoped for. Often, the difficulties in relationships stem from a conflict of values.

价值观（values）赋予我们目标感，以及对活着的感激。当我们的关系、家庭生活和工作中支持着我们最高的价值观时，我们感到幸福、满足与安宁。它们是情境场域中最稳固的元素，影响着我们的感知，以及我们如何构筑自己的故事（story）。我们基于价值观做出人生选择，即便这些选择并不总如我们所愿。关系的困难，常源于价值观的冲突。

A value is more durable than a need. Values emerge early in life and rarely disappear, but they may shift in importance over a lifespan as events and age change our perspective on what we hold most dear. Values for health, friendship, and harmony often increase with age while values for winning, achievement, and challenge recede.

价值观比需求更持久。价值观在生命早期浮现，极少消失，但会随着事件与年龄改变我们对“最珍视之物”的视角，而在重要性上发生偏移。健康、友谊与和谐的价值观，常随年龄增长而提升；而赢、成就与挑战的价值观则退居其次。

Coaching might even reveal a personal conflict of values where something important has been given up while pursuing another value, such as achievement over family or pleasure over health. The denial of a lesser but still important value could cause resentment, regret, or fear of losing something in the future. Yet the awareness of the conflict could lead to creative ideas for compromising.

教练甚至可能揭示一种个人的价值观冲突：在追求另一项价值观时，放弃了某样重要的东西，比如成就凌驾于家庭，或享乐凌驾于健康。对一项次要却仍重要的价值观的否认，可能引发怨恨、后悔，或对未来失去某物的恐惧。然而，对冲突的觉察，可能引向妥协的创造性想法。

Because of life and family circumstances, honoring some values may need to be delayed. Can the client envision a future that honors their values? What can they do at this time in their life to be at peace, knowing they can align with their values better in the future with a specific plan?

由于生活与家庭的境况，尊重某些价值观或许需要延后。客户能否构想一个尊重其价值观的未来？在他们人生此刻，能做些什么以获得平和——同时知道未来凭借一个具体计划，能更好地与价值观对齐？

The downside of values is their rigidity. When clients can’t accept living with other people’s or an organization’s values, they might feel stressed and angry, convinced others are wrong, but see no way to compromise or move on. Coaching can identify these conflicts. Then, when you both look at what is possible for the client to create that would be less stressful, they can better decide what to do with the impasse.

价值观的短板在于其刚性。当客户无法接受与他人或组织的价值观共处，他们可能感到压力与愤怒，确信他人错了，却看不到妥协或向前的路。教练能识别这些冲突。然后，当你们共同审视客户可创造的、压力更小之物时，他们能更好地决定如何应对这一僵局。

The desired outcomes of a coaching session should reflect something your clients value, such as having more love, peace, adventure, freedom, achievement, balance, or success. Once your clients articulate what they want as a coaching outcome, ask what makes this outcome important to them. They will either name values that the outcome will meet or discover that the outcome is more of a should than what they actually want for themselves.

一场教练对话的期望成果（outcome），应当反映客户所珍视的某种东西，比如拥有更多爱、平和、冒险、自由、成就、平衡或成功。一旦你的客户阐明他们想要的教练成果，就问是什么让这成果对他们重要。他们要么会说出成果将满足的价值观，要么会发现这成果更像“应该”，而非他们真正为自己想要的。

Your clients will continue to become more self-aware after you coach them to recognize and examine their beliefs, unspoken desires, unmet social needs, and conflicts of values. You continue to be their thinking partner long after the relationship ends.

在你教练他们去识别并审视自己的信念、未说出口的渴望、未被满足的社会需求（social needs）与价值观冲突之后，你的客户会持续变得更加自我觉察。即便关系结束很久之后，你仍是他们的思维伙伴（thinking partner）。

exercise: values exploration

You can find a list and descriptions of common life values at Covisioning.com under “Values to Live By” on the Exercises & Support Materials page ( covisioning.com/values-to-live-by/ ). Use the list during the following three steps:

你可以在 Covisioning.com 的“练习与辅助材料”（Exercises & Support Materials）页面下，于“值得依此生活的价值观”（Values to Live By）中找到常见人生价值观（values）的清单与说明（covisioning.com/values-to-live-by/）。在以下三个步骤中使用该清单：

  • Identify your top five values. What do you need to feel happy, content, and in alignment? What makes you feel off balance when you can’t live in accordance with this value?

- 找出你最看重的五项价值观。你需要什么才能感到快乐、满足和一致？当你无法按此价值观生活时，什么会让你感到失衡？

  • Name your bottom five values. Which values did you quickly skip because they are not important to you? Knowing which values provide no meaning for you is important so you don’t judge others who have these values.

- 说出你最不看重（垫底）的五项价值观。哪些价值观你迅速跳过了，因为它们对你不重要？知道哪些价值观对你毫无意义，很重要——这样你就不会去评判拥有这些价值观的他人。

  • Consider whether you are experiencing a conflict of values. What are you spending energy doing that doesn’t feel important to you at this time in your life? What values do people tell you should be important to you but you feel doing what they say is more of an obligation than a desire? Can you choose to live by your values and explain to others why you are not living by theirs?

- 考量你是否正经历价值观的冲突。你正把精力花在什么上，而它在你此刻的人生中感觉并不重要？人们告诉你哪些价值观“应该”对你重要，你却觉得按他们说的做更像义务而非渴望？你能选择依自己的价值观生活，并向他人解释你为何不依他们的价值观生活吗？

Tip Sheet

提示单

Coaching Stories and Context

教练故事与情境

These tips will help you coach your clients to reveal the beliefs, core values, and driving social needs embedded in their old narratives, freeing them to compose new stories to live by:

这些提示将帮助你教练客户，揭示嵌入其旧叙事中的信念、核心价值观与驱动性的社会需求（social needs），解放他们去谱写可依此生活的新故事（story）：

  • Accept your clients’ descriptions and interpretations without judgment. Stay in your heart and gut and out of your head while they tell their story. Listen for and ask about comments that follow the words really, but, and should. Start your sentences with phrases such as “So I heard you say what you really want is . . . but then explain why you aren’t getting it” or “You seemed excited (uncomfortable, defensive, etc.) when you described what is most important to you but followed up with something else that you think you should do.” Make sure your tone is encouraging and inquisitive. Trust they will recognize the gaps in their logic when they hear them for themselves.

- 不带评判地接纳客户的描述与诠释。在他们讲述故事时，待在你的心与直觉里，而非头脑里。留意并询问那些跟在“真的”（really）、“但是”（but）和“应该”（should）这些词之后的评论。以这样的句子开头：“所以我听你说，你真正想要的是……但你接着又解释了为何得不到它”，或“当你描述对你最重要的东西时，你似乎很兴奋（不适、防御等），却补充了你认为自己应该做的别的事。”确保你的语气是鼓励且探究的。相信当他们亲耳听到时，会识别出自己逻辑中的漏洞。

  • Notice their emotional reactions to identify underlying beliefs. Clients often get angry or sad when explaining what they hoped would happen but didn’t, become quiet when they admit how their thinking is getting in the way of their desires, and talk faster when they realize what they can create to feel confident and happy. Listen for emotionally charged statements such as “They promised me” or “I can’t put up with this anymore” or “I keep running into the same walls.” Encourage clients to explain their statements. Ask what is making the situation unbearable, disappointing, annoying, or frustrating. What did they hope for that didn’t happen? What is stopping them from moving forward? What are they most afraid will happen if they try to achieve their desires?

- 留意他们的情绪反应，以识别底层信念。客户在解释“曾希望发生却未发生”的事时，常会愤怒或悲伤；当承认自己的思维正阻碍渴望时，会变得安静；当意识到自己能创造什么来感到自信与快乐时，语速会加快。留意那些充满情绪的表述，如“他们答应过我的”或“我再也受不了这个了”或“我不断撞上同一堵墙。”鼓励客户解释他们的表述。问是什么让情境难以忍受、令人失望、恼火或挫败。他们曾希望什么却没发生？是什么阻止他们前进？若他们尝试达成渴望，最怕发生什么？

  • Affirm their efforts and intentions while recognizing the needs not met by others and the compromises made based on life values. Unmet needs and values often result in emotional decision-making or uncertainty of what to do about their difficulties. Once they tell you what is missing for them in their story, reflect what you hear they need and value by stating the positive aspects as well as the conflicts, such as “I understand you want people to recognize you are doing your best to be a good leader” or “It sounds like you are frustrated, maybe hurt, when your children don’t see that your actions come from wanting them to have a good life” or “You’ve explained why efficiency and precision are important to your professional life but have caused difficulties in your personal relationships.” The conversation can then focus on what they want to do to get their needs met or released, envision living with the values most important to them, and determine who they aspire to be as they work toward honoring their needs and values. Coaching clients to muster the courage to say yes to themselves when they face possibilities of disappointment, disregard, or judgment by others is one of the greatest gifts of coaching.

- 在认可他们努力与意图的同时，也看到他人未满足的需求，以及基于人生价值观所做出的妥协。未被满足的需求与价值观，常导致情绪化决策，或对“如何应对困境”感到不确定。一旦他们告诉你其故事中缺失了什么，就通过陈述积极的面向与冲突，反射回你听到的他们所需与所珍视，例如“我理解你希望人们认可你正在尽全力做一个好领导者”，或“听起来你是 frustrated、也许受伤的，当你的孩子没看到你的行为源于想让他们拥有好生活”，或“你已解释过为何效率与精确对你的职业生活重要，却给你的私人关系造成了困难。”对话便可聚焦：他们想做些什么来满足或释放自己的需求，构想与对他们最重要的价值观共同生活，并界定他们渴望成为谁——当他们努力去尊重自己的需求与价值观时。教练客户鼓起勇气，在面对他人的失望、漠视或评判之可能时，对自己说“是”，是教练最珍贵的礼物之一。

Chapter 14

CHAPTER 10 NEW AND NEXT Coaching Insights and Commitments

Until one is committed there is hesitancy, the chance to draw back, always ineffectiveness.

在做出承诺之前，总有三心二意、退却的可能，终归一事无成。

—W ILLIAM H. M URRAY

——威廉·H·默里

A COMMON REGRET of coaches occurs when they realize they missed the opportunity to ask what clients are learning during the conversation instead of waiting until the end. The oversight often comes when coaches don’t see or ask about the slight smile, gasp, or look of regret that indicates a new thought or solution has emerged. This is the moment of learning.

教练常见的一种遗憾，是当他们意识到自己错过了在对话过程中询问客户正在学习什么的机会，而是一直等到最后。这种疏忽常发生在教练没有注意到、或没有去问那一丝微笑、倒吸一口气、或懊悔神情之时——而这些正表明一个新想法或解决方案已经浮现。这就是学习的时刻。

Here’s the scenario: The client shared a powerful new awareness about himself and then declared he knew exactly what to do to solve his problem. The coach acknowledged the client’s shift in demeanor, that he seemed excited about his insight, and then asked if there was anything else the client needed. The client said he was happy, getting exactly what he needed.

情形是这样的：客户分享了对自己一个强有力的新觉知，随后宣称自己确切知道该怎么做来解决他的问题。教练认可了客户举止的转变，说他似乎对自己的洞见感到兴奋，然后问客户是否还需要别的。客户说他很开心，正得到所需的一切。

What did the coach miss? First, the coach didn’t ask the client to clearly and concisely state the insight that emerged. The brain needs to process the information by hearing the idea spoken out loud. When the coach asks how the insight relates to the desired outcome of the conversation, the client will likely recognize how to translate the insight into action.

教练漏掉了什么？首先，教练没有请客户清晰而简洁地陈述浮现出的洞见。大脑需要通过听到想法被大声说出，来处理这些信息。当教练问这个洞见如何与对话的期望成果相关联时，客户很可能就会认识到如何将洞见转化为行动。

Second, even if the client states an action to take, the coach didn’t ensure a commitment to the action at the end of the conversation. The declaration of the steps to take when wrapping up, plus the date to do it by, ensures progress will be made. Instead of just having an idea about solving a problem, the final commitment to actions brings the idea to life.

其次，即便客户说出了要采取的行动，教练也没有在对话结束时确保对行动的承诺。收尾时宣告要采取的步骤、并附上完成的日期，才能保证会有进展。最终的行动承诺，不是仅仅有一个解决问题的念头，而是让这个念头活起来。

This lapse is common because of the emotional shifts that accompany insight formation. As realizations emerge, clients may first feel uncomfortable, even embarrassed or sad. If the coach holds a safe space for clients to process the reaction before asking if they would be willing to share what came to mind, clients are likely to move to acceptance and understand what can be done to move forward.

这种疏漏很常见，因为它伴随着洞见形成时的情绪转变。当领悟浮现，客户最初可能感到不适，甚至尴尬或悲伤。如果教练先为客户创造一个安全的空间去处理这反应，再去问他们是否愿意分享脑海中浮现的念头，客户就很可能会走向接纳，并理解可以做些什么来向前推进。

While lightening up emotionally feels like the end of the conversation, it’s not. Clients may feel like they got what they wanted, but ideas and decisions are fantasies if they don’t lead to clear and committed actions.

虽然情绪上的轻松感让人觉得对话已经结束，但事实并非如此。客户可能觉得自己已得到想要的，但如果想法与决定没有导向清晰而坚定的行动，它们就只是幻想。

When clients experience a new insight, a door opens revealing a new perspective. They have yet to walk through the door.

当客户体验到新的洞见，一扇门便打开，显露出新的视角。但他们尚未穿过这扇门。

Without formally wrapping up the session with asking clients to verbalize their learnings and insights, and then stating what and when they will do next to apply what they learned, clients may move back into their daily life with no change. They might even forget the insights they had.

若没有正式收尾会话——即请客户说出自己的所学与洞见，进而说明下一步将做什么、何时做以应用所学——客户可能毫发未变地回到日常生活。他们甚至可能忘记自己曾有过的洞见。

In chapter 8 , you read about the need to provide bookends for your coaching, starting with clearly defining the desired outcome to focus the conversation. The bookend at the end completes the conversation with a confirmation of what was learned and a concise declaration to take at least one step to ensure clients move toward the change they imagined.

在第 8 章，你读到了为你的教练提供"书挡"的必要性，始于清晰界定期望成果以聚焦对话。结尾的"书挡"以确认所学、并简明宣告至少迈出一步来收束对话，确保客户迈向他们所想象的改变。

seeing insights is as important as seeing beliefs

Much of coaching is shepherding clients to verbally examine their beliefs, biases, fears, and desires as they tell the story of their challenge. When these constructs come to the surface, the clarity prompts realizations that can be surprising, humbling, and often amusing because the solution seems so simple. Clients often laugh at what they feel is a “duh” moment when the sudden realization is something they feel they should have seen all along.

教练的很大一部分，是在客户讲述其挑战的故事时，引导他们用语言审视自己的信念、偏见、恐惧与渴望。当这些构念浮现到表面，那份清晰会催生令人惊讶、谦卑、且常常忍俊不禁的领悟，因为解决方案似乎如此简单。客户常会对自己感觉到的"哎呀"时刻发笑——那突如其来的领悟，是他们觉得本该一直都看得到的。

Regardless of the emotion triggered by the epiphany, your responsibility is to bring their thoughts out of their head and into the conversation. Even if they feel guilty, sad, or angry, don’t sympathize or try to diminish their feelings. Your compassionate and patient silence will allow them to process their experience so they can then talk about what they are seeing, giving shape to what they learned. Clients still need to find the words to explain what has come to mind to understand what just happened in their brains.

无论顿悟触发了何种情绪，你的责任都是把他们的想法从脑中引出、带入对话。即便他们感到内疚、悲伤或愤怒，也不要同情或试图淡化他们的感受。你充满同理与耐心的沉默，会让他们得以处理自己的体验，从而能够谈论他们所看到的，为他们所学赋予形状。客户仍需要找到言辞来解释脑海中浮现的内容，以理解自己大脑中刚刚发生了什么。

The subsequent actions they commit to after articulating their new awareness will more effectively ensure they use their courage and strengths to activate change. If the awareness indicates a big shift in how they see themselves, you might ask if they want to change the focus of the coaching to their self-development or if the original vision of what they want to create still holds. If they identify a personal change, coach them to see who they can become to keep the conversation inspirational and actionable.

他们在清晰表达新觉知后所承诺的后续行动，将更有效地确保他们运用勇气与优势去激活改变。如果这份觉知意味着他们看待自我的方式发生重大转变，你可以问他们是否想把教练的焦点转向自我发展，或者他们想要创造的原始愿景是否仍然成立。如果他们确认了某种个人改变，就教练他们看见自己能够成为谁，以保持对话既鼓舞人心又可付诸行动。

Don’t let breakthrough moments slip by. After having an emotional reaction, they may quickly return to telling their story. You need to redirect them by asking permission to interrupt so you can understand what new thoughts just emerged. Instead of deflecting the insight, you safely but firmly hold them in the moment as they thoughtfully, even courageously, state their insight.

别让突破的时刻溜走。在产生情绪反应后，他们可能很快又回到讲述故事。你需要请求许可打断他们，以重新引导，从而理解刚刚浮现的新想法。不要回避洞见，而是安稳而坚定地将他们留在这个当下，让他们深思地、甚至勇敢地陈述自己的洞见。

The recognition of an insight is like seeing where a piece fits in the puzzle. Articulating insights and learning locks the pieces into place. The altered story is then permanently re-encoded in memory, ensuring both progress in problem-solving and personal growth.

对洞见的识别，犹如看见拼图中某块应放的位置。清晰表达洞见与所学，便将拼块锁定到位。被改写的故事于是被永久地重新编码进记忆，同时保障了问题解决的进展与个人成长。

Case Study

案例研究

A client said she wanted to clarify what her business would look like if she had the courage to do only what she was passionate about. She said her fear of failure was keeping her small. The coach asked her what “keeping her small” meant. She said she was busy doing work she no longer cared to do but worried she needed the work to survive. Staying small meant she wasn’t stepping up to play a bigger, more impactful game.

一位客户说，她想厘清：如果自己有勇气只做热爱之事，她的生意会是什么模样。她说对失败的恐惧让她"固守小节"。教练问她"固守小节"是什么意思。她说自己忙于做早已不再在乎的工作，却又担心需要这份工作才能生存。"固守小节"意味着她没有挺身去玩一场更大、更有影响力的游戏。

The coach asked her what the bigger, more impactful game looked like. She said she wasn’t sure, but she wouldn’t be doing things that wasted her time. The coach asked if she could describe work that was worth her time. With frustration, the client crossed her arms and said, “I do what I’m most passionate about.”

教练问她那场更大、更有影响力的游戏是什么样子。她说不确定，但她不会去做浪费时间的事。教练问她能否描述一下配得上她时间的工作。带着挫败感，客户双臂交叉说道："我做我最热爱的事。"

Instead of going down the same path, the coach said, “Twice you said you want to do more of what you are passionate about, but when I ask you to describe it to me, you don’t offer specifics. Are you not sure what this work looks like, or are you not sure you are ready to make the change?”

教练没有沿同一条路走下去，而是说："你两次都说想更多地做自己热爱的事，可当我请你向我描述它时，你却给不出具体细节。是你不确定这工作是什么样子，还是不确定自己准备好做出改变了？"

The client said, “I’m successful, and it’s easy. What if I find that I will have to work too hard if I change my business?” She then dropped back into her chair, releasing her tension.

客户说："我很成功，而且很轻松。如果我改变生意，发现自己得拼命工作怎么办？"随后她向后靠进椅子，卸下了紧绷。

The coach said, “That was a big exhale. Can you tell me what you just let go of when you collapsed into your chair?”

教练说："那是一次深深的呼气。你能告诉我，当你瘫进椅子时，刚刚放下了什么吗？"

The client responded, “Every time I think about doing more meaningful work, I get stuck, just like you said. I keep talking about playing bigger, or maybe it’s playing more purposefully, but I can’t even let myself imagine it.”

客户回应道："每次我想到做更有意义的工作，就会卡住，就像你说的那样。我一直在说要玩得更大，或者也许更富目的性地去玩，可我甚至不允许自己去想象它。"

The coach asked, “Can we start with you sharing what you just learned about yourself?”

教练问："我们能先从你分享刚刚对自己的所学开始吗？"

After a moment of thought, the client said, “I want to make changes to what I do. I know my work can be more meaningful for me and my clients, but I don’t want my work to consume my life.”

思索片刻后，客户说："我想对自己所做的事做出改变。我知道我的工作对我和我的客户都能更有意义，但我不想让工作吞噬我的生活。"

The coach asked, “So you are not stuck on defining what you can do, but you are afraid if you make changes, your life will be consumed by your work. Is that correct?”

教练问："所以，你并不是卡在'界定自己能做什么'上，而是害怕一旦做出改变，你的生活会被工作吞噬。对吗？"

“Now that you say it back to me, it sounds bizarre,” the client said. “Why would I lose control unless I wanted to? I’m not afraid my business will fail. I’ve just been looking at this as a monster change. Maybe I can make it a slow but deliberate shift. You asked me what a bigger game looks like. Let’s just look at what I might be doing differently by next year.”

"你这么复述给我听，听起来真怪，"客户说，"除非我自己愿意，我怎么会失去控制？我并不怕生意失败。我只是一直把这看作一场怪物般的改变。也许我可以做一个缓慢但审慎的转向。你问过我更大的游戏是什么样子。我们就来看看，到明年我可能会有哪些不同的做法。"

The conversation flowed smoothly to what she wanted to do more and less of in a one-year vision and the steps she would take to bring her vision to life.

对话顺畅地流向她在一年愿景中想多做与少做的事，以及她将采取哪些步骤让愿景成真。

Jayne Warrilow, founder of Sacred Changemakers, said, “Coaches are not here to help people fit into a broken world. We’re here to help birth a new one.” 1 Clients can have many breakthroughs in their thinking throughout the session, each one refining their stories until a new picture of what they want and who they can be emerges. It is up to you to trust the coaching process even when they resist or hesitate.

Sacred Changemakers 创始人 Jayne Warrilow 说："教练来到这里，不是为了帮人去适应一个破碎的世界。我们来到这里，是为了助产一个新世界。"¹ 客户在整场会话中可能产生许多思维上的突破，每一次都在精炼他们的故事，直到浮现出一幅关于他们想要什么、能成为谁的新图景。即便他们抗拒或犹豫，也要由你来信任教练的过程。

Sometimes, realizations prompt negative reactions such as “What if it doesn’t work?” or “What if I’m wrong about what I really want to do?” or “What if they won’t let me change?” Recognize the assumptions as seeing only the downside of changing. Ask what the upside could be. You can also ask, “If you try to live true to your dreams and you face difficulties, what would you do next?” Most people can’t see through the fog of their fears to recognize how they would activate their resilience.

有时，领悟会引发负面反应，比如"万一不管用呢？""万一我对自己真正想做的事判断错了呢？"或"万一他们不让我改变呢？"要意识到这些假设只看到了改变的不利面。问问上行的可能是什么。你也可以问："如果你试着忠于梦想生活，却遭遇困难，你下一步会怎么做？"大多数人无法穿透恐惧的迷雾，去认识到自己将如何激活韧性。

Once you are able to redirect their conversation to what they really want for themselves at that moment or in the future, ask if they are willing to look at what steps they can commit to now. Then, go back to the desired outcome they declared for the conversation. Do they feel the steps are moving them to achieve their vision? The coaching should feel like progress is being made (see figure 7 ).

一旦你能够将他们的对话重新导向他们在当下或未来真正为自己想要的东西，就问他们是否愿意看看现在能承诺哪些步骤。然后，回到他们在对话中所宣告的期望成果。他们是否觉得这些步骤正推动他们实现愿景？教练应当让人感到正在取得进展（见图 7）。

You might notice hesitation when declaring their intended actions. Share your observation and ask whether they still need to resolve something else. Then, coach them to clarify what barrier they want to remove. The coaching might shift to a new topic and outcome. If you don’t have enough time to address the new issue, ask if they can hold the issue for the next session. In the meantime, you still want a commitment for what they will do before you close the session.

在宣告打算采取的行动时，你可能会注意到犹豫。分享你的观察，并问他们是否仍需解决别的事。然后，教练他们厘清自己想清除什么障碍。教练可能会转向新的话题与成果。如果你没有足够时间处理新问题，就问他们能否把这个问题留到下一节。与此同时，在结束会话前，你仍要一份关于他们将做什么的承诺。

Stay curious about what is percolating in their minds so you can help bring forward what they might be seeing but are unsure about speaking of out loud. Every insight changes their story. Every breakthrough can lead to confidently taking the next steps.

对他们脑海中正在酝酿的东西保持好奇，这样你才能帮助引出那些他们或许看见了、却不确定是否说出口的念头。每一次洞见都在改变他们的故事。每一次突破都能导向自信地迈出下一步。

Figure 7. Ensuring progress toward the desired outcome.

图 7. 确保朝向期望成果的进展。

Leftmost box reads “What do you want?”; middle box reads “What do you really want?”; rightmost box is a darker shade to emphasize the bold words “What will you do now?”

最左侧的方框写着"你想要什么？"；中间的方框写着"你真正想要什么？"；最右侧的方框色调更深，以强调加粗的"你现在要做什么？"。

what now?

Formulating the final bookend moves the conversation from spontaneity to asking a few specific, routine questions to ensure progress and growth.

构建最后的"书挡"，将对话从自发性转向提出几个具体的、常规性的问题，以确保进展与成长。

Remember to ask what they learned about themselves and the reality of the situation before moving into stating the actions they will now take. This ensures their plan is based on putting their insights into action instead of dangling the insights at the end of the conversation.

记得在转入陈述他们现在将采取的行动之前，先问他们对自己和情境现实学到了什么。这确保他们的计划建立在将洞见付诸行动之上，而非把洞见悬在对话结尾晃荡。

Turn their insights into commitments to bring their vision to life by asking these questions:

通过以下问题，将他们的洞见转化为承诺，让愿景成真：

  • What will you do now?

- 你现在要做什么？

  • When will you do this (or start the process)?

- 你何时去做（或启动这个过程）？

  • What could get in the way of your commitment?

- 什么可能阻碍你的承诺？

  • What other support or resources could be helpful?

- 还有什么其他支持或资源会有帮助？

  • If the step or plan relies on how someone else will respond, ask, If it doesn’t turn out as you hope, what will you do?

- 如果这个步骤或计划取决于他人将如何回应，就问：如果结果不如你所愿，你会怎么做？

Walking your client through these questions makes the commitment a promise, both to the coach and to the client. The responses activate the confidence and courage needed to follow through when they feel doubt or disappointment. When potholes show up on the path to their desires, they will be able to do what it takes to swerve around or make their way through the obstacles when they agree to the details of their plan.

带着客户走完这些问题，使承诺成为一份对教练、也对客户自己的诺言。这些回应激活了在感到疑虑或失望时坚持下去所需的信心与勇气。当通往渴望的道路上出现坑洼，一旦他们同意了计划的细节，就能采取行动绕过或穿越障碍。

Also, remember that coaching continues after sessions end. Their brains will continue to process the insights. Trust the process when clients say they need time to think about what they just learned before they commit to actions. Accept their request by asking what taking time to think looks like. Do they know when and where they will take time to contemplate what they discovered? This may feel pushy, but most clients readily commit to this step to ensure progress continues.

还要记住，教练在会话结束后仍在继续。他们的大脑会继续处理这些洞见。当客户说在承诺行动前需要时间思考刚刚所学时，请信任这个过程。接受他们的请求，并问"花时间思考"具体是什么样子。他们知道何时、何地会抽出时间去沉思自己的发现吗？这或许让人觉得强推，但多数客户会很乐意承诺这一步，以确保进展持续。

Don’t ever settle for a client saying “Thanks, I know what to do now.” Knowing and doing are not the same. Pledging at least one step helps them keep their promise to themselves.

绝不要满足于客户说一句"谢谢，我知道现在该做什么了"。知道与做到不是一回事。承诺至少一步，能帮助他们信守对自己的诺言。

end the session on a high note

A nice and effective way to end the conversation is to acknowledge the client for their willingness to explore, discover their blocks, and find a way forward. This keeps the focus on the client’s good work even if they choose to thank you for yours.

结束对话一个美好而有效的方式，是认可客户愿意去探索、发现自己的阻碍、并找到前进之路。即便他们选择为你的工作致谢，这也让焦点保持在客户良好的努力上。

If the session went well, they might want to give you the credit for their progress. Accept the praise with a simple “Thank you.” Then, remind them it was their ease of partnering with you, even when it felt uncomfortable, that made the coaching work.

如果会话进展顺利，他们可能想把进步归功于你。用一句简单的"谢谢"接受赞美。然后，提醒他们，正是他们与你合作的从容——即便在感到不适时——让教练得以生效。

If the session was a part of an ongoing relationship, you might remind them of the milestones they have achieved over time since you started coaching together. Appreciate how they have stayed the course even though their days are busy.

如果这次会话是一段持续关系的一部分，你可以提醒他们，自你们开始教练以来随着时间达成的里程碑。欣赏他们即便日子忙碌，仍坚持走完了这段路程。

Humans repeat and strengthen behaviors that are positively recognized. You aren’t just being nice when you acknowledge their willingness to be coached and the progress they have made. You are reinforcing their commitment to achieving their goals.

人类会重复并强化那些获得正向认可的行为。当你认可他们愿意被教练、以及所取得的进展时，你并不只是在示好。你是在强化他们实现目标的承诺。

Our brains want to protect us from disappointment, embarrassment, and the failure felt when making mistakes. Reinforcing your clients’ motivation and actions even when progress is slow gives their brain the rationale it needs to persevere.

我们的大脑想要保护我们免受失望、尴尬，以及犯错时的失败感。即便进展缓慢，也要强化客户的动机与行动，这给了他们大脑坚持下去所需的理由。

Finally, ask the client for their agreement that the session is over. You might say, “Are we complete for today?” You might ask, “Do you want to schedule our next session?” to indicate an ending to the current one. If the client has declared their greatest learning and what actions they will take, you don’t need to fill in the time if there are still minutes left on the session clock. Let them decide what to do with the time by asking, “Is this a good time to end, or is there something else you need that we can discuss with the time we have left?” They might appreciate having the gift of time to go do something else.

最后，征得客户对"会话已结束"的同意。你可以说："我们今天就到这里，可以吗？"你也可以问："你想预约我们的下一次会话吗？"以表明当前会话的结束。如果客户已经宣告了他们最大的所学以及将采取的行动，即便会话时钟上还有几分钟，你也不必填满这段时间。让他们决定如何利用这段时间，你可以问："现在是结束的好时机吗，还是你还有其他需要我们利用剩余时间讨论的事？"他们或许会感激这份时间的礼物，好去做别的事。

If they bring up another issue, be clear whether you feel you can reasonably address their request in your given time frame. Don’t overstep your time boundaries. Hopefully, they will agree to wait until the next session and focus instead on what they will do in the meantime for themselves.

如果他们提起另一个问题，要清楚你是否觉得自己能在既定时间内合理处理他们的请求。不要逾越你的时间边界。希望他们会同意等到下一次会话，转而专注于在此期间他们能为自己做些什么。

Using the essential coaching skills from this book will impact the client long after the session ends. As clients take the steps they committed to with new perspectives of their situations and themselves, they take the coaching process with them. Continuing to reflect and learn is a wonderful side effect of being coached.

运用本书的核心教练技能，其影响将在会话结束后长久地作用于客户。当客户带着对自己情境与自我的新视角，踏上他们所承诺的步骤时，他们也将教练过程带在了身上。持续反思与学习，是被教练的美妙副作用。

ending the relationship

Most coaching contracts have a specific ending. However, anytime during the coaching engagement, the client might request to end the coaching or you might suggest that it feels like the right time to say goodbye.

大多数教练合约都有明确的结束时间。然而，在教练关系的任何阶段，客户都可能要求结束教练，或者你也可能觉得是时候说再见了。

When there is no defined duration for the coaching and a client says it is time to end the relationship, respectfully honor their request. It takes courage to end a relationship. Many people just disappear.

当教练没有约定明确的期限、而客户说该结束这段关系时，请尊重并满足他们的请求。结束一段关系需要勇气。许多人只是悄然消失。

If the client questions the value they received from coaching, you can offer to recommend another coach if they aren’t getting what they hoped for. If the client doesn’t ask but you doubt whether the coaching is working, you can ask the client if they would share what value they are getting from your sessions. They might tell you what else they need, or they might be happy with what has transpired. Their pacing might be different from your expectations.

如果客户质疑从教练中获得的价値，若他们未能得到所期望的，你可以主动推荐另一位教练。如果客户没有开口、而你却怀疑教练是否奏效，你可以询问客户是否愿意分享他们从你的会话中获得了什么价值。他们可能会告诉你还需要什么，也可能对已经发生的感到满意。他们的节奏或许与你预期的不同。

If you have been together for a long time, the coaching might have shifted to more of a friendship with no clear goals to work on, which should be acknowledged as your professional and ethical obligation. Your purpose is to facilitate their growth, not keep them as a client as long as you can. Wish them well as they move on to their next chapter.

如果你们共事已久，教练关系或许已更多转向友谊，没有了明确的工作目标，这应被视作你的专业与伦理责任而加以正视。你的目的是促进他们的成长，而非尽可能长久地将他们留在客户名单上。当他们迈向人生下一章时，祝福他们一切安好。

Parting ways is inevitable. Don’t take parting as a personal loss. Appreciate the time you had together as your opportunity to learn and grow as well.

分道扬镳在所难免。不要把离别当作个人的损失。珍惜你们共度的时光，将其视为你学习与成长的机会。

Tip Sheet

小贴士

Coaching to Articulate Insights and Commitments

教练以清晰表达洞见与承诺

Use the following tips to coach clients to clearly articulate what they have learned about their thinking and themselves and then declare their commitment to the actions they intend to take:

运用以下小贴士，教练客户清晰地表达他们对自己思维与自我的所学，进而宣告他们对打算采取之行动的承诺：

  • Emotional shifts precede words when clients have epiphanies; capture the movement to bring the thoughts to light. Often, clients will look away in silence as their brains try to make sense of the new awareness. Then, a discernible emotion may arise, such as embarrassed laughter or tears. use silence to allow them to process and experience the moment. When the emotions subside but they don’t speak up, you might ask if they would share what just came into their thoughts. If they try to ignore what just happened, you can interrupt by saying, “Can I stop you for a moment? I want to make sure we don’t miss something important for you.” Then, share the gestures you noticed before asking if something new just occurred that would be useful to consider in relation to achieving what they desire. Most likely, they will share what came up for them. If not, give them space to process their reaction. It has changed their perspective even if they are not ready to discuss it right away. It will come up again because you called attention to it.

- 当客户有顿悟时，情绪转变先于言语；捕捉这种流露，让想法得以显现。通常，当客户的大脑试图理解这份新的觉知时，他们会沉默地移开目光。随后，可能会浮现出可辨识的情绪，比如尴尬的笑或眼泪。利用沉默让他们处理并体会这一刻。当情绪平复但他们仍不开口时，你可以问他们是否愿意分享刚刚浮现在脑海中的念头。如果他们试图忽略刚刚发生的事，你可以打断说："我能打断你一下吗？我想确保我们没有错过对你而言重要的东西。"然后，在询问是否有新的、有助于实现其渴望的念头出现之前，先分享你注意到的姿态。多半情况下，他们会说出自己的体会。若没有，就给他们空间去处理自己的反应。即便他们还没准备好立刻讨论，这已经改变了他们的视角。因为它被你点明了，还会再次出现。

  • Invite clients to choose the direction of the coaching after an important revelation. Do they want to redefine what they want to achieve, or do they have a new understanding about their desires that could change the course of action to take? If they say they want to resolve something else based on what they suddenly understand, ask them to share what they now want to achieve by the end of the session. If they say they are clear on the actions they want to take, ask how their actions will help them achieve the outcome they previously agreed to.

- 在重要的领悟之后，邀请客户选择教练的方向。他们是想重新定义想要达成的目标，还是对自己渴望有了新理解、可能改变行动的走向？如果他们说基于突然的理解想先解决别的事，就请他们分享此刻想在会话结束时达成什么。如果他们说已清楚自己想采取的行动，就问这些行动将如何帮助他们实现先前约定的成果。

  • Sessions should end with at least one step to take with a date attached and consideration of anything that could help or get in the way of their intention. Clients are more likely to hold themselves accountable to their words when you ask what they will do, by when, and what could help. Wrapping up the session with a commitment to do at least one thing, even if they say they need time to reflect on the session, strengthens their conviction to act when doubts or busyness creep in.

- 会话结束时应至少带有一个附上日期的步骤，并考量任何可能帮助或阻碍其意图的因素。当你问客户他们将做什么、何时做、以及什么能提供帮助时，他们更可能对自己的话负责。即便他们说需要时间反思会话，也要以至少一个行动的承诺来收尾，这会强化他们在疑虑或忙碌袭来时行动的决心。

Wrap-Up Beyond the Conversation Coaching as a Lifestyle and Culture

Who we are and what we do are so tightly connected.

我们是谁，与我们做什么，紧密相连。

—H ERMINIA I BARRA

——赫尔米尼亚·伊瓦拉

A YEAR AFTER I started my coach training, I was at lunch with a group of longtime friends. During the meal, one of them said, “Marcia, I want you to know how enjoyable it is to be with you these days.” I sort of smiled and asked what she meant. “I don’t know, you seem more interested in us.” I tilted my head, prompting her to go on. “Don’t get me wrong, you’re so smart, you always have good advice. And you tell great stories! I think now, you listen more, connect a little better. Maybe it just feels better. I just wanted to let you know.” Everyone at the table agreed. Then, my closest friend lifted her glass to toast our friendship and changed the subject to a new restaurant she found that we should check out soon.

在我开始教练培训一年后，我与一群老友共进午餐。席间，其中一位说："Marcia，我想让你知道，这些天和你在一起多令人愉快。"我微微一笑，问她什么意思。"我说不清，你似乎对我们更感兴趣了。"我偏了偏头，引她继续说下去。"别误会，你那么聪明，总有好建议。而且你很会讲故事！我想现在，你听得更认真，联结也更好了些。也许就是感觉更好了。我只是想让你知道。"桌上所有人都表示赞同。随后，我最要好的朋友举起酒杯，为我们的友谊干杯，并把话题转向她新发现、我们很快该去试试的一家餐厅。

The conversation haunted me for days. Had I been the know-it-all, the fixer, and the type of person who most frustrated me? After days of contemplation, I woke up thinking, “They are my friends. They miss me when work gets in the way of our being together. I am grateful I annoyed them and they loved me anyway!” I forgave myself for who I used to be and thankful I found coaching.

这段对话萦绕在我心头数日。我曾不会是那个万事通、那个修补者、以及我最反感的那种人吧？数日沉思后，我醒来时想道："他们是我的朋友。当工作妨碍我们相聚时，他们会想念我。我感激自己曾惹恼他们、而他们仍然爱我！"我原谅了曾经的自己，并感谢自己遇见了教练。

That’s when I realized my coaching habits were becoming my life habits.

那时我才意识到，我的教练习惯正变成我的生活习惯。

I recalled one of my first coach trainers saying, “First you start by doing coaching, then as coaching gets in your bones, you start being a coach.” The mental habits of being present, receiving instead of listening, and releasing judgment changed the dynamics in all my relationships. Using reflective statements followed by compassionately curious questions decreased the bad habit of already knowing. My conversations are more open and rewarding.

我想起我最早的教练培训师之一曾说："一开始你从'做教练'起步，然后当教练深入你的骨血，你便开始'成为教练'。"临在、接收而非倾听、以及放下评判这些心智习惯，改变了我所有关系中的互动动态。在反思性陈述之后，跟进以富有同理好奇的提问，减少了"早已知道"的坏习惯。我的对话更加开放，也更有收获。

My friend’s honesty helped me see that I was on my way to being a coach.

朋友的坦诚让我看见，我正走在成为教练的路上。

being a coach

From the first day you chose to learn and practice coaching, your identity started shifting. Herminia Ibarra, professor of organizational behavior at INSEAD and Harvard, said we don’t become who we are when we have money and prestige. She said we define ourselves daily by “the things we do, the company we keep, and the stories we tell about our work and our lives.” 1 No matter your level of skill development, as you witness the positive impact of your coaching conversations, you have stepped onto the path of coaching mastery.

从你选择学习并实践教练的第一天起，你的身份认同就开始转变。INSEAD 与哈佛的组织行为学教授 Herminia Ibarra 说，我们并非在有金钱与声望时才成为自己。她说，我们每天通过"所做的事、所交往的人、以及关于工作与生活的故事"来定义自己。¹ 无论你的技能发展到何种程度，当你见证自己教练对话的积极影响时，你已踏上教练精通之路。

The more you coach, the more comfortable you are telling people you are a coach. When you begin to embrace this identity, you shift from just doing coaching to being a coach. The way you engage with others—live or online, in meetings and hallways, and when sharing meals—changes as coaching transforms your way of being with others. People feel seen, heard, and valued with you. This may be the only time in their busy days they feel so deeply acknowledged and accepted.

你教练得越多，就越能坦然告诉别人你是一名教练。当你开始拥抱这个身份，你就从只是"做教练"转向"成为教练"。你与他人互动的方式——无论是线下还是线上、在会议与走廊里、还是在共进餐食时——都随着教练改变你与他人相处的方式而改变。和你在一起时，人们感到被看见、被倾听、被重视。这或许是他们忙碌日子里唯一如此深切地被认可与接纳的时刻。

Thank you for choosing to be a coach.

感谢你选择成为一名教练。

I started writing the first edition of this book with the intention of demystifying coaching for coaches looking to improve their skills. The more I wrote, the more I realized the book could be useful for anyone with a desire to have more meaningful conversations. As I started teaching from the book, I recognized how important it is for people to use coaching skills with compassionate and courageous curiosity in any uncomfortable conversation. The more divisive our world, our communities, and even our households become, the more valuable it is to use a coaching approach in our conversations.

我开始撰写本书第一版时，意图是为想要提升技能的教练揭开教练的神秘面纱。我写得越多，越意识到这本书对任何一个渴望拥有更有意义对话的人都有用。当我开始以这本书授课时，我认识到，让人们在任何令人不适的对话中，以富有同理与勇气的好奇心运用教练技巧，是多么重要。我们的世界、社群、甚至家庭变得越对立，在对话中采用教练式方法就越有价值。

We may not save the world or fix the forces that divide us. But we can inspire optimism and hope for a better tomorrow when we move through our days not just doing coaching but being a coach.

我们或许拯救不了世界，也修复不了分裂我们的力量。但当我们度过每一天时，不只是"做教练"、而是"成为教练"，我们就能激发对更美好明天的乐观与希望。

being a coaching culture

Not only can coaching change our one-on-one conversations, but when everyone around you develops a coaching mindset, teams, organizations, and communities progress with speed and care. In chapter 1 , I described the influence coaching has on the brain to expand perceptions to recognize new possibilities and refine the definitions of self. These discoveries inspire changes in goals, intentions, and actions. Multiply this impact on individuals to teams and entire organizations and you have more innovation, higher levels of engagement, and often the surpassing of goals.

教练不仅能改变我们一对一的对话，而且当你周围每个人都发展出教练心态时，团队、组织与社群便会以速度与关怀并进。在第 1 章，我描述了教练对大脑的影响——拓展感知以认出新可能，并精炼自我的定义。这些发现激发目标、意图与行动的改变。把这种对个人的影响放大到团队与整个组织，你便会得到更多创新、更高水平的投入，常常还能超越目标。

The ICF now has over twenty years of research that documents what happens when companies embrace coaching. These studies show results ranging from teaching leaders coaching skills to use in their work conversations to changing the way all employees engage each other so that the culture change is evident. 2

ICF（国际教练联合会）如今已有超过二十年的研究，记录了企业拥抱教练时发生的事。这些研究展示的成果，从教导领导者将教练技巧用于工作对话，到改变所有员工彼此互动的方式，从而使文化变革清晰可见。²

The increase in employee engagement in these reports translates to decreases in absenteeism and turnover, higher levels of productivity, and quicker adaptation to change. Even when traditional cultures perpetuated hierarchical decision-making and control, coaching has made a big difference. 3

这些报告中员工投入度的提升，转化为缺勤率与流失率的下降、生产力水平的提高，以及对变化更快的适应。即便在传统文化延续等级式决策与控制时，教练仍带来了巨大不同。³

Organizations that commit to transforming their culture to embrace coaching start with four tactics: (1) senior leaders are provided with external coaches to develop their strategic thinking, communication skills, and business acumen, with the intention of them becoming advocates for coaching; (2) leaders at all levels are taught how to use coaching skills in their conversations; (3) an internal pool of coaches are trained and certified to support leaders; and (4) all employees are educated in the process and value of coaching and are provided a means to seek coaching from their managers or through a platform where they can request an internal coach to work with.

致力于转变文化以拥抱教练的组织，从四种策略入手：（1）为高管提供外部教练，培养其战略思维、沟通技巧与商业敏锐度，意在使之成为教练的倡导者；（2）教导各层级领导者如何在对话中运用教练技巧；（3）培训并认证一支内部教练队伍，以支持领导者；（4）对所有员工进行教练过程与价值的教导，并提供途径，使他们能向经理寻求教练，或通过平台申请与内部教练合作。

Case Study

案例研究

Aeon Vietnam is a retail company. I met with their human resources leaders to plan how to shift the hierarchical mindset of employees by teaching and inspiring leaders to embrace a coaching approach, leading to a permanent shift in the work culture.

Aeon Vietnam 是一家零售公司。我与他们的人力资源负责人会面，规划如何通过教导并激励领导者拥抱教练式方法，来转变员工的等级式思维，从而带来工作文化的永久性转变。

The market was pushing the change. New competitors emerged daily. They needed to stand out as one of the best places for employees to work and for customers to buy their brands.

市场正在推动这场变革。新的竞争者每天涌现。他们需要脱颖而出，成为员工工作、顾客购买其品牌的最佳场所之一。

Yet their employees were accustomed to a routine, following instructions and not taking initiative to solve problems in their daily interactions with hundreds of demanding customers. Traditional leadership training provided minor results. The HR executives hoped coaching would make the difference in the mindset and behavior of the leaders so they would desire to empower individuals to take more risks, work together better, and hopefully enjoy the work experience more.

然而，他们的员工习惯于按部就班、遵循指令，在与数百名挑剔客户的日常互动中不主动解决问题。传统的领导力培训收效甚微。人力资源高管希望教练能在领导者的心态与行为上带来不同，使他们愿意赋能个人去承担更多风险、更好地协作，并希望更享受工作体验。

Just as they began to implement their plan, the COVID-19 pandemic hit. They saw the crisis as a catalyst for change, not a hindrance. They had a clear reason to boost innovation, strengthen collaboration, and keep spirits high. With this perspective, most of the leaders embraced the coaching behaviors. As a result, they created trust and inspired the courage to deal with the difficulties of the pandemic on a retail organization.

就在他们开始实施计划时，COVID-19 疫情来袭。他们将这场危机视为变革的催化剂，而非阻碍。他们有了明确的理由去提振创新、加强协作、保持高昂士气。带着这种视角，大多数领导者拥抱了教练行为。结果，他们建立了信任，并激发出应对疫情给零售组织带来困难的勇气。

Aeon Vietnam also created a platform where all employees can register for coaching at any time, with no involvement from their managers. Adding group and team coaching to the mix, Aeon Vietnam enhanced cross-departmental collaboration, which led to their impressive results in productivity, engagement, and happiness.

Aeon Vietnam 还创建了一个平台，所有员工可随时注册接受教练，无需经理介入。加入团体与团队教练后，Aeon Vietnam 增强了跨部门协作，从而在生产力、投入度与幸福感上取得了令人瞩目的成果。

As a result, from 2021 to 2023 during the pandemic, team performance scores jumped by 12 percent. They exceeded expectations in retention and performance and earned multiple awards designating Aeon Vietnam as one of the best workplaces in Asia.

结果，在 2021 至 2023 疫情年间，团队绩效得分跃升了 12%。他们在留任与绩效上超出预期，并荣获多项大奖，将 Aeon Vietnam 评为亚洲最佳职场之一。

In 2024, I was honored to stand alongside them to receive the prestigious Impact Award from ICF for their coaching culture. You can read the case study on the ICF website. 4

2024 年，我荣幸地与他们并肩，因他们的教练文化而领取 ICF 颁发的著名 Impact Award（影响力大奖）。你可以在 ICF 网站上阅读这则案例研究。⁴

coaching makes a difference

Margaret Wheatley says we may not save the world but we can make a difference together by uplifting spirits and expanding consciousness with as many people as we can. 5 When people are overwhelmed, stressed, and angry, coaching reminds them of their purpose, dreams, and personal power to move forward with courage and grace.

Margaret Wheatley 说，我们或许拯救不了世界，但我们可以与尽可能多的人一起，通过提振精神、拓展意识，共同带来改变。⁵ 当人们感到不堪重负、压力重重、满腔怒火时，教练会提醒他们自己的目标、梦想，以及带着勇气与优雅继续前行的个人力量。
